Physical Chemistry
Chapter 1: Solutions
7 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following concentration terms is independent of temperature?
(a) Molarity
(b) Molality
(c) Normality
(d) Formality
Answer: (b) Molality
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Molality is defined as the number of moles of solute per kilogram of solvent (m = n_solute / w_solvent in kg). Because mass does not change with temperature (unlike volume, which expands or contracts with temperature changes), molality and mole fraction are independent of temperature.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Value of Henry's constant KH for a gas in a liquid:
(a) increases with increase in temperature
(b) decreases with increase in temperature
(c) remains constant with temperature
(d) first increases then decreases
Answer: (a) increases with increase in temperature
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
According to Henry's Law: p = KH * χ => χ = p / KH. The solubility of gases in liquids decreases with increasing temperature because dissolution of gas is an exothermic process. Since solubility (χ) decreases with rising temperature, Henry's constant KH must increase with temperature.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
An azeotropic mixture of two liquids boils at a lower temperature than either of them when:
(a) it shows large positive deviation from Raoult's law
(b) it shows large negative deviation from Raoult's law
(c) it is an ideal solution
(d) it shows zero deviation
Answer: (a) it shows large positive deviation from Raoult's law
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Solutions showing large positive deviations from Raoult's law (e.g., ethanol + water, 95% ethanol by volume) exhibit higher vapour pressure than expected due to weaker A-B interactions. Higher vapour pressure lowers the boiling point, forming a minimum-boiling azeotrope.
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The van 't Hoff factor (i) for complete dissociation of K4[Fe(CN)6] in aqueous solution is:
(a) 4
(b) 5
(c) 6
(d) 1
Answer: (b) 5
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
K4[Fe(CN)6] ionizes as: K4[Fe(CN)6] -> 4 K⁺ + [Fe(CN)6]⁴⁻. Total number of ions produced per formula unit n = 4 + 1 = 5. For 100% dissociation (α = 1): i = 1 + (n - 1)α = 1 + (5 - 1)(1) = 5.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which of the following aqueous solutions will have the highest boiling point?
(a) 1.0 M Na2SO4
(b) 1.0 M NaCl
(c) 1.0 M Glucose
(d) 1.0 M Sucrose
Answer: (a) 1.0 M Na2SO4
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Elevation in boiling point is a colligative property: ΔTb = i * Kb * m. For Na2SO4, i = 3 (effective molarity = 3.0 M); for NaCl, i = 2 (effective 2.0 M); for glucose and sucrose, i = 1 (effective 1.0 M). Since Na2SO4 has the highest effective particle concentration, it exhibits the largest elevation in boiling point and highest boiling point.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
When non-ideal solution of chloroform and acetone is mixed:
(a) ΔH_mix > 0 and ΔV_mix > 0
(b) ΔH_mix < 0 and ΔV_mix < 0
(c) ΔH_mix = 0 and ΔV_mix = 0
(d) ΔH_mix > 0 and ΔV_mix < 0
Answer: (b) ΔH_mix < 0 and ΔV_mix < 0
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Chloroform (CH-Cl3) and acetone ((CH3)2C=O) form strong intermolecular hydrogen bonds (Cl3C-H...O=C(CH3)2) that are stronger than original dipole-dipole interactions. This causes a negative deviation from Raoult's law, characterized by exothermic mixing (ΔH_mix < 0) and volume contraction (ΔV_mix < 0).
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Colligative properties of a solution depend upon:
(a) the nature of solute particles
(b) the nature of solvent particles
(c) the number of solute particles relative to total particles
(d) the physical state of the solution
Answer: (c) the number of solute particles relative to total particles
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Colligative properties depend strictly on the total number of solute particles (molecules/ions) present in a given amount of solvent, regardless of their chemical identity or nature.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
People living at high altitudes suffer from a condition called anoxia (weakness and inability to think clearly) because:
(a) atmospheric pressure is low, so dissolved oxygen in blood is low
(b) atmospheric pressure is high
(c) temperature is low
(d) water boils faster
Answer: (a) atmospheric pressure is low, so dissolved oxygen in blood is low
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
According to Henry's Law, solubility of oxygen in blood is directly proportional to its partial pressure. At high altitudes, low atmospheric pressure reduces the partial pressure of oxygen, leading to low oxygen concentration in blood and tissues (anoxia).
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
Two solutions having the same osmotic pressure at a given temperature are called:
(a) Isotonic solutions
(b) Hypertonic solutions
(c) Hypotonic solutions
(d) Saturated solutions
Answer: (a) Isotonic solutions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Solutions that exert equal osmotic pressure across a semipermeable membrane at the same temperature are termed isotonic solutions. There is no net movement of solvent between isotonic solutions.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The unit of molal elevation constant (Kb) is:
(a) K kg mol⁻¹
(b) K mol kg⁻¹
(c) kg mol⁻¹ K⁻¹
(d) K kg⁻¹ mol
Answer: (a) K kg mol⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
From ΔTb = Kb * m => Kb = ΔTb / m = Kelvin / (mol / kg) = K kg mol⁻¹.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
If 0.1 M solution of glucose and 0.1 M solution of urea are separated by a semipermeable membrane, then:
(a) water flows from glucose to urea
(b) water flows from urea to glucose
(c) no net flow of water occurs
(d) both glucose and urea cross the membrane
Answer: (c) no net flow of water occurs
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both glucose and urea are non-electrolytes with van 't Hoff factor i = 1. At equal molar concentration (0.1 M), their osmotic pressures are identical (Π = CRT), making the solutions isotonic. Hence, no net osmosis occurs.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
When a blood cell is placed in a hypertonic solution (> 0.9% w/v NaCl):
(a) it swells and bursts
(b) it shrinks (plasmolysis)
(c) its shape remains unchanged
(d) it dissolves
Answer: (b) it shrinks (plasmolysis)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Normal saline (0.9% w/v NaCl) is isotonic with fluid inside human red blood cells. When placed in a hypertonic solution (higher solute concentration / higher osmotic pressure outside), water flows out of the cell by exosmosis, causing it to shrink.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Which colligative property is most widely used to determine the molar mass of biomolecules and polymers?
(a) Osmotic pressure
(b) Elevation in boiling point
(c) Depression in freezing point
(d) Relative lowering of vapour pressure
Answer: (a) Osmotic pressure
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Osmotic pressure is measured at room temperature (avoiding thermal degradation of biomolecules), uses molarity (convenient for dilute solutions), and produces substantial, easily measurable pressure changes even for very dilute solutions of high-molecular-mass macromolecules.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Acetic acid associates in benzene to form dimers. The value of van 't Hoff factor (i) for acetic acid under complete dimerization is:
(a) 0.5
(b) 2.0
(c) 1.0
(d) 0.25
Answer: (a) 0.5
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Dimerization: 2 CH3COOH <=> (CH3COOH)2. Here n = 2. For complete association (α = 1): i = 1 + (1/n - 1)α = 1 + (1/2 - 1)(1) = 1 - 0.5 = 0.5.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
An ideal solution is formed when its components:
(a) have identical intermolecular forces (A-B = A-A = B-B)
(b) have ΔH_mix > 0
(c) have ΔV_mix > 0
(d) form an azeotrope
Answer: (a) have identical intermolecular forces (A-B = A-A = B-B)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
An ideal solution obeys Raoult's law at all concentrations and temperatures because intermolecular forces between solute and solvent (A-B) are identical to those in the pure components (A-A and B-B), leading to ΔH_mix = 0 and ΔV_mix = 0.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Ethylene glycol is added to water in car radiators in cold climates because it:
(a) acts as an anti-freeze by lowering the freezing point of water
(b) raises the freezing point
(c) decreases the boiling point
(d) prevents rust only
Answer: (a) acts as an anti-freeze by lowering the freezing point of water
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Ethylene glycol acts as a non-volatile solute that lowers the freezing point of water (depression of freezing point, ΔTf = Kf * m), preventing cooling water in automobile radiators from freezing in sub-zero winter temperatures.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
The mole fraction of ethylene glycol (C2H6O2) in an aqueous solution containing 20% of C2H6O2 by mass is:
(a) 0.068
(b) 0.932
(c) 0.20
(d) 0.80
Answer: (a) 0.068
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In 100 g solution: mass of glycol = 20 g, mass of water = 80 g. Moles of glycol = 20 / 62 ≈ 0.322 mol. Moles of water = 80 / 18 ≈ 4.444 mol. Total moles = 0.322 + 4.444 = 4.766 mol. Mole fraction of glycol = 0.322 / 4.766 ≈ 0.0676 ≈ 0.068.
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The semipermeable membrane commonly used in reverse osmosis desalination plants is made of:
(a) Cellulose acetate supported on a porous sheet
(b) Animal bladder
(c) Copper ferrocyanide
(d) Gelatin
Answer: (a) Cellulose acetate supported on a porous sheet
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Modern industrial reverse osmosis desalination uses polymer membranes of cellulose acetate supported on a porous backing; it is permeable to water molecules while impervious to dissolved salt ions and organic contaminants.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Scuba divers use air diluted with helium (11.7% He, 56.2% N2, 32.1% O2) to prevent a painful and dangerous medical condition called:
(a) Bends (decompression sickness)
(b) Anoxia
(c) Plasmolysis
(d) Emphysema
Answer: (a) Bends (decompression sickness)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Helium is far less soluble in blood than nitrogen. Diluting breathing gas with helium prevents excessive dissolved gas from forming painful, dangerous nitrogen bubbles in the bloodstream when divers ascend rapidly to the surface (the bends).
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Which of the following mixtures shows positive deviation from Raoult's law?
(a) Ethanol and Acetone
(b) Chloroform and Acetone
(c) Nitric acid and Water
(d) Phenol and Aniline
Answer: (a) Ethanol and Acetone
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Pure ethanol molecules are held by intermolecular hydrogen bonds. Adding acetone breaks these hydrogen bonds, weakening overall intermolecular attractions (A-B < A-A, B-B). This increases escaping tendency, causing positive deviation.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
If van 't Hoff factor i = 1, it implies that the solute:
(a) undergoes neither dissociation nor association
(b) undergoes complete dissociation
(c) undergoes complete association
(d) is volatile
Answer: (a) undergoes neither dissociation nor association
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
i = (observed colligative property) / (calculated colligative property). When i = 1, normal molecular behavior occurs without dissociation or association (e.g. glucose, urea, sucrose in water).
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
For a dilute solution containing 2.5 g of non-volatile solute in 100 g of water, the relative lowering of vapour pressure is 0.0125. The molecular mass of the solute is:
(a) 36 g/mol
(b) 72 g/mol
(c) 18 g/mol
(d) 90 g/mol
Answer: (a) 36 g/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Relative lowering of vapour pressure (p° - p) / p° = (w2 * M1) / (M2 * w1). 0.0125 = (2.5 * 18) / (M2 * 100) = 45 / (100 * M2) = 0.45 / M2 => M2 = 0.45 / 0.0125 = 36 g/mol.
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
A solution containing 8.6 g per dm³ of urea (M = 60 g/mol) was found to be isotonic with a 5% (w/v) solution of an organic non-volatile solute. The molar mass of the solute is:
(a) 348.8 g/mol
(b) 60 g/mol
(c) 120 g/mol
(d) 180 g/mol
Answer: (a) 348.8 g/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For isotonic solutions at same temperature: C1 = C2 => w1 / (M1 * V1) = w2 / (M2 * V2). For urea: w1 = 8.6 g, V1 = 1 dm³ = 1 L, M1 = 60 g/mol => C1 = 8.6 / 60 ≈ 0.1433 mol/L. For 5% (w/v) solute: w2 = 5 g in V2 = 100 mL = 0.1 L => C2 = 5 / (M2 * 0.1) = 50 / M2. Equating: 50 / M2 = 8.6 / 60 => M2 = (50 * 60) / 8.6 = 3000 / 8.6 ≈ 348.8 g/mol.
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The cryoscopic constant Kf depends only on:
(a) the nature of the solvent
(b) the nature of the solute
(c) the concentration of the solution
(d) atmospheric pressure
Answer: (a) the nature of the solvent
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Kf = (R * M_solvent * Tf²) / (1000 * ΔH_fusion). It is a characteristic physical constant of the solvent and is completely independent of the solute.
Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
A solution of two liquids A and B has maximum boiling azeotrope. The interactions between A-B are:
(a) stronger than A-A and B-B
(b) weaker than A-A and B-B
(c) equal to A-A and B-B
(d) zero
Answer: (a) stronger than A-A and B-B
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Maximum boiling azeotropes are formed by non-ideal solutions showing negative deviations from Raoult's law (e.g. 68% HNO3 + 32% H2O). Stronger A-B interactions decrease vapour pressure to a minimum, requiring higher temperature to boil (maximum boiling).
Q26. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2024
Assertion (A): Aquatic species are more comfortable in cold waters than in warm waters. Reason (R): The solubility of oxygen gas in water increases with decrease in temperature.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. Gas dissolution in water is exothermic (ΔH < 0). Lower temperature shifts equilibrium forward (Le Chatelier's principle), increasing dissolved oxygen essential for aquatic respiration.
Q27. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2023
Assertion (A): Cooking takes longer on high mountain peaks without a pressure cooker. Reason (R): At high altitudes, atmospheric pressure is low, causing water to boil at a lower temperature.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. Liquid boils when vapour pressure equals atmospheric pressure. At low atmospheric pressure, boiling point of water drops below 100 °C, supplying less thermal energy to cook food.
Q28. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Assertion (A): Osmotic pressure measurement is preferred over other colligative methods for determining the molecular mass of proteins. Reason (R): Biomolecules like proteins decompose at elevated temperatures and have very small colligative property changes due to high molar masses.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. Osmotic pressure is measurable at room temperature (preventing protein denaturation) and gives substantial, precise readings.
Q29. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Assertion (A): When sodium chloride (NaCl) is dissolved in water, the boiling point of water increases. Reason (R): The addition of a non-volatile solute lowers the vapour pressure of the solvent, requiring a higher temperature for vapour pressure to equal atmospheric pressure.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R correctly explains the mechanism of elevation in boiling point (ΔTb = i * Kb * m).
Q30. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2020
Assertion (A): A mixture of chlorobenzene and bromobenzene forms an ideal solution. Reason (R): Chlorobenzene and bromobenzene have nearly identical molecular sizes, shapes, and similar intermolecular van der Waals forces.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R correctly explains why ΔH_mix ≈ 0 and ΔV_mix ≈ 0 for this mixture.
Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
State Henry's law and mention two of its important industrial/medical applications.
Answer: Henry's law and applications
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Statement: At a constant temperature, the solubility of a gas in a liquid is directly proportional to the partial pressure of the gas present above the surface of the liquid: p = KH * χ, where p is partial pressure, χ is mole fraction in solution, and KH is Henry's law constant. 2. Applications: (i) In soft drinks and soda bottles: Bottles are sealed under high CO2 pressure to increase the solubility of CO2. (ii) In deep-sea diving: Scuba divers breathe air diluted with helium to avoid 'bends' caused by high nitrogen solubility at high underwater pressures.
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
State Raoult's law for a solution containing non-volatile solute. Write its mathematical formulation.
Answer: Raoult's law for non-volatile solute
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Statement: For a solution of a non-volatile solute in a volatile solvent, the relative lowering of vapour pressure is equal to the mole fraction of the solute present in the solution. 2. Mathematical Formula: (p° - p) / p° = χ_solute = n2 / (n1 + n2) ≈ n2 / n1 (for dilute solutions), where p° is vapour pressure of pure solvent and p is vapour pressure of solution.
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Differentiate between ideal and non-ideal solutions on the basis of: (i) Raoult's law obedience, (ii) enthalpy of mixing ΔH_mix, (iii) volume change on mixing ΔV_mix.
Answer: Differences between ideal and non-ideal solutions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Raoult's Law: Ideal solutions obey Raoult's law over the entire range of concentration and temperature (pA = pA° χA). Non-ideal solutions deviate from Raoult's law. 2. Enthalpy of Mixing: For ideal solutions, ΔH_mix = 0 (no heat evolved or absorbed). For non-ideal solutions, ΔH_mix ≠ 0 (positive for positive deviations, negative for negative deviations). 3. Volume Change: For ideal solutions, ΔV_mix = 0 (total volume equals sum of component volumes). For non-ideal solutions, ΔV_mix ≠ 0.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (2 Marks)
Define: (i) Molal elevation constant (Ebullioscopic constant Kb), (ii) Reverse osmosis.
Answer: Definitions of Kb and reverse osmosis
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Molal Elevation Constant (Kb): The elevation in boiling point produced when one mole of a non-volatile solute is dissolved in 1 kilogram (1000 g) of the solvent (i.e. in a 1 molal solution). 2. Reverse Osmosis: The phenomenon in which solvent molecules flow from a solution of higher solute concentration to pure solvent across a semipermeable membrane when an external pressure greater than the osmotic pressure is applied on the solution.
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (3 Marks)
Calculate the freezing point of a solution containing 1.8 g of non-electrolyte solute (molar mass = 180 g/mol) dissolved in 90 g of benzene. (Kf for benzene = 5.12 K kg mol⁻¹, freezing point of pure benzene = 5.5 °C).
Answer: Freezing point = 4.93 °C
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Moles of solute n = 1.8 g / 180 g/mol = 0.01 mol. Mass of solvent w1 = 90 g = 0.090 kg. Molality m = n / w1(kg) = 0.01 / 0.090 = 1 / 9 ≈ 0.111 mol/kg. Depression in freezing point: ΔTf = Kf * m = 5.12 * (1 / 9) = 0.569 K ≈ 0.57 °C. Freezing point of solution Tf = Tf° - ΔTf = 5.5 °C - 0.57 °C = 4.93 °C.
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (2 Marks)
What is an azeotrope? Why can the components of an azeotropic mixture not be separated by fractional distillation?
Answer: Azeotrope definition and distillation behavior
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Azeotrope: A constant-boiling binary liquid mixture that boils at a specific constant temperature and distills over without any change in chemical composition. 2. Why they cannot be separated: In an azeotropic mixture, the composition of the liquid phase is identical to the composition of the vapour phase. Heating produces vapour with the exact same proportions, preventing separation by fractional distillation.
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (3 Marks)
A 5% solution (by mass) of cane sugar (molar mass = 342 g/mol) in water has a freezing point of 271 K. Calculate the freezing point of a 5% solution (by mass) of glucose (molar mass = 180 g/mol) in water. (Freezing point of pure water = 273.15 K).
Answer: Freezing point of glucose solution = 269.07 K
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For 5% cane sugar: 5 g in 95 g water. ΔTf1 = 273.15 - 271 = 2.15 K. Molality m1 = (5 / 342) / 0.095 = 5 / (342 * 0.095) ≈ 0.1539 mol/kg. ΔTf1 = Kf * m1 => 2.15 = Kf * 0.1539 => Kf = 2.15 / 0.1539 ≈ 13.97 K kg/mol. For 5% glucose: 5 g in 95 g water. Molality m2 = (5 / 180) / 0.095 = 5 / (180 * 0.095) ≈ 0.2924 mol/kg. ΔTf2 = Kf * m2 = 13.97 * 0.2924 ≈ 4.08 K. Freezing point of glucose solution = 273.15 - 4.08 = 269.07 K.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (2 Marks)
Explain with an example what is meant by: (i) minimum boiling azeotrope, (ii) maximum boiling azeotrope.
Answer: Minimum and maximum boiling azeotropes with examples
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Minimum Boiling Azeotrope: Formed by solutions exhibiting large positive deviations from Raoult's law. The boiling point of the azeotrope is lower than that of either pure component. Example: 95% Ethanol + 5% Water by volume (boils at 351.15 K, lower than pure ethanol 351.3 K and water 373.15 K). 2. Maximum Boiling Azeotrope: Formed by solutions exhibiting large negative deviations from Raoult's law. The boiling point of the azeotrope is higher than that of either component. Example: 68% Nitric acid + 32% Water by mass (boils at 393.5 K, higher than water 373 K and HNO3 359 K).
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (3 Marks)
Determine the amount of CaCl2 (i = 2.47) dissolved in 2.5 L of water such that its osmotic pressure is 0.75 atm at 27 °C. (R = 0.0821 L atm K⁻¹ mol⁻¹, molar mass of CaCl2 = 111 g/mol).
Answer: Mass of CaCl2 = 3.42 g
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Given: Π = 0.75 atm, V = 2.5 L, T = 27 + 273 = 300 K, i = 2.47, M = 111 g/mol. Formula: Π = i * (w / (M * V)) * R * T => w = (Π * M * V) / (i * R * T) w = (0.75 * 111 * 2.5) / (2.47 * 0.0821 * 300) Numerator = 208.125 Denominator = 2.47 * 24.63 = 60.836 w = 208.125 / 60.836 ≈ 3.42 g.
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
Why is the molar mass of benzoic acid determined in benzene found to be nearly 244 g/mol instead of its actual molar mass of 122 g/mol?
Answer: Dimerization of benzoic acid in benzene
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In non-polar solvents like benzene, benzoic acid molecules undergo intermolecular hydrogen bonding between their carboxylic groups (-COOH), associating into stable dimers: 2 C6H5COOH <=> (C6H5COOH)2. Dimerization halves the total number of solute particles in solution. Since colligative properties are inversely proportional to molar mass, the experimental molar mass appears doubled (~244 g/mol).
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) State Raoult's law for a solution of two volatile liquids. (b) A solution is prepared by mixing 2 moles of liquid A (pA° = 400 mm Hg) and 3 moles of liquid B (pB° = 200 mm Hg). Assuming ideal behavior, calculate: (i) the vapour pressure of each component in solution, (ii) the total vapour pressure of the solution, (iii) the mole fraction of each component in the vapour phase.
Answer: Raoult's law derivation and complete numerical calculation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Raoult's Law Statement (1 mark): - For a solution of volatile liquids, the partial vapour pressure of each volatile component in the solution is directly proportional to its mole fraction: pA = pA° * χA and pB = pB° * χB. (b) Numerical (4 marks): - Total moles in liquid phase = 2 + 3 = 5 moles. - Mole fractions in liquid: χA = 2/5 = 0.40, χB = 3/5 = 0.60. (i) Partial vapour pressures: pA = pA° * χA = 400 mm Hg * 0.40 = 160 mm Hg. pB = pB° * χB = 200 mm Hg * 0.60 = 120 mm Hg. (ii) Total vapour pressure: p_total = pA + pB = 160 + 120 = 280 mm Hg. (iii) Mole fractions in vapour phase (Dalton's Law: y_i = p_i / p_total): yA = pA / p_total = 160 / 280 = 4 / 7 ≈ 0.571. yB = pB / p_total = 120 / 280 = 3 / 7 ≈ 0.429.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) Define van 't Hoff factor i. Write expressions relating i with: (i) degree of dissociation α, (ii) degree of association α. (b) A 0.2 molal aqueous solution of KCl freezes at -0.680 °C. Calculate the van 't Hoff factor and degree of dissociation of KCl. (Kf for water = 1.86 K kg mol⁻¹).
Answer: van 't Hoff factor theory, derivations, and degree of dissociation numerical
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Definition & Formulas (2 marks): - Definition: i = (Observed colligative property) / (Calculated colligative property) = (Normal molar mass) / (Abnormal molar mass). (i) For dissociation: A -> n B. Initial 1 mol, equilibrium: 1 - α + nα. Total particles = 1 + (n - 1)α => i = 1 + (n - 1)α => α = (i - 1) / (n - 1). (ii) For association: n A -> An. Equilibrium particles = 1 - α + α/n => i = 1 + (1/n - 1)α => α = (1 - i) / (1 - 1/n). (b) Numerical (3 marks): - m = 0.2 mol/kg, Kf = 1.86 K kg/mol, ΔTf_obs = 0 - (-0.680) = 0.680 K. - Calculated ΔTf = Kf * m = 1.86 * 0.2 = 0.372 K. - van 't Hoff factor i = ΔTf_obs / ΔTf_calc = 0.680 / 0.372 ≈ 1.828. - For KCl: KCl -> K⁺ + Cl⁻ (n = 2 ions). - Degree of dissociation α = (i - 1) / (n - 1) = (1.828 - 1) / (2 - 1) = 0.828 = 82.8%.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) What is elevation in boiling point? Derive the relationship between elevation in boiling point and molar mass of solute. (b) 18 g of glucose (C6H12O6) is dissolved in 1 kg of water in a saucepan. At what temperature will this water boil at 1.013 bar pressure? (Kb for water = 0.52 K kg mol⁻¹).
Answer: Elevation in boiling point derivation and boiling point calculation = 373.202 K
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Derivation (3 marks): - Elevation in boiling point ΔTb = Tb - Tb° is proportional to molality m: ΔTb = Kb * m. - Molality m = (w2 / M2) / (w1 / 1000) = (1000 * w2) / (M2 * w1), where w2 is mass of solute, M2 is molar mass of solute, and w1 is mass of solvent in grams. - Substituting: ΔTb = [ 1000 * Kb * w2 ] / [ M2 * w1 ]. - Rearranging for molar mass: M2 = [ 1000 * Kb * w2 ] / [ ΔTb * w1 ]. (b) Numerical (2 marks): - Solute: Glucose w2 = 18 g, M2 = 180 g/mol => moles = 18 / 180 = 0.1 mol. - Solvent: Water w1 = 1 kg. - Molality m = 0.1 mol / 1 kg = 0.1 m. - ΔTb = Kb * m = 0.52 K kg/mol * 0.1 mol/kg = 0.052 K. - Normal boiling point of water at 1.013 bar = 100 °C = 373.15 K. - Boiling point of solution Tb = Tb° + ΔTb = 373.15 + 0.052 = 373.202 K (or 100.052 °C).
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Intravenous Injections and Osmotic Pressure Red blood cells (erythrocytes) are bounded by semipermeable cell membranes. The fluid inside the cells has an osmotic pressure equal to that of a 0.9% (mass/volume) sodium chloride solution (normal saline). When preparing intravenous injections, the medicine must be dissolved in a vehicle that is strictly isotonic with blood. If a patient is injected with pure distilled water, water rushes into the cells via endosmosis, causing the cells to swell and burst (hemolysis). Conversely, hypertonic solutions cause water to rush out (crenation). (i) What type of solution is normal saline relative to human blood? (ii) What happens to red blood cells if suspended in 1.5% NaCl solution? (iii) Calculate the osmotic pressure of a 0.9% (w/v) NaCl solution at 37 °C (body temperature), assuming complete dissociation. (M of NaCl = 58.5 g/mol, R = 0.0821 L atm K⁻¹ mol⁻¹). (iv) State the phenomenon underlying the preservation of meat by salting and fruits by sugar syrup.
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Osmotic Pressure
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Normal saline is an isotonic solution with blood plasma (same osmotic pressure). (ii) A 1.5% NaCl solution is hypertonic (higher osmotic pressure than cellular fluid). Water flows out of erythrocytes by exosmosis, causing them to shrink and shrivel (crenation/plasmolysis). (iii) In 100 mL, mass = 0.9 g => In 1 L (1000 mL), mass w = 9.0 g. Moles n = 9.0 / 58.5 ≈ 0.1538 mol. Concentration C = 0.1538 mol/L. T = 37 + 273 = 310 K. For NaCl, i = 2. Π = i * C * R * T = 2 * (0.1538) * (0.0821) * (310) ≈ 7.83 atm. (iv) Osmosis (exosmosis): High salt or sugar concentration draws water out of bacterial and fungal cells via exosmosis, causing plasmolysis, dehydration, and death of microorganisms, preventing food spoilage.
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) Derive the relationship between depression in freezing point ΔTf and molar mass of solute M2. (b) 45 g of ethylene glycol (C2H6O2) is mixed with 600 g of water. Calculate: (i) the freezing point depression, (ii) the freezing point of the solution. (Kf for water = 1.86 K kg mol⁻¹).
Answer: Depression in freezing point derivation and freezing point calculation = -2.25 °C
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Derivation (3 marks): - Freezing point depression ΔTf = Tf° - Tf is directly proportional to molality m: ΔTf = Kf * m. - Molality m = (w2 / M2) / (w1 / 1000) = (1000 * w2) / (M2 * w1). - Substituting into formula: ΔTf = [ 1000 * Kf * w2 ] / [ M2 * w1 ]. - Rearranging for M2: M2 = [ 1000 * Kf * w2 ] / [ ΔTf * w1 ]. (b) Numerical (2 marks): - Molar mass of ethylene glycol C2H6O2 = 2(12) + 6(1) + 2(16) = 24 + 6 + 32 = 62 g/mol. - Moles of glycol = 45 / 62 ≈ 0.7258 mol. - Mass of water w1 = 600 g = 0.600 kg. - Molality m = 0.7258 / 0.600 ≈ 1.21 mol/kg. (i) Freezing point depression ΔTf = Kf * m = 1.86 K kg/mol * 1.21 mol/kg ≈ 2.25 K (or 2.25 °C). (ii) Freezing point of solution Tf = 0 °C - 2.25 °C = -2.25 °C (or 270.90 K).
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Explain positive and negative deviations from Raoult's law with vapour pressure-composition diagrams. (b) Give one example of each type and explain the molecular interactions responsible.
Answer: Detailed analysis of positive and negative deviations from Raoult's law
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Descriptions & Graphs: 1. Positive Deviation: Vapour pressure of each component and total vapour pressure is higher than predicted by Raoult's law (pA > pA° χA, pB > pB° χB, p_total > pA + pB). Graph shows curves bowing upward above the ideal linear dashed lines. 2. Negative Deviation: Vapour pressure of each component and total vapour pressure is lower than predicted by Raoult's law (pA < pA° χA, pB < pB° χB, p_total < pA + pB). Graph shows curves bowing downward below ideal lines. (b) Molecular Interactions & Examples: 1. Positive Deviation Example: Ethanol + Acetone. - Explanation: Intermolecular forces between solute and solvent (A-B) are weaker than those between pure components (A-A and B-B). Pure ethanol has strong hydrogen bonds. Adding acetone breaks these hydrogen bonds, allowing molecules to escape more easily into the vapour phase. ΔH_mix > 0 (endothermic), ΔV_mix > 0 (expansion). 2. Negative Deviation Example: Chloroform + Acetone (or Phenol + Aniline). - Explanation: Intermolecular forces between A-B are stronger than A-A and B-B. Chloroform and acetone form strong hydrogen bonds between the hydrogen of chloroform and carbonyl oxygen of acetone. Escaping tendency is reduced. ΔH_mix < 0 (exothermic), ΔV_mix < 0 (contraction).
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
A 100 g aqueous solution of a polymer containing 1.0 g of the polymer exerts an osmotic pressure of 0.20 mm Hg at 27 °C. Calculate the molar mass of the polymer. (R = 0.0821 L atm K⁻¹ mol⁻¹, 1 atm = 760 mm Hg).
Answer: Molar mass of polymer = 93,594 g/mol ≈ 9.36 × 10⁴ g/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Given: w2 = 1.0 g, V ≈ 100 mL = 0.10 L, T = 27 + 273 = 300 K. Osmotic pressure Π = 0.20 mm Hg = 0.20 / 760 atm ≈ 2.632 × 10⁻⁴ atm. Formula: Π = (w2 * R * T) / (M2 * V) => M2 = (w2 * R * T) / (Π * V) Substitute values: M2 = (1.0 * 0.0821 * 300) / [ (2.632 × 10⁻⁴) * 0.10 ] Numerator = 24.63 Denominator = 2.632 × 10⁻⁵ M2 = 24.63 / (2.632 × 10⁻⁵) ≈ 93,579 g/mol ≈ 9.36 × 10⁴ g/mol.
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) State the condition for reverse osmosis. Mention one large-scale application. (b) 2.0 g of benzoic acid (C6H5COOH) dissolved in 25.0 g of benzene shows a depression in freezing point equal to 1.62 K. Molal depression constant for benzene is 4.9 K kg mol⁻¹. If benzoic acid associates into dimers in benzene, calculate the percentage association of the acid.
Answer: Reverse osmosis condition and percentage association = 99.2%
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Reverse Osmosis (1.5 marks): - Condition: An external hydrostatic pressure greater than the osmotic pressure (P_ext > Π) must be applied to the solution side. - Application: Desalination of seawater to produce potable drinking water. (b) Numerical (3.5 marks): - Solute mass w2 = 2.0 g, solvent benzene w1 = 25.0 g = 0.025 kg, ΔTf = 1.62 K, Kf = 4.9 K kg/mol. - Molar mass of benzoic acid C7H6O2 = 7(12) + 6(1) + 2(16) = 84 + 6 + 32 = 122 g/mol. - Theoretical molality m = (2.0 / 122) / 0.025 = 0.6557 mol/kg. - Calculated ΔTf_calc = Kf * m = 4.9 * 0.6557 = 3.213 K. - van 't Hoff factor i = ΔTf_obs / ΔTf_calc = 1.62 / 3.213 ≈ 0.5042. - For dimerization (n = 2): 2 C6H5COOH <=> (C6H5COOH)2. i = 1 - α + α/2 = 1 - α/2 => α / 2 = 1 - i = 1 - 0.5042 = 0.4958 => α = 2 * 0.4958 = 0.9916 = 99.2%. - The percentage association of benzoic acid is 99.2%.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
A solution is prepared by dissolving 10.0 g of a non-volatile solute in 200 g of water. It has a vapour pressure of 31.60 mm Hg at 30 °C. The vapour pressure of pure water at 30 °C is 31.82 mm Hg. Calculate the molar mass of the solute.
Answer: Molar mass of solute = 126.8 g/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Given: p° = 31.82 mm Hg, p = 31.60 mm Hg, w2 = 10.0 g, w1 = 200 g, M1 (water) = 18 g/mol. Relative lowering of vapour pressure: (p° - p) / p° = (w2 * M1) / (M2 * w1) (31.82 - 31.60) / 31.82 = (10.0 * 18) / (M2 * 200) 0.22 / 31.82 = 180 / (200 * M2) = 0.90 / M2 0.006914 = 0.90 / M2 => M2 = 0.90 / 0.006914 ≈ 130.2 g/mol. (Using exact formula (p° - p)/p = n2/n1: 0.22/31.60 = (10/M2) / (200/18) = (10*18) / (200*M2) = 0.90/M2 => M2 = 0.90 * (31.60 / 0.22) = 129.27 g/mol).
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) State the reasons why abnormal molecular masses are observed for certain solutes. (b) Phenol associates in benzene to an extent of 75% to form dimers. Calculate the freezing point of a solution containing 2.0 g of phenol dissolved in 100 g of benzene. (Kf for benzene = 5.12 K kg mol⁻¹, freezing point of benzene = 5.5 °C, molar mass of phenol = 94 g/mol).
Answer: Abnormal molecular mass reasons and freezing point calculation = 4.86 °C
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Reasons for Abnormal Molecular Mass (2 marks): 1. Association of solute molecules: Solute molecules associate in non-polar solvents via hydrogen bonding or dimer formation (e.g. carboxylic acids in benzene). The number of particles decreases, colligative property decreases, and experimental molar mass becomes abnormally high. 2. Dissociation of solute into ions: Electrolytes (salts, acids, bases) dissociate into ions in polar solvents. The number of particles increases, colligative property increases, and experimental molar mass becomes abnormally low. (b) Numerical (3 marks): - Molar mass of phenol C6H5OH = 94 g/mol. - Degree of association α = 75% = 0.75, dimer formation n = 2. - van 't Hoff factor i = 1 - α/2 = 1 - 0.75/2 = 1 - 0.375 = 0.625. - Moles of phenol = 2.0 g / 94 g/mol = 0.02128 mol. - Molality m = 0.02128 mol / 0.100 kg = 0.2128 mol/kg. - Freezing point depression: ΔTf = i * Kf * m = 0.625 * 5.12 K kg/mol * 0.2128 mol/kg ≈ 0.681 °C. - Freezing point of solution Tf = Tf° - ΔTf = 5.5 °C - 0.681 °C = 4.82 °C.
Physical Chemistry
Chapter 2: Electrochemistry
9 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The unit of molar conductivity (Λm) is:
(a) S cm² mol⁻¹
(b) S cm⁻¹ mol
(c) S cm mol⁻¹
(d) S⁻¹ cm² mol
Answer: (a) S cm² mol⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Molar conductivity Λm = (κ * 1000) / M. Conductivity κ has unit S cm⁻¹ and concentration M has unit mol cm⁻³. Therefore, Λm = S cm⁻¹ / (mol cm⁻³) = S cm² mol⁻¹.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following statements is TRUE regarding the effect of dilution on conductivity (κ) and molar conductivity (Λm)?
(a) Both κ and Λm increase with dilution
(b) Both κ and Λm decrease with dilution
(c) κ decreases while Λm increases with dilution
(d) κ increases while Λm decreases with dilution
Answer: (c) κ decreases while Λm increases with dilution
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Conductivity κ decreases on dilution because the number of current-carrying ions per unit volume decreases. In contrast, molar conductivity Λm = κ * V increases on dilution because the increase in volume V containing 1 mole of electrolyte vastly outweighs the decrease in conductivity κ.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The cell potential E_cell is related to Gibbs free energy change ΔrG by the expression:
(a) ΔrG = - n F E_cell
(b) ΔrG = + n F E_cell
(c) ΔrG = - n F / E_cell
(d) ΔrG = - E_cell / (n F)
Answer: (a) ΔrG = - n F E_cell
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The maximum electrical work done by a galvanic cell is equal to the decrease in Gibbs free energy: W_max = - ΔrG = n F E_cell => ΔrG = - n F E_cell.
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
According to Kohlrausch's law, the limiting molar conductivity of an electrolyte AxBy is given by:
(a) x λ°_A + y λ°_B
(b) λ°_A + λ°_B
(c) (x + y)(λ°_A + λ°_B)
(d) x/y (λ°_A + λ°_B)
Answer: (a) x λ°_A + y λ°_B
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Kohlrausch's law of independent migration of ions states that limiting molar conductivity of an electrolyte is the sum of the individual contributions of the cation and anion: Λ°m(AxBy) = x λ°(A^(y+)) + y λ°(B^(x-)).
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
In a lead-acid storage battery, during discharging, the electrolyte H2SO4:
(a) is consumed and its density decreases
(b) is formed and density increases
(c) remains unchanged
(d) evaporates
Answer: (a) is consumed and its density decreases
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Overall discharging reaction: Pb(s) + PbO2(s) + 2 H2SO4(aq) -> 2 PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l). Sulfuric acid is continuously consumed and water is produced, causing the specific gravity of the battery acid to drop below 1.20 g/cm³.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The quantity of charge required to reduce 1 mole of Cr2O7²⁻ to Cr³⁺ in acidic medium is:
(a) 6 F
(b) 3 F
(c) 1 F
(d) 2 F
Answer: (a) 6 F
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Reduction half-reaction: Cr2O7²⁻ + 14 H⁺ + 6 e⁻ -> 2 Cr³⁺ + 7 H2O. Since 1 mole of Cr2O7²⁻ requires 6 moles of electrons, the quantity of charge required is 6 Faradays (6 F = 6 * 96500 C).
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
For the Daniell cell Zn | Zn²⁺ || Cu²⁺ | Cu, when an external opposing voltage E_ext > 1.1 V is applied:
(a) electrons flow from Zn to Cu
(b) electrons flow from Cu to Zn and current flows from Zn to Cu
(c) no current flows
(d) cell stops completely
Answer: (b) electrons flow from Cu to Zn and current flows from Zn to Cu
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
When E_ext > E_cell (1.1 V), the cell reverses its role and functions as an electrolytic cell: the non-spontaneous reverse reaction proceeds, electrons flow from Cu to Zn, and zinc dissolves at the cathode.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
The electrode potential of standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) is arbitrarily fixed as:
(a) 0.00 V
(b) 1.00 V
(c) -1.00 V
(d) 0.76 V
Answer: (a) 0.00 V
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
By IUPAC international convention, the standard reduction potential of the standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) Pt, H2(g, 1 bar) | H⁺(aq, 1 M) is assigned as 0.00 V at all temperatures.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
Which of the following metals is used for the cathodic protection of underground iron pipelines to prevent rusting?
(a) Zinc or Magnesium
(b) Copper
(c) Lead
(d) Silver
Answer: (a) Zinc or Magnesium
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Zinc and magnesium have more negative standard reduction potentials than iron (E°(Mg²⁺/Mg) = -2.37 V, E°(Zn²⁺/Zn) = -0.76 V vs E°(Fe²⁺/Fe) = -0.44 V). Acting as sacrificial anodes, they corrode preferentially, protecting the iron cathode.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
A conductivity cell has a cell constant of 0.5 cm⁻¹. When filled with 0.1 M KCl solution, its resistance is 100 Ω. The conductivity of the solution is:
(a) 0.005 S cm⁻¹
(b) 0.05 S cm⁻¹
(c) 50 S cm⁻¹
(d) 200 S cm⁻¹
Answer: (a) 0.005 S cm⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Conductivity κ = Cell constant (G*) / Resistance (R) = 0.5 cm⁻¹ / 100 Ω = 0.005 S cm⁻¹ (or 0.5 S m⁻¹).
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
In the H2-O2 fuel cell, the reaction occurring at the anode is:
(a) 2 H2(g) + 4 OH⁻(aq) -> 4 H2O(l) + 4 e⁻
(b) O2(g) + 2 H2O(l) + 4 e⁻ -> 4 OH⁻(aq)
(c) H⁺ + e⁻ -> 1/2 H2
(d) 2 H2O -> 2 H2 + O2
Answer: (a) 2 H2(g) + 4 OH⁻(aq) -> 4 H2O(l) + 4 e⁻
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In alkaline H2-O2 fuel cells, hydrogen gas is oxidized at the anode: 2 H2 + 4 OH⁻ -> 4 H2O + 4 e⁻. Oxygen gas is reduced at the cathode: O2 + 2 H2O + 4 e⁻ -> 4 OH⁻. Overall reaction: 2 H2 + O2 -> 2 H2O.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Which of the following represents the correct graph for variation of Λm with √c for a weak electrolyte like acetic acid (CH3COOH)?
(a) A steep curve rising asymptotically at infinite dilution (near c = 0)
(b) A straight line with negative slope
(c) A horizontal straight line
(d) A parabolic curve bowing downwards
Answer: (a) A steep curve rising asymptotically at infinite dilution (near c = 0)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For weak electrolytes, degree of dissociation α increases steeply on extreme dilution (Ostwald's dilution law). Λm rises sharply near c -> 0 and does not intercept the y-axis linearly, making it impossible to obtain Λ°m by linear extrapolation.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
How much electricity in Coulombs is required to deposit 27 g of aluminium from molten Al2O3? (Molar mass of Al = 27 g/mol, 1 F = 96500 C):
(a) 289500 C
(b) 96500 C
(c) 193000 C
(d) 32166 C
Answer: (a) 289500 C
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Al³⁺ + 3 e⁻ -> Al. Depositing 1 mole (27 g) of Al requires 3 moles of electrons = 3 Faradays. Charge Q = 3 * 96500 C = 289500 C.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The standard reduction potentials of four metals A, B, C, and D are -0.76 V, +0.34 V, -2.37 V, and +0.80 V respectively. The strongest reducing agent is:
(a) C
(b) A
(c) D
(d) B
Answer: (a) C
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The lower (more negative) the standard reduction potential, the greater the ease of oxidation, and hence the stronger the reducing agent. Metal C with E° = -2.37 V is the strongest reducing agent.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Rusting of iron is an electrochemical process where the cathode reaction is:
(a) O2(g) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 4 e⁻ -> 2 H2O(l)
(b) Fe(s) -> Fe²⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻
(c) Fe²⁺ + 2 e⁻ -> Fe
(d) 2 H2O -> O2 + 4 H⁺ + 4 e⁻
Answer: (a) O2(g) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 4 e⁻ -> 2 H2O(l)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In atmospheric corrosion (rusting), iron acts as anode (Fe -> Fe²⁺ + 2 e⁻), and dissolved oxygen in acidic moisture is reduced at cathodic areas: O2 + 4 H⁺ + 4 e⁻ -> 2 H2O.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
The equilibrium constant Kc for a cell reaction with E°_cell = 0.2955 V at 298 K (for n = 1) is:
(a) 10⁵
(b) 10¹⁰
(c) 10²
(d) 10⁻⁵
Answer: (a) 10⁵
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
E°_cell = (0.0591 / n) log Kc => 0.2955 = (0.0591 / 1) log Kc => log Kc = 0.2955 / 0.0591 = 5. Therefore, Kc = 10⁵.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Mercury cell gives a constant potential of 1.35 V throughout its operational life because:
(a) the overall cell reaction does not involve any ion in solution whose concentration can change
(b) it uses a liquid electrolyte
(c) its internal resistance is zero
(d) mercury is a liquid metal
Answer: (a) the overall cell reaction does not involve any ion in solution whose concentration can change
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Overall reaction: Zn(Hg) + HgO(s) -> ZnO(s) + Hg(l). Because there are no ions in solution whose concentration changes during discharge, its potential remains stable at 1.35 V until depleted.
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
During the electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride (brine) using inert platinum electrodes, the products obtained at cathode and anode are:
(a) H2 at cathode, Cl2 at anode
(b) Na at cathode, Cl2 at anode
(c) H2 at cathode, O2 at anode
(d) Na at cathode, O2 at anode
Answer: (a) H2 at cathode, Cl2 at anode
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
At cathode, reduction of water (H⁺) has higher potential than Na⁺, releasing H2 gas. At anode, chloride ions are oxidized preferentially over water due to oxygen overpotential, releasing Cl2 gas.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
The cell constant G* of a conductivity cell is determined using a standard solution of:
(a) KCl whose conductivity is accurately known
(b) NaCl
(c) HCl
(d) NaOH
Answer: (a) KCl whose conductivity is accurately known
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cell constant G* = l/A is difficult to measure geometrically. It is determined experimentally by measuring the resistance R of standard KCl solutions whose conductivities κ are known with high precision at various temperatures (G* = κ * R).
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
For the reaction: 2 Fe³⁺(aq) + 2 I⁻(aq) -> 2 Fe²⁺(aq) + I2(s), the value of standard cell potential is 0.236 V. The standard Gibbs free energy change ΔrG° is:
(a) -45.55 kJ/mol
(b) +45.55 kJ/mol
(c) -91.10 kJ/mol
(d) -22.77 kJ/mol
Answer: (a) -45.55 kJ/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Here n = 2 electrons. ΔrG° = - n F E°_cell = - 2 * 96500 C * 0.236 V = - 45548 J/mol = - 45.55 kJ/mol.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
If molar conductivities at infinite dilution of NaCl, HCl, and CH3COONa are 126.4, 425.9, and 91.0 S cm² mol⁻¹ respectively, Λ°m for CH3COOH is:
(a) 390.5 S cm² mol⁻¹
(b) 425.9 S cm² mol⁻¹
(c) 516.9 S cm² mol⁻¹
(d) 208.5 S cm² mol⁻¹
Answer: (a) 390.5 S cm² mol⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
By Kohlrausch's law: Λ°m(CH3COOH) = Λ°m(CH3COONa) + Λ°m(HCl) - Λ°m(NaCl) = 91.0 + 425.9 - 126.4 = 516.9 - 126.4 = 390.5 S cm² mol⁻¹.
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
A device that converts the chemical energy of a fuel directly into electrical energy is called a:
(a) Fuel cell
(b) Electrolytic cell
(c) Primary dry cell
(d) Lead-acid storage battery
Answer: (a) Fuel cell
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Fuel cells continuously convert the chemical energy of combustion of fuels (like hydrogen, methane, methanol) directly into electrical energy with high thermodynamic efficiency (~70%).
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
In a dry cell (Leclanché cell), manganese dioxide (MnO2) acts as:
(a) a depolarizer
(b) electrolyte
(c) reducing agent
(d) catalyst only
Answer: (a) a depolarizer
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
MnO2 surrounds the carbon rod cathode and acts as a depolarizer by oxidizing hydrogen gas to water (preventing hydrogen bubble build-up on the electrode) while manganese is reduced from Mn(IV) to Mn(III) as MnO(OH).
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Electrolysis of dilute aqueous H2SO4 using platinum electrodes produces:
(a) H2 at cathode and O2 at anode
(b) H2 at cathode and SO2 at anode
(c) O2 at cathode and H2 at anode
(d) SO4²⁻ at cathode
Answer: (a) H2 at cathode and O2 at anode
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In dilute sulfuric acid, water undergoes electrolysis: 2 H⁺ + 2 e⁻ -> H2 at cathode, and 2 H2O -> O2 + 4 H⁺ + 4 e⁻ at anode.
Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The standard reduction potentials are E°(Cu²⁺/Cu) = +0.34 V and E°(Ag⁺/Ag) = +0.80 V. The standard EMF of the cell Cu | Cu²⁺ || Ag⁺ | Ag is:
(a) +0.46 V
(b) -0.46 V
(c) +1.14 V
(d) +0.12 V

Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
E°_cell = E°_cathode - E°_anode = E°(Ag⁺/Ag) - E°(Cu²⁺/Cu) = +0.80 V - (+0.34 V) = +0.46 V.
Q26. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2024
Assertion (A): Conductivity of an electrolytic solution decreases with dilution. Reason (R): On dilution, the number of current-carrying ions per unit volume of the solution decreases.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. Conductivity κ is the conductance of 1 cm³ of solution. Dilution increases total volume, dispersing ions and decreasing ion count per unit volume.
Q27. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2023
Assertion (A): For a weak electrolyte, limiting molar conductivity (Λ°m) cannot be obtained by direct extrapolation of Λm vs √c plot. Reason (R): For weak electrolytes, the plot of Λm vs √c is not linear and curves upward asymptotically near zero concentration.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. Because degree of dissociation increases rapidly near infinite dilution, the curve becomes parallel to the y-axis, requiring Kohlrausch's law to determine Λ°m.
Q28. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Assertion (A): A galvanic cell stops functioning after some time. Reason (R): As the reaction proceeds, the cell potential gradually decreases and becomes zero when the reaction reaches chemical equilibrium.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. From Nernst equation E_cell = E°_cell - (0.0591/n) log Q: as reactants are consumed and products accumulate, Q increases until E_cell = 0 at equilibrium.
