Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
State Kohlrausch's law of independent migration of ions. Write its mathematical expression for an electrolyte of type AxBy.
Answer: Kohlrausch's law and mathematical expression
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Statement: Limiting molar conductivity of an electrolyte can be represented as the sum of the individual contributions of the anion and cation of the electrolyte at infinite dilution. 2. Expression: Λ°m(AxBy) = x λ°(A^(y+)) + y λ°(B^(x-)), where λ°(A^(y+)) and λ°(B^(x-)) are the limiting molar conductivities of the cation and anion respectively.
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
Write the Nernst equation for the following electrochemical cell at 298 K: Ni(s) | Ni²⁺(aq) || Ag⁺(aq) | Ag(s).
Answer: Nernst equation for Ni-Ag cell
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cell reaction: Ni(s) + 2 Ag⁺(aq) -> Ni²⁺(aq) + 2 Ag(s). Number of electrons transferred n = 2. Nernst equation at 298 K: E_cell = E°_cell - (0.0591 / 2) * log [ [Ni²⁺] / [Ag⁺]² ]. where E°_cell = E°(Ag⁺/Ag) - E°(Ni²⁺/Ni).
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
A solution of Ni(NO3)2 is electrolysed between platinum electrodes using a current of 5.0 amperes for 20 minutes. What mass of nickel is deposited at the cathode? (Molar mass of Ni = 58.7 g/mol, 1 F = 96500 C).
Answer: Mass of Ni deposited = 1.825 g
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cathode reaction: Ni²⁺ + 2 e⁻ -> Ni(s). Quantity of electricity Q = I * t = 5.0 A * (20 * 60 s) = 5.0 * 1200 = 6000 C. 2 moles of electrons (2 * 96500 C) deposit 1 mole (58.7 g) of Ni. Mass deposited w = (M * I * t) / (n * F) = (58.7 * 6000) / (2 * 96500) = 352200 / 193000 ≈ 1.825 g.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (2 Marks)
What is a fuel cell? Write the cathode and anode reactions of hydrogen-oxygen fuel cell.
Answer: Fuel cell definition and H2-O2 reactions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Definition: A galvanic cell that converts the chemical energy from the combustion of fuels (like hydrogen, carbon monoxide, methane) directly into electrical energy. 2. Reactions in alkaline H2-O2 fuel cell: - Anode: 2 H2(g) + 4 OH⁻(aq) -> 4 H2O(l) + 4 e⁻ - Cathode: O2(g) + 2 H2O(l) + 4 e⁻ -> 4 OH⁻(aq) - Overall reaction: 2 H2(g) + O2(g) -> 2 H2O(l).
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (3 Marks)
The electrical resistance of a column of 0.05 M NaOH solution of diameter 1 cm and length 50 cm is 5.55 × 10³ Ω. Calculate its resistivity, conductivity, and molar conductivity.
Answer: Resistivity = 87.135 Ω cm, Conductivity = 0.01148 S/cm, Molar conductivity = 229.6 S cm²/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Radius r = 0.5 cm, Area A = π r² = 3.1416 * 0.25 = 0.7854 cm², Length l = 50 cm. 1. Resistivity ρ = R * A / l = (5.55 × 10³ Ω * 0.7854 cm²) / 50 cm = 4358.97 / 50 ≈ 87.18 Ω cm. 2. Conductivity κ = 1 / ρ = 1 / 87.18 ≈ 0.01147 S cm⁻¹. 3. Molar Conductivity Λm = (κ * 1000) / M = (0.01147 * 1000) / 0.05 = 11.47 / 0.05 = 229.4 S cm² mol⁻¹.
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (2 Marks)
Write the chemistry of recharging the lead storage battery, highlighting all reactions at cathode and anode.
Answer: Recharging reactions of lead storage battery
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
During recharging, a direct current is passed from an external source, reversing the cell reactions: - At Cathode (Negative electrode during recharge): PbSO4(s) + 2 e⁻ -> Pb(s) + SO4²⁻(aq) - At Anode (Positive electrode during recharge): PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l) -> PbO2(s) + SO4²⁻(aq) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻ - Overall Recharging Reaction: 2 PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l) -> Pb(s) + PbO2(s) + 2 H2SO4(aq).
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (3 Marks)
Calculate the EMF and ΔrG of the cell: Mg(s) | Mg²⁺(0.001 M) || Cu²⁺(0.0001 M) | Cu(s) at 298 K. (Given E°(Mg²⁺/Mg) = -2.37 V, E°(Cu²⁺/Cu) = +0.34 V).
Answer: E_cell = 2.68 V, ΔrG = -517.2 kJ/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cell reaction: Mg(s) + Cu²⁺(aq) -> Mg²⁺(aq) + Cu(s) (n = 2). E°_cell = E°_cathode - E°_anode = +0.34 - (-2.37) = +2.71 V. Nernst equation: E_cell = E°_cell - (0.0591 / 2) log [ [Mg²⁺] / [Cu²⁺] ] = 2.71 - (0.02955) log [ 10⁻³ / 10⁻⁴ ] = 2.71 - 0.02955 * log(10) = 2.71 - 0.02955 ≈ 2.68 V. ΔrG = - n F E_cell = - 2 * 96500 * 2.68 J = - 517240 J/mol = - 517.24 kJ/mol.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (2 Marks)
Explain the electrochemical mechanism of rusting of iron.
Answer: Electrochemical mechanism of rusting
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Rusting occurs via miniature electrochemical cells set up on the iron surface in presence of moisture and dissolved oxygen: 1. Anodic Site: Fe(s) -> Fe²⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻ (E° = -0.44 V). 2. Cathodic Site: Electrons travel through metal to another spot where dissolved oxygen is reduced: O2(g) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 4 e⁻ -> 2 H2O(l) (E° = +1.23 V). 3. Overall Reaction: 2 Fe + O2 + 4 H⁺ -> 2 Fe²⁺ + 2 H2O. Fe²⁺ ions are further oxidized by atmospheric oxygen to form hydrated ferric oxide (rust: Fe2O3·xH2O).
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (3 Marks)
The conductivity of 0.001028 mol L⁻¹ acetic acid is 4.95 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹. Calculate its dissociation constant Ka if Λ°m for acetic acid is 390.5 S cm² mol⁻¹.
Answer: Ka = 1.78 × 10⁻⁵ mol/L
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Molar conductivity Λm = (κ * 1000) / c = (4.95 × 10⁻⁵ * 1000) / 0.001028 = 0.0495 / 0.001028 ≈ 48.15 S cm² mol⁻¹. Degree of dissociation α = Λm / Λ°m = 48.15 / 390.5 ≈ 0.1233. Dissociation constant Ka = (c * α²) / (1 - α) = [ 0.001028 * (0.1233)² ] / (1 - 0.1233) = [ 0.001028 * 0.0152 ] / 0.8767 = (1.563 × 10⁻⁵) / 0.8767 ≈ 1.78 × 10⁻⁵ mol L⁻¹.
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
Can you store copper sulfate solution in a zinc pot? Justify using standard electrode potentials (E°(Zn²⁺/Zn) = -0.76 V, E°(Cu²⁺/Cu) = +0.34 V).
Answer: No, zinc pot will react and get holes
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Standard potential for oxidation of zinc is higher than copper: E°(Zn²⁺/Zn) = -0.76 V is more negative than E°(Cu²⁺/Cu) = +0.34 V. Therefore, zinc is more reactive than copper and will spontaneously displace copper from copper sulfate solution: Zn(s) + Cu²⁺(aq) -> Zn²⁺(aq) + Cu(s). The zinc vessel will dissolve, forming holes. Hence, CuSO4 cannot be stored in a zinc pot.
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) Represent the electrochemical cell in which the reaction is: 2 Cr(s) + 3 Fe²⁺(0.01 M) -> 2 Cr³⁺(0.01 M) + 3 Fe(s). (b) Calculate the EMF of the cell at 298 K. (Given: E°(Cr³⁺/Cr) = -0.74 V, E°(Fe²⁺/Fe) = -0.44 V). (c) Calculate the maximum work that can be obtained from this cell.
Answer: Cell representation, Nernst EMF = +0.31 V, and maximum work = 179.5 kJ
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Cell Representation (1 mark): - Cr(s) | Cr³⁺(0.01 M) || Fe²⁺(0.01 M) | Fe(s). (b) EMF Calculation (2.5 marks): - E°_cell = E°_cathode - E°_anode = E°(Fe²⁺/Fe) - E°(Cr³⁺/Cr) = -0.44 V - (-0.74 V) = +0.30 V. - Number of electrons transferred n = 6 (2 Cr lose 6e⁻, 3 Fe²⁺ gain 6e⁻). - Reaction quotient Q = [Cr³⁺]² / [Fe²⁺]³ = (0.01)² / (0.01)³ = 1 / 0.01 = 100 = 10². - Nernst equation: E_cell = E°_cell - (0.0591 / n) log Q = +0.30 - (0.0591 / 6) log(10²) = 0.30 - (0.0591 / 6) * 2 = 0.30 - 0.0591 / 3 = 0.30 - 0.0197 = +0.2803 V ≈ 0.28 V. (c) Maximum Work (1.5 marks): - W_max = - ΔrG = n F E_cell = 6 * 96500 C * 0.2803 V = 162,293 J ≈ 162.3 kJ.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) State Faraday's first and second laws of electrolysis. (b) A steady current of 2.0 A is passed through three electrolytic cells A, B, and C containing solutions of ZnSO4, AgNO3, and CuSO4 connected in series until 1.45 g of silver is deposited at the cathode of cell B. How long did the current flow? What mass of copper and zinc were deposited? (Molar masses: Ag = 108 g/mol, Cu = 63.5 g/mol, Zn = 65.4 g/mol).
Answer: Faraday's laws and numerical calculations: time = 647.7 s, m_Cu = 0.426 g, m_Zn = 0.439 g
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Statements of Faraday's Laws (2 marks): - First Law: The mass of any substance deposited or liberated at any electrode is directly proportional to the quantity of electricity passed through the electrolyte: w = Z * Q = Z * I * t. - Second Law: When the same quantity of electricity is passed through different electrolytes connected in series, the masses of substances liberated are directly proportional to their chemical equivalent weights: w1 / w2 = E1 / E2. (b) Calculations (3 marks): - For Ag: Ag⁺ + e⁻ -> Ag. Equivalent weight E_Ag = 108 / 1 = 108. - Time required: w_Ag = (E_Ag * I * t) / F => 1.45 = (108 * 2.0 * t) / 96500 => t = (1.45 * 96500) / (216) = 139925 / 216 ≈ 647.8 seconds (10.8 minutes). - By Faraday's Second Law: w_Cu / w_Ag = E_Cu / E_Ag. E_Cu = 63.5 / 2 = 31.75. w_Cu = w_Ag * (E_Cu / E_Ag) = 1.45 * (31.75 / 108) ≈ 0.426 g. - For Zn: E_Zn = 65.4 / 2 = 32.7. w_Zn = w_Ag * (E_Zn / E_Ag) = 1.45 * (32.7 / 108) ≈ 0.439 g.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) Explain with a neat diagram the construction and working of a lead storage battery. (b) Write the chemical reactions occurring at both electrodes during: (i) discharging, (ii) recharging.
Answer: Complete theory and reactions of lead storage battery
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Construction & Working (2 marks): - Anode: Spongy lead (Pb) plates. - Cathode: Lead grid packed with lead dioxide (PbO2). - Electrolyte: 38% aqueous sulfuric acid solution (density ≈ 1.30 g/cm³). (b) Electrode Reactions (3 marks): (i) During Discharging: - At Anode: Pb(s) + SO4²⁻(aq) -> PbSO4(s) + 2 e⁻ - At Cathode: PbO2(s) + SO4²⁻(aq) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻ -> PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l) - Overall: Pb(s) + PbO2(s) + 2 H2SO4(aq) -> 2 PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l) (E_cell ≈ 2.0 V per cell, 12 V for 6 cells). (ii) During Recharging (Reverses the discharging reactions): - At Cathode: PbSO4(s) + 2 e⁻ -> Pb(s) + SO4²⁻(aq) - At Anode: PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l) -> PbO2(s) + SO4²⁻(aq) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻ - Overall: 2 PbSO4(s) + 2 H2O(l) -> Pb(s) + PbO2(s) + 2 H2SO4(aq).
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Commercial Electrochemical Cells Electrochemical cells are classified into primary and secondary cells. In primary cells, the reaction occurs only once, and after use over a period of time, the battery dies and cannot be reused (e.g. Leclanché dry cell and mercury button cell). In secondary cells, after discharging, the cell can be recharged by passing electric current through it in the opposite direction (e.g. Lead-acid accumulator and nickel-cadmium cell). Another category is fuel cells, which run continuously as long as fuel reactants are supplied. (i) Why cannot a primary dry cell be recharged? (ii) What is the electrolyte used in a mercury cell? (iii) Write the overall cell reaction of a mercury cell. (iv) State two major advantages of hydrogen-oxygen fuel cells over conventional thermal power plants.
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Commercial Batteries
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) In a primary cell, electrode reactions are chemically irreversible; internal components degrade and reaction products cannot be restored to original states by reversing the current. (ii) A moist paste of potassium hydroxide (KOH) and zinc oxide (ZnO). (iii) Zn(Hg) + HgO(s) -> ZnO(s) + Hg(l). (iv) Advantages of fuel cells: (1) Very high thermodynamic efficiency (~70% compared to ~40% for thermal power plants). (2) Eco-friendly and pollution-free (by-product is clean pure water, unlike toxic greenhouse gases emitted by coal plants).
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) Define conductivity and molar conductivity for an electrolytic solution. How do they vary with concentration? (b) The resistance of a conductivity cell containing 0.001 M KCl solution at 298 K is 1500 Ω. What is the cell constant if conductivity of 0.001 M KCl at 298 K is 0.146 × 10⁻³ S cm⁻¹?
Answer: Conductivity definitions, dilution trends, and cell constant calculation G* = 0.219 cm⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Definitions & Trends (3 marks): - Conductivity (κ): The conductance of a solution of 1 cm length with cross-sectional area of 1 cm² (conductance of unit volume). It decreases on dilution because the number of ions per unit volume decreases. - Molar Conductivity (Λm): The conducting power of all the ions produced by dissolving one mole of an electrolyte in solution: Λm = (κ * 1000) / M. It increases on dilution because the increase in volume V containing 1 mole of electrolyte compensates for the decrease in κ. - For strong electrolytes, Λm increases slowly following Debye-Hückel-Onsager equation: Λm = Λ°m - A √c. - For weak electrolytes, Λm increases steeply at high dilution due to increased ionization. (b) Numerical (2 marks): - Given: R = 1500 Ω, κ = 0.146 × 10⁻³ S cm⁻¹. - Cell constant G* = κ * R = (0.146 × 10⁻³ S cm⁻¹) * (1500 Ω) = 0.219 cm⁻¹ (or 21.9 m⁻¹).
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) State the relationship between standard cell potential E°_cell, equilibrium constant Kc, and standard Gibbs energy ΔrG°. (b) Calculate the equilibrium constant Kc and ΔrG° for the reaction at 298 K: Ni(s) + 2 Ag⁺(aq) -> Ni²⁺(aq) + 2 Ag(s). (Given: E°(Ag⁺/Ag) = +0.80 V, E°(Ni²⁺/Ni) = -0.25 V).
Answer: E°_cell = +1.05 V, ΔrG° = -202.65 kJ/mol, Kc = 3.6 × 10³⁵
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Relationships (1.5 marks): 1. ΔrG° = - n F E°_cell. 2. ΔrG° = - 2.303 R T log Kc => log Kc = (n F E°_cell) / (2.303 R T) = (n E°_cell) / 0.0591 at 298 K. (b) Numerical (3.5 marks): - E°_cell = E°(Ag⁺/Ag) - E°(Ni²⁺/Ni) = +0.80 - (-0.25) = +1.05 V. - n = 2 electrons. - ΔrG° = - n F E°_cell = - 2 * 96500 C * 1.05 V = - 202,650 J/mol = - 202.65 kJ/mol. - log Kc = (n E°_cell) / 0.0591 = (2 * 1.05) / 0.0591 = 2.10 / 0.0591 ≈ 35.533. - Kc = antilog(35.533) = 10^(0.533) × 10³⁵ ≈ 3.41 × 10³⁵.
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
Predict the products of electrolysis in each of the following cases: (i) An aqueous solution of AgNO3 with silver electrodes. (ii) An aqueous solution of AgNO3 with platinum electrodes. (iii) A dilute solution of H2SO4 with platinum electrodes. (iv) An aqueous solution of CuCl2 with platinum electrodes.
Answer: Electrolysis products under four distinct conditions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Aqueous AgNO3 with silver electrodes (attackable electrodes): - Cathode: Ag⁺(aq) + e⁻ -> Ag(s) (silver deposits). - Anode: Ag(s) -> Ag⁺(aq) + e⁻ (silver anode dissolves). (ii) Aqueous AgNO3 with platinum electrodes (inert electrodes): - Cathode: Ag⁺(aq) + e⁻ -> Ag(s) (silver deposits). - Anode: 2 H2O(l) -> O2(g) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 4 e⁻ (oxygen gas is liberated). (iii) Dilute H2SO4 with platinum electrodes: - Cathode: 2 H⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻ -> H2(g) (hydrogen gas evolves). - Anode: 2 H2O(l) -> O2(g) + 4 H⁺(aq) + 4 e⁻ (oxygen gas evolves). (iv) Aqueous CuCl2 with platinum electrodes: - Cathode: Cu²⁺(aq) + 2 e⁻ -> Cu(s) (copper metal deposits). - Anode: 2 Cl⁻(aq) -> Cl2(g) + 2 e⁻ (chlorine gas evolves due to lower overpotential than O2).
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) What is corrosion? Name two factors that promote corrosion. (b) Explain why iron does not rust when coated with zinc, even when the zinc coating is scratched and broken.
Answer: Corrosion definition, factors, and sacrificial protection of zinc
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Definition & Factors (2 marks): - Corrosion: The slow, spontaneous deterioration and eating away of a metal surface by chemical or electrochemical reaction with atmospheric moisture, oxygen, and other gases. - Promoting Factors: (1) Presence of moisture and oxygen/air, (2) Presence of dissolved electrolytes (salts/acids in water), (3) Strained or rough metal surfaces. (b) Sacrificial Protection (3 marks): - Standard reduction potentials: E°(Zn²⁺/Zn) = -0.76 V, E°(Fe²⁺/Fe) = -0.44 V. - Because zinc has a more negative reduction potential than iron, zinc has a higher tendency to get oxidized (Zn -> Zn²⁺ + 2 e⁻). - When zinc-coated iron (galvanized iron) is scratched, an electrochemical cell is formed with zinc and iron exposed to moisture. Zinc acts as the anode and iron acts as the cathode. - Electrons released by zinc flow to iron, preventing iron from losing electrons. Thus, zinc oxidizes sacrificially, protecting the underlying iron from rusting completely until the zinc is consumed.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
A cell is constructed by dipping a zinc rod in 0.1 M ZnSO4 solution and a lead rod in 0.02 M Pb(NO3)2 solution. Calculate the EMF of the cell at 298 K. (Given: E°(Zn²⁺/Zn) = -0.76 V, E°(Pb²⁺/Pb) = -0.13 V).
Answer: E_cell = +0.609 V
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cell reaction: Zn(s) + Pb²⁺(aq) -> Zn²⁺(aq) + Pb(s) (n = 2). E°_cell = E°(Pb²⁺/Pb) - E°(Zn²⁺/Zn) = -0.13 V - (-0.76 V) = +0.63 V. Nernst equation at 298 K: E_cell = E°_cell - (0.0591 / 2) * log [ [Zn²⁺] / [Pb²⁺] ] = 0.63 - (0.02955) * log [ 0.1 / 0.02 ] = 0.63 - 0.02955 * log(5) Since log(5) = 0.6990: E_cell = 0.63 - 0.02955 * 0.6990 = 0.63 - 0.0207 = +0.6093 V ≈ 0.61 V.
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) State the relationship between cell constant, resistance, and conductivity. (b) The molar conductivity of 0.025 mol L⁻¹ methanoic acid (HCOOH) is 46.1 S cm² mol⁻¹. Calculate its degree of dissociation α and dissociation constant Ka. (Given: λ°(H⁺) = 349.6 S cm² mol⁻¹, λ°(HCOO⁻) = 54.6 S cm² mol⁻¹).
Answer: Cell constant relations, α = 0.1138, and Ka = 3.67 × 10⁻⁴ mol/L
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Relationships (1.5 marks): 1. Conductance G = 1 / R. 2. Conductivity κ = G * (l / A) = (1 / R) * G*, where G* = l/A is the cell constant. 3. Cell constant G* = κ * R. (b) Numerical (3.5 marks): - By Kohlrausch's law for HCOOH: Λ°m(HCOOH) = λ°(H⁺) + λ°(HCOO⁻) = 349.6 + 54.6 = 404.2 S cm² mol⁻¹. - Degree of dissociation α = Λm / Λ°m = 46.1 / 404.2 ≈ 0.1140 (or 11.4%). - Concentration c = 0.025 mol/L. - Dissociation constant Ka = (c * α²) / (1 - α) Ka = [ 0.025 * (0.1140)² ] / (1 - 0.1140) = [ 0.025 * 0.0130 ] / 0.8860 = (3.249 × 10⁻⁴) / 0.8860 ≈ 3.67 × 10⁻⁴ mol L⁻¹.
Physical Chemistry
Chapter 3: Chemical Kinetics
7 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The unit of rate constant for a zero order reaction is:
(a) mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹
(b) s⁻¹
(c) L mol⁻¹ s⁻¹
(d) mol⁻¹ L s⁻¹
Answer: (a) mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
General unit of rate constant is (mol L⁻¹)^(1 - n) s⁻¹, where n is reaction order. For zero order (n = 0): unit is (mol L⁻¹)^(1 - 0) s⁻¹ = mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The half-life of a first order reaction is 69.3 seconds. The rate constant k of the reaction is:
(a) 0.01 s⁻¹
(b) 0.1 s⁻¹
(c) 10 s⁻¹
(d) 0.001 s⁻¹
Answer: (a) 0.01 s⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For a first order reaction: t_1/2 = 0.693 / k => k = 0.693 / t_1/2 = 0.693 / 69.3 s = 0.01 s⁻¹ = 10⁻² s⁻¹.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
For a reaction A + B -> Products, doubling the concentration of A quadruples the rate, while doubling B has no effect on rate. The rate law is:
(a) Rate = k [A]²
(b) Rate = k [A] [B]
(c) Rate = k [A]² [B]
(d) Rate = k [A]
Answer: (a) Rate = k [A]²
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
When [A] is doubled, Rate increases 4-fold (2² = 4), meaning order with respect to A is 2. When [B] is doubled, Rate is unchanged (2⁰ = 1), meaning order with respect to B is 0. Rate = k [A]² [B]⁰ = k [A]².
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
A catalyst increases the rate of a chemical reaction by:
(a) lowering the activation energy
(b) increasing the activation energy
(c) increasing enthalpy change ΔH
(d) changing the equilibrium constant
Answer: (a) lowering the activation energy
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
A catalyst provides an alternative reaction pathway with a lower activation energy barrier (Ea), allowing a vastly larger fraction of colliding molecules to possess sufficient kinetic energy to react. It does not alter ΔH, ΔG, or K_eq.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which of the following is an example of a pseudo-first-order reaction?
(a) Acid-catalyzed hydrolysis of ethyl acetate
(b) Thermal decomposition of HI
(c) Radioactive decay
(d) Decomposition of N2O5
Answer: (a) Acid-catalyzed hydrolysis of ethyl acetate
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Hydrolysis of ethyl acetate: CH3COOC2H5 + H2O -(H⁺)-> CH3COOH + C2H5OH. Water is present in such huge excess that its concentration remains practically constant throughout. The reaction obeys pseudo-first-order kinetics: Rate = k' [CH3COOC2H5].
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The slope of the line in the Arrhenius plot of ln k versus 1/T is equal to:
(a) - Ea / R
(b) + Ea / R
(c) - Ea / (2.303 R)
(d) - R / Ea
Answer: (a) - Ea / R
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Arrhenius equation: ln k = ln A - (Ea / R) * (1 / T). Comparing with linear equation y = c + m x, plotting y = ln k against x = 1/T yields slope m = - Ea / R. (For log10 k, slope is - Ea / (2.303 R)).
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Molecularity of an elementary chemical reaction CANNOT be:
(a) zero or fractional
(b) 1
(c) 2
(d) 3
Answer: (a) zero or fractional
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Molecularity is the number of reactant species colliding simultaneously in an elementary step. Because collision requires actual, whole physical particles, molecularity must be a non-zero positive integer (1, 2, or rarely 3) and can never be zero, fractional, or negative.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
For a zero order reaction R -> P, the plot of reactant concentration [R] versus time t gives a straight line with slope equal to:
(a) - k
(b) + k
(c) - k / 2.303
(d) - k / 2
Answer: (a) - k
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Integrated rate equation for zero order: [R] = - k t + [R]₀. A plot of [R] on the y-axis against t on the x-axis gives a straight line with slope m = - k and intercept = [R]₀.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
In a first-order reaction, the time taken for 99.9% completion of the reaction is roughly how many times the half-life t_1/2?
(a) 10 times
(b) 2 times
(c) 4 times
(d) 100 times
Answer: (a) 10 times
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For 99.9% completion: [R] = [R]₀ - 0.999 [R]₀ = 0.001 [R]₀ = 10⁻³ [R]₀. t_99.9% = (2.303 / k) log([R]₀ / 10⁻³ [R]₀) = (2.303 / k) log(10³) = 3 * (2.303 / k) = 3 * (2.303 / (0.693 / t_1/2)) = 3 * (2.303 / 0.693) t_1/2 ≈ 3 * 3.32 t_1/2 ≈ 10 * t_1/2.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
For the reaction 2 N2O5(g) -> 4 NO2(g) + O2(g), the rate of disappearance of N2O5 is related to the rate of formation of NO2 as:
(a) - 1/2 d[N2O5]/dt = + 1/4 d[NO2]/dt
(b) - d[N2O5]/dt = + d[NO2]/dt
(c) - 2 d[N2O5]/dt = + 4 d[NO2]/dt
(d) - 1/4 d[N2O5]/dt = + 1/2 d[NO2]/dt
Answer: (a) - 1/2 d[N2O5]/dt = + 1/4 d[NO2]/dt
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Rate of reaction is expressed by dividing the rate of change of each species by its stoichiometric coefficient: Rate = - 1/2 d[N2O5]/dt = + 1/4 d[NO2]/dt = + d[O2]/dt.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
If the concentration is expressed in mol L⁻¹ and time in minutes, the unit of rate of reaction is:
(a) mol L⁻¹ min⁻¹
(b) mol L min⁻¹
(c) mol⁻¹ L min
(d) min⁻¹
Answer: (a) mol L⁻¹ min⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Rate of reaction = Change in concentration / Time taken = (mol L⁻¹) / min = mol L⁻¹ min⁻¹.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
The rate constant of a reaction is k = 3.2 × 10⁻⁴ L mol⁻¹ s⁻¹. The overall order of the reaction is:
(a) Second order
(b) First order
(c) Zero order
(d) Third order
Answer: (a) Second order
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Units of rate constant are (mol L⁻¹)^(1 - n) s⁻¹ = L^(n - 1) mol^(1 - n) s⁻¹. Here unit is L mol⁻¹ s⁻¹, which matches n - 1 = 1 => n = 2 (Second order).
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
For a reaction, the temperature coefficient is 2. By what factor will the reaction rate increase if temperature is raised from 20 °C to 50 °C?
(a) 8 times
(b) 6 times
(c) 4 times
(d) 16 times
Answer: (a) 8 times
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
ΔT = 50 - 20 = 30 °C. The number of 10 °C increments is n = 30 / 10 = 3. Increase factor = (Temperature coefficient)^n = 2³ = 8 times.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
A first order reaction is 50% completed in 20 minutes. The time required for 75% completion of the reaction is:
(a) 40 minutes
(b) 60 minutes
(c) 30 minutes
(d) 80 minutes
Answer: (a) 40 minutes
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
75% completion means 25% remains: [R] = [R]₀ / 4 = [R]₀ / 2². This represents 2 half-lives: t_75% = 2 * t_1/2 = 2 * 20 min = 40 minutes.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
For an endothermic reaction where ΔH is positive, the minimum value of activation energy (Ea) must be:
(a) greater than ΔH
(b) less than ΔH
(c) equal to zero
(d) equal to ΔH / 2
Answer: (a) greater than ΔH
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For an endothermic reaction: ΔH = Ea(forward) - Ea(backward). Because the reverse activation energy Ea(backward) must be greater than zero, Ea(forward) = ΔH + Ea(backward) > ΔH.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
In the collision theory of chemical reactions, the steric factor P accounts for:
(a) proper spatial orientation of colliding molecules
(b) activation energy
(c) temperature of the system
(d) collision frequency alone
Answer: (a) proper spatial orientation of colliding molecules
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
According to modified collision theory (Rate = P * Z_AB * exp(-Ea/RT)), the steric factor (probability factor P) accounts for the fraction of collisions in which reacting molecules collide with the proper spatial geometric orientation to facilitate bond breaking and formation.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
If the rate of a reaction is independent of the concentration of all reactants, the reaction is:
(a) zero order
(b) first order
(c) second order
(d) fractional order
Answer: (a) zero order
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
By definition, a zero order reaction has Rate = k [A]⁰ = k, meaning the reaction rate is completely independent of reactant concentration.
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The decomposition of phosphine (PH3) on tungsten at low pressure is a first order reaction, but at high pressure it becomes zero order because:
(a) surface of tungsten becomes fully saturated at high pressure
(b) rate constant decreases
(c) tungsten melts
(d) PH3 dissociates completely
Answer: (a) surface of tungsten becomes fully saturated at high pressure
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
At high pressure, all active catalytic sites on the tungsten metal surface become completely occupied (saturated) with adsorbed PH3 molecules. Further increase in gas pressure cannot increase the surface concentration, causing the rate to become independent of pressure (zero order).
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
The rate constant k for a reaction is found to double when temperature is increased from 300 K to 310 K. The activation energy Ea of the reaction is approximately (R = 8.314 J K⁻¹ mol⁻¹):
(a) 53.6 kJ/mol
(b) 100 kJ/mol
(c) 25.2 kJ/mol
(d) 12.5 kJ/mol
Answer: (a) 53.6 kJ/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
log(k2/k1) = (Ea / (2.303 R)) * [ (T2 - T1) / (T1 * T2) ]. log(2) = 0.3010 = [ Ea / (2.303 * 8.314) ] * [ 10 / (300 * 310) ] 0.3010 = [ Ea / 19.147 ] * [ 10 / 93000 ] = [ Ea / 19.147 ] * (1.075 × 10⁻⁴) => Ea = (0.3010 * 19.147) / (1.075 × 10⁻⁴) ≈ 5.763 / (1.075 × 10⁻⁴) ≈ 53,600 J/mol = 53.6 kJ/mol.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
For a first order reaction, a plot of log [R] versus time t gives a straight line with slope equal to:
(a) - k / 2.303
(b) - k
(c) + k / 2.303
(d) 2.303 / k
Answer: (a) - k / 2.303
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
First order equation: log [R] = - (k / 2.303) t + log [R]₀. Plotting log [R] on y-axis against t on x-axis gives a straight line with slope m = - k / 2.303.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
A reaction has activation energy Ea = 0. Its rate constant k:
(a) is independent of temperature and equals the frequency factor A
(b) becomes infinite
(c) becomes zero
(d) increases exponentially with temperature
Answer: (a) is independent of temperature and equals the frequency factor A
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
From Arrhenius equation: k = A exp(-Ea / RT). If Ea = 0, exp(0) = 1, so k = A at all temperatures (the reaction rate is completely independent of temperature, meaning every collision with proper orientation leads to reaction).
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
The rate law for a reaction A + 2 B -> C is Rate = k [A]^(1/2) [B]². The overall order of reaction is:
(a) 2.5 (or 5/2)
(b) 3
(c) 1.5
(d) 2
Answer: (a) 2.5 (or 5/2)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Overall order is the sum of exponents of concentration terms in the rate law: Order = 1/2 + 2 = 2.5 (or 5/2).
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following is NOT affected by the presence of a catalyst in a reversible reaction?
(a) Equilibrium constant K_eq and enthalpy of reaction ΔH
(b) Activation energy
(c) Rate of forward reaction
(d) Rate of backward reaction
Answer: (a) Equilibrium constant K_eq and enthalpy of reaction ΔH
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
A catalyst speeds up both forward and reverse reactions equally by lowering activation energy by the same amount. It has zero effect on standard free energy change ΔG°, enthalpy change ΔH, and the equilibrium constant K_eq.
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The half-life period t_1/2 for a zero order reaction is proportional to:
(a) [R]₀
(b) 1 / [R]₀
(c) [R]₀²
(d) independent of [R]₀
Answer: (a) [R]₀
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For a zero order reaction: t_1/2 = [R]₀ / (2k). Therefore, t_1/2 is directly proportional to initial concentration [R]₀.
Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
For a gaseous reaction: 2 A(g) -> B(g) + C(g), the rate law is Rate = k pA². If total pressure increases, the rate will:
(a) increase
(b) decrease
(c) remain constant
(d) become zero
Answer: (a) increase
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Increasing total pressure compresses the gas mixture, increasing the partial pressure pA of reactant A. Since Rate ∝ pA², the rate of reaction increases proportionally to the square of partial pressure.
Q26. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2024
Assertion (A): Order of a reaction can be zero or fractional, but molecularity is always a positive integer. Reason (R): Order is an experimentally determined quantity, whereas molecularity is theoretical and represents the number of molecules colliding simultaneously in an elementary step.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R correctly explains A. Because collision involves whole discrete particles, molecularity cannot be fractional or zero, whereas order is an empirical power fit.
Q27. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2023
Assertion (A): The rate of reaction generally doubles for every 10 °C rise in temperature. Reason (R): An increase in temperature increases the total collision frequency by roughly 100%.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (c) A is true but R is false.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Assertion is true. Reason is false: A 10 °C rise increases collision frequency by only 1-2% (Z ∝ √T). The doubling of rate is caused by a dramatic increase (often 100-200%) in the fraction of molecules with kinetic energy exceeding activation energy (Boltzmann factor exp(-Ea/RT)).

(c) A is true but R is false.

Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A directly.

(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.

Q30. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2020
Assertion (A): A catalyst increases the speed of a reaction without changing the position of equilibrium. Reason (R): A catalyst lowers the activation energy of both the forward and reverse reactions by an equal amount.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A. Because forward and backward rates increase by the exact same ratio, equilibrium is reached faster without shifting equilibrium concentrations.
Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
Distinguish between order and molecularity of a chemical reaction (any two points).
Answer: Differences between order and molecularity
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Definition: Order is the sum of powers of concentration terms of reactants in the experimentally determined rate law. Molecularity is the total number of reacting species colliding simultaneously in an elementary step. 2. Nature of Values: Order can be zero, fractional, integer, or negative, and is determined solely by experiment. Molecularity is always a non-zero positive integer (1, 2, or 3) and cannot be zero or fractional. 3. Applicability: Order applies to both elementary and complex reactions. Molecularity has meaning only for elementary steps.
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
Derive the integrated rate equation for a zero-order reaction: R -> Products.
Answer: Derivation of zero-order integrated rate equation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For zero-order reaction: Rate = - d[R]/dt = k [R]⁰ = k. => d[R] = - k dt. Integrating both sides: ∫ d[R] = - k ∫ dt => [R] = - k t + I, where I is integration constant. At t = 0: [R] = [R]₀ => I = [R]₀. Substituting I: [R] = - k t + [R]₀. Rearranging for rate constant: k = ( [R]₀ - [R] ) / t.
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
A first order reaction has a rate constant k = 1.15 × 10⁻³ s⁻¹. How long will 5 g of this reactant take to reduce to 3 g?
Answer: Time required t = 444 seconds
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
First order equation: t = (2.303 / k) * log([R]₀ / [R]). Given: k = 1.15 × 10⁻³ s⁻¹, [R]₀ = 5 g, [R] = 3 g. [R]₀ / [R] = 5 / 3 = 1.667. log(1.667) = log 5 - log 3 = 0.6990 - 0.4771 = 0.2219. t = [ 2.303 / (1.15 × 10⁻³) ] * 0.2219 = (2002.6) * 0.2219 ≈ 444.4 seconds (or ~7.4 minutes).
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (2 Marks)
Show that for a first order reaction, the time required for 99% completion is twice the time required for 90% completion.
Answer: Proof of t_99% = 2 * t_90%
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For first order reaction: t = (2.303 / k) * log([R]₀ / [R]). 1. For 90% completion: [R] = [R]₀ - 0.90 [R]₀ = 0.10 [R]₀ = [R]₀ / 10. t_90% = (2.303 / k) * log(10) = 2.303 / k ... (1) 2. For 99% completion: [R] = [R]₀ - 0.99 [R]₀ = 0.01 [R]₀ = [R]₀ / 100. t_99% = (2.303 / k) * log(100) = (2.303 / k) * 2 = 2 * (2.303 / k) ... (2) Dividing (2) by (1): t_99% / t_90% = [ 2 * (2.303 / k) ] / [ 2.303 / k ] = 2. Therefore, t_99% = 2 * t_90%. (Proven).
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (3 Marks)
The rate of a reaction triples when the temperature changes from 20 °C to 50 °C. Calculate the energy of activation for this reaction. (R = 8.314 J K⁻¹ mol⁻¹, log 3 = 0.4771).
Answer: Activation energy Ea = 28.8 kJ/mol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Given: T1 = 20 + 273 = 293 K, T2 = 50 + 273 = 323 K, k2 / k1 = 3. Arrhenius equation: log(k2 / k1) = [ Ea / (2.303 * R) ] * [ (T2 - T1) / (T1 * T2) ] log(3) = [ Ea / (2.303 * 8.314) ] * [ (323 - 293) / (293 * 323) ] 0.4771 = [ Ea / 19.147 ] * [ 30 / 94639 ] = [ Ea / 19.147 ] * (3.170 × 10⁻⁴) Ea = (0.4771 * 19.147) / (3.170 × 10⁻⁴) = 9.135 / (3.170 × 10⁻⁴) ≈ 28,817 J/mol ≈ 28.82 kJ/mol.
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (2 Marks)
Define: (i) Pseudo-first-order reaction, (ii) Activation energy.
Answer: Definitions of pseudo-first-order and activation energy
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Pseudo-First-Order Reaction: A higher-order reaction that behaves kinetics-wise as a first-order reaction because one of the reacting reactants is present in large excess compared to the other. 2. Activation Energy (Ea): The minimum extra energy that reacting molecules must absorb above their ground state average kinetic energy to reach the transition state (threshold energy) and form products.
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (3 Marks)
The following results were obtained during kinetic studies of the reaction: 2 A + B -> C + D: Exp 1: [A] = 0.1 M, [B] = 0.1 M, Initial Rate = 6.0 × 10⁻³ M/min Exp 2: [A] = 0.3 M, [B] = 0.2 M, Initial Rate = 7.2 × 10⁻² M/min Exp 3: [A] = 0.3 M, [B] = 0.4 M, Initial Rate = 2.88 × 10⁻¹ M/min Exp 4: [A] = 0.4 M, [B] = 0.1 M, Initial Rate = 2.4 × 10⁻² M/min Determine the rate law and rate constant for the reaction.
Answer: Rate = k [A] [B]², k = 6.0 L² mol⁻² min⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Let Rate = k [A]^x [B]^y. Comparing Exp 1 and Exp 4 ([B] is constant at 0.1 M): Rate4 / Rate1 = (2.4 × 10⁻²) / (6.0 × 10⁻³) = 4. [A]4 / [A]1 = 0.4 / 0.1 = 4. 4 = 4^x => x = 1 (First order in A). Comparing Exp 2 and Exp 3 ([A] is constant at 0.3 M): Rate3 / Rate2 = (2.88 × 10⁻¹) / (7.2 × 10⁻²) = 4. [B]3 / [B]2 = 0.4 / 0.2 = 2. 4 = 2^y => 2² = 2^y => y = 2 (Second order in B). Rate Law: Rate = k [A] [B]². Overall order = 1 + 2 = 3. Calculating k using Exp 1: 6.0 × 10⁻³ = k (0.1) (0.1)² = k * 10⁻³ => k = 6.0 L² mol⁻² min⁻¹.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (2 Marks)
What is the effect of adding a catalyst on the activation energy and enthalpy of reaction (ΔH)? Draw an energy profile diagram showing this effect.
Answer: Catalyst effect and energy profile diagram
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Effect: A catalyst lowers the activation energy barrier for both the forward and reverse reactions by providing an alternate reaction pathway. It has zero effect on the enthalpy of reaction ΔH (ΔH = H_products - H_reactants remains strictly unchanged). 2. Diagram: Potential energy on y-axis vs reaction coordinate on x-axis. Shows a high energy hump for uncatalyzed reaction (Ea) and a significantly lower energy hump for catalyzed reaction (Ea'), with initial reactant and final product energy levels identical in both.
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (3 Marks)
A first order reaction takes 40 minutes for 30% decomposition. Calculate its half-life period t_1/2. (log 10 = 1, log 7 = 0.8451).
Answer: Half-life t_1/2 = 77.7 minutes
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For 30% decomposition: [R]₀ = 100, [R] = 100 - 30 = 70, t = 40 min. Rate constant k = (2.303 / t) * log([R]₀ / [R]) k = (2.303 / 40) * log(100 / 70) = (2.303 / 40) * log(10 / 7) log(10/7) = log 10 - log 7 = 1.0000 - 0.8451 = 0.1549. k = (2.303 / 40) * 0.1549 = 0.3567 / 40 ≈ 8.918 × 10⁻³ min⁻¹. Half-life period: t_1/2 = 0.693 / k = 0.693 / (8.918 × 10⁻³) ≈ 77.7 minutes.
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
State the two essential criteria required for effective collisions according to collision theory.
Answer: Two criteria for effective collisions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Energy Criterion: The colliding reactant molecules must possess a minimum amount of kinetic energy, called threshold energy (E_threshold = E_ground + Ea), to overcome the activation energy barrier and break existing chemical bonds. 2. Orientation Criterion: The colliding molecules must collide with proper spatial orientation so that the reacting atoms face each other to facilitate breaking of old bonds and formation of new bonds.
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) Derive the integrated rate equation for a first order reaction: R -> Products. (b) Show that the half-life period of a first order reaction is independent of initial concentration. (c) A first order reaction is 20% complete in 10 minutes. Calculate the time taken for the reaction to go to 75% completion. (log 10 = 1, log 8 = 0.9030, log 2 = 0.3010).
Answer: First-order integrated rate derivation, half-life proof, and time calculation = 62.18 min
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Integrated Rate Equation Derivation (2 marks): - Rate = - d[R]/dt = k [R] => d[R] / [R] = - k dt. - Integrating: ln [R] = - k t + I. - At t = 0: [R] = [R]₀ => I = ln [R]₀. - ln [R] = - k t + ln [R]₀ => k t = ln([R]₀ / [R]). - Converting to common logarithm: k = (2.303 / t) * log([R]₀ / [R]). (b) Half-Life Proof (1 mark): - At t = t_1/2: [R] = [R]₀ / 2. - k = (2.303 / t_1/2) * log([R]₀ / ([R]₀/2)) = (2.303 / t_1/2) * log(2) = (2.303 * 0.3010) / t_1/2 = 0.693 / t_1/2. - t_1/2 = 0.693 / k. Because the equation contains no [R]₀ term, t_1/2 is independent of initial concentration. (c) Numerical (2 marks): - For 20% completion: [R]₀ = 100, [R] = 80, t = 10 min. k = (2.303 / 10) * log(100 / 80) = (2.303 / 10) * log(10 / 8) = 0.2303 * (1 - 0.9030) = 0.2303 * 0.0970 ≈ 0.02234 min⁻¹. - For 75% completion: [R]₀ = 100, [R] = 25. t = (2.303 / k) * log(100 / 25) = (2.303 / 0.02234) * log(4) = (2.303 / 0.02234) * 2(0.3010) = (2.303 * 0.6020) / 0.02234 = 1.3864 / 0.02234 ≈ 62.06 minutes.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) State Arrhenius equation. Express it in logarithmic form relating rate constants k1 and k2 at two different temperatures T1 and T2. (b) The rate constants of a reaction at 500 K and 700 K are 0.02 s⁻¹ and 0.07 s⁻¹ respectively. Calculate the values of Ea and A. (R = 8.314 J K⁻¹ mol⁻¹, log 3.5 = 0.5441).
Answer: Arrhenius equation, Ea = 18.23 kJ/mol, A = 1.61 s⁻¹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Arrhenius Equation Formulation (2 marks): - k = A * exp(- Ea / RT), where A is frequency factor, Ea is activation energy, R is gas constant, T is absolute temperature. - Taking log10: log k = log A - Ea / (2.303 R T). - At T1 and T2: log(k2 / k1) = [ Ea / (2.303 R) ] * [ (T2 - T1) / (T1 * T2) ]. (b) Numerical (3 marks): - T1 = 500 K, k1 = 0.02 s⁻¹; T2 = 700 K, k2 = 0.07 s⁻¹. - k2 / k1 = 0.07 / 0.02 = 3.5. log(3.5) = 0.5441. - 0.5441 = [ Ea / (2.303 * 8.314) ] * [ (700 - 500) / (500 * 700) ] 0.5441 = [ Ea / 19.147 ] * [ 200 / 350000 ] = [ Ea / 19.147 ] * (5.714 × 10⁻⁴) => Ea = (0.5441 * 19.147) / (5.714 × 10⁻⁴) ≈ 10.418 / (5.714 × 10⁻⁴) ≈ 18,232 J/mol = 18.23 kJ/mol. - Calculation of A at T1 = 500 K: log k1 = log A - Ea / (2.303 R T1) log(0.02) = - 1.6990 = log A - 18232 / (19.147 * 500) = log A - 18232 / 9573.5 = log A - 1.9044 => log A = - 1.6990 + 1.9044 = 0.2054 => A = antilog(0.2054) ≈ 1.605 s⁻¹ ≈ 1.61 s⁻¹.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) For the reaction: A + B -> Products, write the differential rate equation. (b) The following data were obtained for the reaction: 2 NO(g) + Cl2(g) -> 2 NOCl(g) at 298 K: Exp 1: [NO] = 0.15 M, [Cl2] = 0.15 M, Rate = 0.60 M/min Exp 2: [NO] = 0.15 M, [Cl2] = 0.30 M, Rate = 1.20 M/min Exp 3: [NO] = 0.30 M, [Cl2] = 0.15 M, Rate = 2.40 M/min Exp 4: [NO] = 0.25 M, [Cl2] = 0.25 M, Rate = ? Find: (i) Order with respect to NO and Cl2, (ii) Overall order of the reaction, (iii) Rate constant k with units, (iv) Rate of reaction in Experiment 4.
Answer: (i) Order in NO = 2, in Cl2 = 1; (ii) Overall = 3; (iii) k = 177.8 L² mol⁻² min⁻¹; (iv) Rate4 = 2.78 M/min
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Differential rate equation: Rate = - 1/2 d[NO]/dt = - d[Cl2]/dt = + 1/2 d[NOCl]/dt. (b) Calculations: Let Rate = k [NO]^x [Cl2]^y. (i) From Exp 1 and 2 ([NO] constant at 0.15 M): Rate2 / Rate1 = 1.20 / 0.60 = 2. [Cl2]2 / [Cl2]1 = 0.30 / 0.15 = 2. 2 = 2^y => y = 1 (First order with respect to Cl2). From Exp 1 and 3 ([Cl2] constant at 0.15 M): Rate3 / Rate1 = 2.40 / 0.60 = 4. [NO]3 / [NO]1 = 0.30 / 0.15 = 2. 4 = 2^x => 2² = 2^x => x = 2 (Second order with respect to NO). (ii) Overall order = x + y = 2 + 1 = 3 (Third order). (iii) Rate Law: Rate = k [NO]² [Cl2]. Using Exp 1: 0.60 M/min = k * (0.15 M)² * (0.15 M) = k * (0.0225) * (0.15) = k * (3.375 × 10⁻³ M³) k = 0.60 / (3.375 × 10⁻³) ≈ 177.78 L² mol⁻² min⁻¹. (iv) In Exp 4 ([NO] = 0.25 M, [Cl2] = 0.25 M): Rate4 = 177.78 * (0.25)² * (0.25) = 177.78 * (0.015625) ≈ 2.778 M/min.
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Chemical Kinetics in Food Preservation Chemical reactions responsible for food spoilage—such as lipid oxidation, microbial enzymatic reactions, and enzymatic browning—obey the fundamental laws of chemical kinetics. The rates of these reactions depend exponentially on temperature according to the Arrhenius relation k = A exp(-Ea/RT). Lowering storage temperature reduces the fraction of molecules with thermal energy equal to or greater than activation energy, drastically slowing down spoilage kinetics. Thus, refrigeration (4 °C) and deep freezing (-18 °C) extend the shelf life of perishable food items. (i) Why does food spoil much faster in summer than in winter? (ii) State the mathematical form of the Arrhenius equation and identify each parameter. (iii) If a food spoilage reaction has an activation energy of 50 kJ/mol, by what factor will its rate constant change when temperature is decreased from 25 °C (298 K) to 5 °C (278 K)? (R = 8.314 J K⁻¹ mol⁻¹). (iv) What is the effect of food preservatives (antioxidants) from a kinetic perspective?
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Food Preservation Kinetics
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Summer temperatures are higher (35-40 °C vs 15-20 °C). By Arrhenius equation, reaction rate increases exponentially with temperature because a much larger fraction of reactant molecules possess energy exceeding the activation energy threshold. (ii) k = A exp(-Ea / RT), where k = rate constant, A = pre-exponential frequency factor, Ea = activation energy, R = universal gas constant, T = absolute temperature in Kelvin. (iii) log(k_25 / k_5) = [ Ea / (2.303 R) ] * [ (298 - 278) / (298 * 278) ] = [ 50000 / (19.147) ] * [ 20 / 82844 ] = 2611.37 * (2.414 × 10⁻⁴) ≈ 0.6304. k_25 / k_5 = antilog(0.6304) ≈ 4.27. The rate constant at 5 °C is reduced by a factor of ~4.3 (spoilage reaction is more than 4 times slower). (iv) Preservatives act as inhibitors or free radical scavengers that interfere with chain propagation steps, effectively creating alternative pathways with higher activation energy barriers or blocking active enzyme sites, drastically diminishing spoilage rates.
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) What is the physical significance of activation energy? (b) The decomposition of A into products has a rate constant of 4.5 × 10³ s⁻¹ at 10 °C and energy of activation 60 kJ/mol. At what temperature will its rate constant be 1.5 × 10⁴ s⁻¹? (R = 8.314 J K⁻¹ mol⁻¹, log 3.33 = 0.5224).
Answer: Significance of activation energy and temperature calculation T2 = 297 K = 24 °C
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Physical Significance (1.5 marks): - Activation energy (Ea) represents the energy barrier that reactants must overcome to convert into products. It determines the height of the transition state potential energy peak. - Reactions with high Ea have very few molecules with sufficient energy to react, resulting in slow reaction rates. Reactions with low Ea have many molecules exceeding the barrier and proceed rapidly. (b) Numerical (3.5 marks): - T1 = 10 + 273 = 283 K, k1 = 4.5 × 10³ s⁻¹, k2 = 1.5 × 10⁴ s⁻¹, Ea = 60,000 J/mol. - k2 / k1 = (1.5 × 10⁴) / (4.5 × 10³) = 15 / 4.5 = 3.333. log(3.333) = 0.5228. - log(k2 / k1) = [ Ea / (2.303 R) ] * [ (T2 - T1) / (T1 * T2) ] - 0.5228 = [ 60000 / (2.303 * 8.314) ] * [ (T2 - 283) / (283 T2) ] 0.5228 = [ 60000 / 19.147 ] * [ (T2 - 283) / (283 T2) ] = 3133.65 * [ (T2 - 283) / (283 T2) ] - (T2 - 283) / (283 T2) = 0.5228 / 3133.65 ≈ 1.668 × 10⁻⁴ - T2 - 283 = (1.668 × 10⁻⁴) * 283 T2 = 0.0472 T2 - T2 - 0.0472 T2 = 283 => 0.9528 T2 = 283 - T2 = 283 / 0.9528 ≈ 297.0 K. - In Celsius: T2 = 297 - 273 = 24 °C.
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Explain pseudo-first-order reaction with a suitable chemical reaction. (b) A first order reaction takes 30 minutes for 50% completion. Calculate the time required for 90% completion of this reaction. (log 10 = 1, log 2 = 0.3010).
Answer: Pseudo-first-order explanation and time calculation t = 99.7 min
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Pseudo-First-Order Reaction (2 marks): - A reaction which is bimolecular (molecularity = 2) but obeys first-order kinetics because one of the reactants is present in large excess. - Example: Inversion of cane sugar (sucrose): C12H22O11 + H2O -(H⁺)-> C6H12O6(glucose) + C6H12O6(fructose). - Because water is the solvent, its concentration (~55.5 M) remains virtually unchanged throughout the reaction. Rate = k [C12H22O11] [H2O] = k' [C12H22O11], where k' = k [H2O]. (b) Numerical (3 marks): - t_1/2 = 30 min => k = 0.693 / 30 = 0.0231 min⁻¹. - For 90% completion: [R]₀ = 100, [R] = 100 - 90 = 10. - t = (2.303 / k) * log([R]₀ / [R]) = (2.303 / 0.0231) * log(100 / 10) = (2.303 / 0.0231) * log(10) = 2.303 / 0.0231 ≈ 99.7 minutes.
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
For a chemical reaction: R -> P, the variation in the concentration ln [R] vs time t plot is given as a straight line with slope -0.05 s⁻¹ and intercept 0.693. (i) What is the order of the reaction? (ii) What is the unit of rate constant? (iii) What is the half-life of the reaction?
Answer: (i) First order, (ii) s⁻¹, (iii) t_1/2 = 13.86 s
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) For a first order reaction, integrated equation is: ln [R] = - k t + ln [R]₀. Plotting ln [R] against t gives a straight line. Hence, the reaction is of First Order. (ii) For a first order reaction, the unit of rate constant k is s⁻¹ (or time⁻¹). (iii) From equation, slope = - k => - k = - 0.05 s⁻¹ => k = 0.05 s⁻¹. Half-life period: t_1/2 = 0.693 / k = 0.693 / 0.05 = 13.86 seconds.
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) What is the difference between average rate and instantaneous rate of a reaction? How are they determined graphically? (b) The decomposition of NH3 on platinum surface is zero order reaction. If k = 2.5 × 10⁻⁴ mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹, what are the rates of production of N2 and H2?
Answer: Rate distinctions, graphical determination, and rates of production
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Distinctions & Graphical Determination (2.5 marks): 1. Average Rate: The change in concentration of a reactant or product divided by the finite time interval Δt taken: r_avg = - Δ[R] / Δt = + Δ[P] / Δt. Determined graphically by finding the slope of the chord (secant line) connecting two points at t1 and t2 on the concentration vs time curve. 2. Instantaneous Rate: The rate of reaction at a specific instant of time: r_inst = lim(Δt->0) - Δ[R]/Δt = - d[R]/dt. Determined graphically by drawing a tangent to the concentration vs time curve at that specific time t and finding the negative slope of that tangent line. (b) Production Rates (2.5 marks): Reaction: 2 NH3 -(Pt)-> N2 + 3 H2. Rate of reaction = - 1/2 d[NH3]/dt = d[N2]/dt = 1/3 d[H2]/dt. For zero-order reaction: Rate of reaction = k = 2.5 × 10⁻⁴ mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹. 1. Rate of production of N2: d[N2]/dt = Rate = 2.5 × 10⁻⁴ mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹. 2. Rate of production of H2: d[H2]/dt = 3 * Rate = 3 * (2.5 × 10⁻⁴) = 7.5 × 10⁻⁴ mol L⁻¹ s⁻¹.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
Show that in a first order reaction, time required for completion of 99.9% is 10 times that required for completion of 50% of the reaction.
Answer: Proof that t_99.9% = 10 * t_50%
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
For first order reaction: t = (2.303 / k) * log([R]₀ / [R]). 1. For 50% completion (half-life): [R] = [R]₀ / 2 => t_50% = (2.303 / k) * log(2) = (2.303 * 0.3010) / k = 0.693 / k ... (1) 2. For 99.9% completion: [R] = [R]₀ - 0.999 [R]₀ = 0.001 [R]₀ = 10⁻³ [R]₀. t_99.9% = (2.303 / k) * log([R]₀ / (10⁻³ [R]₀)) = (2.303 / k) * log(10³) = (2.303 * 3) / k = 6.909 / k ... (2) 3. Ratio of (2) to (1): t_99.9% / t_50% = (6.909 / k) / (0.693 / k) = 6.909 / 0.693 ≈ 9.969 ≈ 10. Therefore: t_99.9% = 10 * t_50%. (Proven).
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) State the postulates of collision theory of chemical reactions. (b) Why do all molecular collisions not lead to product formation? (c) The activation energy for the reaction 2 HI(g) -> H2(g) + I2(g) is 209.5 kJ/mol at 581 K. Calculate the fraction of molecules having energy equal to or greater than activation energy.
Answer: Collision theory postulates, collision barriers, and fraction calculation = 1.47 × 10⁻¹⁹
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Postulates of Collision Theory (2 marks): 1. Reactant molecules are assumed to be hard, impenetrable spheres, and chemical reaction occurs only when these spheres collide with each other. 2. The number of collisions taking place per second per unit volume of reaction mixture is called collision frequency (Z). 3. Collisions are effective only if colliding molecules possess a minimum threshold energy and collide with proper spatial orientation. (b) Why all collisions do not result in reaction (1.5 marks): - Energy Barrier: Most molecules at room temperature have kinetic energy much lower than the activation energy (E < Ea). Collisions between sub-threshold molecules are completely elastic and bounce apart without reaction. - Orientation Barrier: Even energetic collisions fail to react if molecules collide with improper orientation (atoms that need to bond do not contact each other). (c) Calculation of Fraction of Molecules (1.5 marks): - Fraction x = exp(- Ea / RT) => log x = - Ea / (2.303 R T). - Given: Ea = 209.5 kJ/mol = 209,500 J/mol, T = 581 K, R = 8.314 J K⁻¹ mol⁻¹. - log x = - 209500 / (2.303 * 8.314 * 581) = - 209500 / (11124.7) = - 18.832. - x = antilog(-18.832) = antilog(-19 + 0.168) = 10^(0.168) × 10⁻¹⁹ ≈ 1.47 × 10⁻¹⁹. (Only about 1 in every 10¹⁹ collisions has sufficient energy!).
Inorganic Chemistry
Chapter 4: d- and f-Block Elements
7 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following 3d transition metal ions is diamagnetic in nature?
(a) Sc³⁺
(b) Ti³⁺
(c) V³⁺
(d) Cr³⁺
Answer: (a) Sc³⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Sc (Z = 21) has ground state configuration [Ar] 3d¹ 4s². Upon losing 3 electrons to form Sc³⁺, its configuration becomes [Ar] 3d⁰. With zero unpaired electrons (n = 0), Sc³⁺ is completely diamagnetic.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The spin-only magnetic moment of Fe²⁺ ion (Z = 26) is approximately:
(a) 4.90 BM
(b) 5.92 BM
(c) 3.87 BM
(d) 1.73 BM
Answer: (a) 4.90 BM
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Fe²⁺ (Z = 26): Configuration is [Ar] 3d⁶. Number of unpaired electrons n = 4. Spin-only magnetic moment μ = √(n(n+2)) = √(4(4+2)) = √24 ≈ 4.90 BM.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Zirconium (Zr) and Hafnium (Hf) exhibit almost identical atomic and ionic radii because of:
(a) Lanthanoid contraction
(b) Actinoid contraction
(c) Diagonal relationship
(d) Belonging to the same group
Answer: (a) Lanthanoid contraction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The filling of 4f orbitals before 5d series in Hf results in poor shielding by 4f electrons. The gradual increase in effective nuclear charge causes a steady contraction in size (lanthanoid contraction), making the atomic radius of 5d Hf (159 pm) almost identical to 4d Zr (160 pm).
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which 3d transition element exhibits the maximum number of oxidation states?
(a) Mn
(b) Cr
(c) Fe
(d) Ti
Answer: (a) Mn
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Manganese (Mn, Z = 25) has electronic configuration [Ar] 3d⁵ 4s². Because both 3d and 4s electrons have comparable energies, all 7 electrons can participate in bonding, giving oxidation states from +2 to +7.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which of the following transition metal ions is colourless in aqueous solution?
(a) Cu⁺ and Sc³⁺
(b) Cu²⁺ and Fe²⁺
(c) Ni²⁺ and Co²⁺
(d) Cr³⁺ and Mn²⁺
Answer: (a) Cu⁺ and Sc³⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cu⁺ has [Ar] 3d¹⁰ (completely filled d orbitals) and Sc³⁺ has [Ar] 3d⁰ (empty d orbitals). In the absence of unpaired d electrons, d-d electronic transitions are impossible, making both ions colourless.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The standard reduction potential E°(Cu²⁺/Cu) is positive (+0.34 V) mainly because:
(a) high enthalpy of atomization and ionization enthalpy are not balanced by hydration enthalpy
(b) low enthalpy of sublimation
(c) high negative hydration enthalpy
(d) high electron gain enthalpy
Answer: (a) high enthalpy of atomization and ionization enthalpy are not balanced by hydration enthalpy
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The transformation Cu(s) -> Cu²⁺(aq) requires high sublimation/atomization energy and very high sum of first and second ionization enthalpies (ΔiH1 + ΔiH2). The hydration enthalpy of Cu²⁺ is not sufficiently negative to compensate for these large energy inputs, resulting in a positive E°.
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
In acidic medium, orange dichromate ion (Cr2O7²⁻) oxidizes Fe²⁺ to Fe³⁺. The reduced form of chromium is:
(a) Cr³⁺ (green)
(b) Cr²⁺ (blue)
(c) CrO4²⁻ (yellow)
(d) CrO2 (black)
Answer: (a) Cr³⁺ (green)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Reaction: Cr2O7²⁻ + 14 H⁺ + 6 Fe²⁺ -> 2 Cr³⁺ (green) + 6 Fe³⁺ + 7 H2O. Chromium is reduced from +6 oxidation state to +3 oxidation state.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Ce⁴⁺ is a well-known oxidizing agent because:
(a) Ce³⁺ is the most stable oxidation state of cerium
(b) Ce⁴⁺ has noble gas configuration
(c) Ce has very low electronegativity
(d) Ce⁴⁺ is easily oxidized
Answer: (a) Ce³⁺ is the most stable oxidation state of cerium
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Although Ce⁴⁺ possesses a stable noble gas configuration ([Xe] 4f⁰), +3 is the characteristic and thermodynamically most stable oxidation state of lanthanoids. Ce⁴⁺ readily gains an electron (E° = +1.74 V) to convert to Ce³⁺, making it a strong oxidizing agent.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
Which of the following oxides of manganese is amphoteric in nature?
(a) Mn2O7
(b) MnO
(c) Mn2O3
(d) MnO2
Answer: (d) MnO2
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
MnO is purely basic (+2 state), Mn2O3 is weakly basic (+3 state), MnO2 (+4 state) is amphoteric (reacts with both acids and strong bases), and Mn2O7 (+7 state) is strongly acidic covalent green oil.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Transition metals and their compounds exhibit excellent catalytic activity primarily because of their:
(a) variable oxidation states and ability to form coordination complexes
(b) diamagnetic nature
(c) high melting and boiling points
(d) high electrical conductivity
Answer: (a) variable oxidation states and ability to form coordination complexes
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Transition metals can adopt multiple oxidation states to participate in redox steps and provide large surface areas with vacant d orbitals to form unstable intermediate complexes with reactants, lowering activation energy.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
The basicity of lanthanoid hydroxides Ln(OH)3 across the series from La(OH)3 to Lu(OH)3:
(a) decreases
(b) increases
(c) remains constant
(d) first decreases then increases
Answer: (a) decreases
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Due to lanthanoid contraction, the ionic radius of Ln³⁺ ions decreases from La³⁺ (103 pm) to Lu³⁺ (86 pm). According to Fajan's rules, smaller cation size increases the covalent character of the Ln-OH bond, decreasing OH⁻ release tendency. Thus, La(OH)3 is most basic and Lu(OH)3 is least basic.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
When yellow chromate solution is acidified with dilute sulfuric acid, it turns orange due to the formation of:
(a) Cr2O7²⁻
(b) Cr³⁺
(c) CrO3
(d) [Cr(H2O)6]³⁺
Answer: (a) Cr2O7²⁻
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In aqueous solution, chromate and dichromate exist in pH-dependent equilibrium: 2 CrO4²⁻ (yellow) + 2 H⁺ ⇌ Cr2O7²⁻ (orange) + H2O. Adding acid shifts equilibrium to the right, forming orange dichromate.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Interstitial compounds formed by transition metals are characterized by:
(a) very high melting points and retention of metallic conductivity
(b) ionic bonding and softness
(c) very high chemical reactivity
(d) low hardness
Answer: (a) very high melting points and retention of metallic conductivity
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Interstitial compounds are formed when small non-metal atoms (H, B, C, N) are trapped inside interstitial voids of transition metal lattices. They are extremely hard, chemically inert, possess melting points higher than pure metals, and retain metallic conductivity.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Why is Cr²⁺ a reducing agent while Mn³⁺ is an oxidizing agent, although both have d⁴ electronic configuration?
(a) Cr³⁺ has half-filled t2g³ level, whereas Mn²⁺ has half-filled 3d⁵ configuration
(b) Cr is a non-metal
(c) Mn has higher ionization energy
(d) Cr has higher electronegativity
Answer: (a) Cr³⁺ has half-filled t2g³ level, whereas Mn²⁺ has half-filled 3d⁵ configuration
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cr²⁺ (d⁴) loses 1 electron to form Cr³⁺ (d³), which has an exceptionally stable half-filled t2g³ subshell in octahedral crystal field, making Cr²⁺ a strong reducing agent. Mn³⁺ (d⁴) gains 1 electron to form Mn²⁺ (d⁵), which has a half-filled 3d⁵ configuration, making Mn³⁺ a strong oxidizing agent.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Actinoids show a greater number of oxidation states than lanthanoids because:
(a) 5f, 6d, and 7s energy levels are comparable in energy
(b) 4f orbitals are closer to nucleus
(c) actinoids are all radioactive
(d) actinoid contraction is smaller
Answer: (a) 5f, 6d, and 7s energy levels are comparable in energy
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In actinoids, the energy difference between 5f, 6d, and 7s subshells is extremely small, allowing electrons from all three subshells to participate in chemical bonding, producing variable oxidation states up to +7 (e.g. Np, Pu).
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Which of the following transition elements has the highest enthalpy of atomization in the 3d series?
(a) V
(b) Cr
(c) Fe
(d) Zn
Answer: (b) Cr
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Enthalpy of atomization depends directly on the strength of metallic bonding, which is governed by the number of unpaired d-electrons participating in interatomic bonding. Chromium ([Ar] 3d⁵ 4s¹) has 6 unpaired valence electrons, giving it the highest enthalpy of atomization in the 3d series.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
In potassium permanganate (KMnO4), the oxidation state of Mn is +7. The deep purple colour of KMnO4 is due to:
(a) Charge transfer from oxygen to manganese (L -> M charge transfer)
(b) d-d transition of electrons
(c) f-f transition
(d) polarization of potassium ion
Answer: (a) Charge transfer from oxygen to manganese (L -> M charge transfer)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Mn in KMnO4 is in +7 state with [Ar] 3d⁰ configuration (no d-electrons), so d-d transitions are impossible. The intense purple color arises from Ligand-to-Metal Charge Transfer (LMCT), where an electron is momentarily transferred from the 2p orbital of oxide ligand (O²⁻) into an empty 3d orbital of Mn(VII).
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Mischmetal is an alloy consisting predominantly of:
(a) Lanthanoid metals (~95%) and iron (~5%)
(b) Actinoids (~95%) and copper (~5%)
(c) Chromium and nickel
(d) Titanium and aluminum
Answer: (a) Lanthanoid metals (~95%) and iron (~5%)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Mischmetal is a well-known pyrophoric alloy containing about 95% lanthanoid metal (chiefly cerium ~50%, lanthanum and neodymium) and ~5% iron, along with traces of S, C, Ca, and Al. Used in cigarette lighter flints and tracer bullets.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Which of the following compounds has the lowest oxidation state of the metal?
(a) [Ni(CO)4]
(b) K2Cr2O7
(c) KMnO4
(d) FeSO4
Answer: (a) [Ni(CO)4]
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In tetracarbonylnickel(0) [Ni(CO)4], CO is a neutral ligand, so the oxidation state of Ni is 0. In FeSO4 it is +2, in K2Cr2O7 it is +6, and in KMnO4 it is +7.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Europium (Eu, Z = 63) and Ytterbium (Yb, Z = 70) show stable +2 oxidation states because of:
(a) half-filled 4f⁷ and completely filled 4f¹⁴ configurations
(b) completely empty 4f⁰ configuration
(c) noble gas configuration
(d) inert pair effect
Answer: (a) half-filled 4f⁷ and completely filled 4f¹⁴ configurations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Eu²⁺ has electronic configuration [Xe] 4f⁷ (half-filled f-subshell) and Yb²⁺ has [Xe] 4f¹⁴ (completely filled f-subshell). Extra exchange energy confers special thermodynamic stability to these configurations.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
What is the equivalent weight of KMnO4 in acidic medium? (Molecular weight = M):
(a) M / 5
(b) M / 3
(c) M / 1
(d) M / 6
Answer: (a) M / 5
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In acidic medium: MnO4⁻ + 8 H⁺ + 5 e⁻ -> Mn²⁺ + 4 H2O. The change in oxidation state of Mn is from +7 to +2 (n-factor = 5). Equivalent weight = Molar mass / n-factor = M / 5 = 158 / 5 = 31.6 g/eq.
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
Zinc (Zn), Cadmium (Cd), and Mercury (Hg) are NOT regarded as typical transition elements because:
(a) they have completely filled d-orbitals in their ground state as well as common oxidation states
(b) they are soft metals
(c) they have low melting points
(d) they do not form coordination compounds
Answer: (a) they have completely filled d-orbitals in their ground state as well as common oxidation states
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
By definition, a transition element is an element having incompletely filled (partially filled) d-subshell in its atomic state or common oxidation state. Group 12 elements (Zn, Cd, Hg) have (n-1)d¹⁰ ns² ground state and (n-1)d¹⁰ in +2 state, so they do not exhibit typical transition characteristics.
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Pyrolusite ore (MnO2) on fusion with KOH in presence of atmospheric oxygen or KNO3 produces:
(a) K2MnO4 (dark green)
(b) KMnO4 (purple)
(c) Mn2O3 (brown)
(d) MnSO4 (pink)
Answer: (a) K2MnO4 (dark green)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Reaction: 2 MnO2 + 4 KOH + O2 -> 2 K2MnO4 (potassium manganate, dark green) + 2 H2O. Potassium manganate is then disproportionated or electrolytically oxidized to KMnO4.
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which transition metal ion in 3d series has magnetic moment μ = 1.73 BM?
(a) Ti³⁺
(b) Fe³⁺
(c) Co²⁺
(d) Ni²⁺
Answer: (a) Ti³⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
μ = √(n(n+2)) = 1.73 BM corresponds to n = 1 unpaired electron (√(1(3)) = √3 ≈ 1.73). Ti³⁺ (Z = 22) has configuration [Ar] 3d¹, which has exactly 1 unpaired electron.
Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The highest oxidation state of manganese is exhibited in its oxide (Mn2O7) rather than in its fluoride (MnF4) because:
(a) oxygen has the ability to form multiple pπ-dπ bonds with manganese
(b) fluorine is less electronegative than oxygen
(c) oxygen has smaller size than fluorine
(d) fluorine cannot act as oxidizing agent
Answer: (a) oxygen has the ability to form multiple pπ-dπ bonds with manganese
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Oxygen can stabilize high oxidation states via multiple bonding (pπ-dπ overlap), allowing Mn to reach +7 in Mn2O7 (Mn=O bonds). Fluorine can only form single covalent bonds, sterically limiting manganese to MnF4.
