Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
Give chemical reasons for the following: (i) Transition metals show variable oxidation states. (ii) Sc³⁺ is colourless while Ti³⁺ is purple in aqueous solution.
Answer: Reasons for variable oxidation states and ion colour
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Variable Oxidation States: In transition metals, the energy levels of (n-1)d and ns orbitals are very close. Therefore, both ns electrons and unpaired (n-1)d electrons can be readily lost or shared in chemical bond formation. (ii) Colour Comparison: Sc³⁺ (Z = 21) has configuration [Ar] 3d⁰ (empty d-orbitals, no d-d transition possible, colourless). Ti³⁺ (Z = 22) has configuration [Ar] 3d¹ (one unpaired electron undergoes d-d transition by absorbing yellow-green light and transmitting purple).
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Describe the preparation of potassium dichromate (K2Cr2O7) from chromite ore (FeCr2O4) giving balanced chemical equations for all steps.
Answer: Preparation of K2Cr2O7 from chromite ore
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Step 1: Conversion of chromite ore to sodium chromate: 4 FeCr2O4 + 8 Na2CO3 + 7 O2 -> 8 Na2CrO4 (yellow solution) + 2 Fe2O3 (insoluble residue) + 8 CO2. Step 2: Conversion of sodium chromate to sodium dichromate: 2 Na2CrO4 + H2SO4 (conc.) -> Na2Cr2O7 (orange) + Na2SO4 + H2O. Step 3: Conversion of sodium dichromate to potassium dichromate: Na2Cr2O7 + 2 KCl -> K2Cr2O7 (orange crystals) + 2 NaCl. (K2Cr2O7 is less soluble in cold water than NaCl and crystallizes out readily).
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
What is lanthanoid contraction? State two important consequences of lanthanoid contraction.
Answer: Definition and consequences of lanthanoid contraction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Definition: The steady and gradual decrease in the atomic and ionic radii of lanthanoid elements with increasing atomic number from Lanthanum (57) to Lutetium (71) due to poor shielding of 4f electrons. 2. Consequences: (a) Similarity in radii of 4d and 5d elements: Pairs like Zr/Hf and Nb/Ta have nearly identical radii and identical chemical properties, making separation difficult. (b) Decrease in basic strength of hydroxides: Basicity decreases progressively from La(OH)3 (most basic) to Lu(OH)3 (least basic) as covalent character increases.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (3 Marks)
Explain the following observations: (a) Transition metals exhibit high enthalpy of atomization. (b) Cr²⁺ is reducing while Mn³⁺ is oxidizing though both have d⁴ configuration. (c) The highest oxidation state of a metal is exhibited in its oxide or fluoride.
Answer: Explanations for atomization enthalpy, redox behavior, and high oxidation states
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) High Enthalpy of Atomization: Transition metals have many unpaired electrons in their (n-1)d orbitals that participate in extensive interatomic covalent bonding in addition to metallic bonding. (b) Redox Nature: Cr²⁺ (3d⁴) loses an electron to form Cr³⁺ (3d³), having half-filled stable t2g³ level in octahedral crystal field. Mn³⁺ (3d⁴) gains an electron to form Mn²⁺ (3d⁵), achieving stable half-filled 3d⁵ subshell. (c) Fluorides/Oxides: Small size and exceptionally high electronegativities of fluorine and oxygen enable them to oxidize transition metals to their highest oxidation states; oxygen additionally forms strong pπ-dπ multiple bonds.
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (2 Marks)
Complete and balance the following chemical equations: (i) MnO4⁻ + C2O4²⁻ + H⁺ -> (ii) Cr2O7²⁻ + Fe²⁺ + H⁺ ->
Answer: Balanced redox equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) 2 MnO4⁻ + 5 C2O4²⁻ + 16 H⁺ -> 2 Mn²⁺ + 10 CO2 + 8 H2O (Permanganate oxidizes oxalate ion to carbon dioxide gas while Mn(VII) is reduced to Mn²⁺). (ii) Cr2O7²⁻ + 6 Fe²⁺ + 14 H⁺ -> 2 Cr³⁺ + 6 Fe³⁺ + 7 H2O (Dichromate oxidizes ferrous ion to ferric ion while Cr(VI) is reduced to Cr³⁺).
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (3 Marks)
Calculate the spin-only magnetic moments of the following ions in aqueous solution: (i) Mn²⁺, (ii) Cr³⁺, (iii) Ni²⁺. (Atomic numbers: Mn = 25, Cr = 24, Ni = 28).
Answer: (i) 5.92 BM, (ii) 3.87 BM, (iii) 2.83 BM
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Formula: μ = √(n(n+2)) BM, where n is number of unpaired electrons. (i) Mn²⁺ (Z = 25): [Ar] 3d⁵ => n = 5 unpaired electrons. μ = √(5(5+2)) = √35 ≈ 5.92 BM. (ii) Cr³⁺ (Z = 24): [Ar] 3d³ => n = 3 unpaired electrons. μ = √(3(3+2)) = √15 ≈ 3.87 BM. (iii) Ni²⁺ (Z = 28): [Ar] 3d⁸ => n = 2 unpaired electrons. μ = √(2(2+2)) = √8 ≈ 2.83 BM.
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (2 Marks)
Account for the following: (i) E° value for Mn³⁺/Mn²⁺ couple is much more positive (+1.57 V) than for Cr³⁺/Cr²⁺ (-0.41 V). (ii) Zn is not regarded as a transition element.
Answer: Explanations for E°(Mn³⁺/Mn²⁺) and non-transition nature of Zn
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Mn²⁺ has an exceptionally stable half-filled 3d⁵ configuration, making the reduction of Mn³⁺ (3d⁴) to Mn²⁺ highly energetically favorable (high positive E°). For Cr, Cr³⁺ has half-filled stable t2g³ level, so Cr³⁺ resists reduction, giving a negative E°. (ii) Transition metals are defined as elements having partially filled d-orbitals in ground or common ionic states. Zn ([Ar] 3d¹⁰ 4s²) and Zn²⁺ ([Ar] 3d¹⁰) have completely filled d-orbitals in both elemental and +2 state, so Zn is not a transition element.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (3 Marks)
Compare lanthanoids and actinoids with respect to: (i) Oxidation states, (ii) Chemical reactivity, (iii) Magnetic properties.
Answer: Comparison between Lanthanoids and Actinoids
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Oxidation States: Lanthanoids exhibit mainly +3, with occasional +2 and +4 due to large energy gap between 4f and 5d. Actinoids show a wide range of oxidation states (+3, +4, +5, +6, +7) because 5f, 6d, and 7s are comparable in energy. 2. Chemical Reactivity: Actinoids are significantly more reactive than lanthanoids due to lower ionization enthalpies; actinoids react readily with boiling water, acids, and halogens. 3. Magnetic Properties: Both show paramagnetism, but the magnetic behavior of actinoids is far more complex than that of lanthanoids due to stronger spin-orbit coupling and interaction with crystal field.
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (2 Marks)
How would you account for the following: (i) Transition metals and their compounds act as good catalysts. (ii) The d¹ configuration is very unstable in transition metals.
Answer: Catalytic activity and instability of d¹ configuration
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Catalytic Activity: Transition metals have variable oxidation states allowing them to form intermediate compounds, vacant d-orbitals to coordinate with reactant molecules, and large surface areas for chemisorption. (ii) Instability of d¹: Ions with d¹ configuration (like Ti³⁺) readily lose the single d-electron to achieve the noble gas configuration ([Ar] 3d⁰) or disproportionate, making d¹ species strong reducing agents.
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
What happens when: (i) Potassium dichromate is treated with an alkali? (ii) Potassium permanganate is heated strongly?
Answer: Reactions with alkali and thermal decomposition
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Reaction with Alkali: Orange dichromate turns into yellow chromate: Cr2O7²⁻ (orange) + 2 OH⁻ -> 2 CrO4²⁻ (yellow) + H2O. (ii) Heating KMnO4: Potassium permanganate undergoes thermal decomposition at 513 K to give potassium manganate, manganese dioxide, and oxygen gas: 2 KMnO4 -(heat)-> K2MnO4 (dark green) + MnO2 (black) + O2(g).
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) Describe the industrial preparation of potassium permanganate (KMnO4) from pyrolusite ore (MnO2). (b) Write balanced chemical equations for the reaction of acidified KMnO4 with: (i) Oxalic acid (H2C2O4) (ii) Iron(II) sulfate (FeSO4) (iii) Potassium iodide (KI).
Answer: Industrial preparation of KMnO4 and balanced redox reactions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Preparation of KMnO4 (2 marks): - Step 1: Conversion of MnO2 to Potassium Manganate: Finely powdered pyrolusite (MnO2) is fused with KOH in presence of air (O2) or KNO3: 2 MnO2 + 4 KOH + O2 -> 2 K2MnO4 (dark green) + 2 H2O. - Step 2: Oxidation of Manganate to Permanganate: The green mass is dissolved in water and oxidized electrolytically (or by bubbling CO2/chlorine): At Anode: MnO4²⁻ (green) -> MnO4⁻ (purple) + e⁻. (Chemical route: 3 MnO4²⁻ + 4 H⁺ -> 2 MnO4⁻ + MnO2 + 2 H2O). (b) Balanced Redox Equations (3 marks): (i) With oxalic acid: 2 MnO4⁻ + 5 C2O4²⁻ + 16 H⁺ -> 2 Mn²⁺ + 10 CO2 + 8 H2O. (ii) With iron(II) sulfate: MnO4⁻ + 5 Fe²⁺ + 8 H⁺ -> Mn²⁺ + 5 Fe³⁺ + 4 H2O. (iii) With potassium iodide: 2 MnO4⁻ + 10 I⁻ + 16 H⁺ -> 2 Mn²⁺ + 5 I2 + 8 H2O.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
Account for each of the following observations: (i) E°(M²⁺/M) value for copper is positive (+0.34 V). (ii) Transition metals have high enthalpies of atomization. (iii) Transition metals form interstitial compounds. (iv) Cr²⁺ is a strong reducing agent while Mn³⁺ is a strong oxidizing agent. (v) The basic strength of Ln(OH)3 decreases across the lanthanoid series.
Answer: Detailed explanations for 5 core properties of d- and f-block elements
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme (1 mark each): (i) Positive E°(Cu²⁺/Cu): The sum of enthalpy of sublimation (atomization) and ionization enthalpies (ΔiH1 + ΔiH2) for copper is exceptionally high and is not compensated by its hydration enthalpy. (ii) High Enthalpy of Atomization: Due to the presence of large numbers of unpaired electrons in (n-1)d orbitals, extensive interatomic covalent bonding occurs in addition to metallic bonding. (iii) Interstitial Compounds: The crystal lattices of transition metals have interstitial spaces (voids) where small non-metal atoms (H, B, C, N) get trapped without distorting lattice symmetry. (iv) Cr²⁺ vs Mn³⁺: Oxidation of Cr²⁺ (d⁴) yields Cr³⁺ (d³), which possesses an exceptionally stable half-filled t2g³ level in octahedral crystal field. Mn³⁺ (d⁴) readily accepts an electron to form Mn²⁺ (d⁵), gaining high exchange stability of half-filled d-subshell. (v) Basicity of Ln(OH)3: Due to lanthanoid contraction, the ionic radii of Ln³⁺ decrease from La³⁺ to Lu³⁺. By Fajan's rules, the polarizability increases and covalent character of Ln-OH bond increases, decreasing OH⁻ dissociation.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) What is the effect of increasing pH on a solution of potassium dichromate? (b) Write the ionic equations for the reaction of acidified K2Cr2O7 with: (i) H2S (ii) Sn²⁺ (iii) SO2. (c) Why do Zr and Hf exhibit very similar physical and chemical properties?
Answer: Effect of pH, redox reactions of dichromate, and lanthanoid contraction effect on Zr/Hf
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Effect of pH (1 mark): Increasing pH (adding base) converts orange dichromate into yellow chromate: Cr2O7²⁻ (orange) + 2 OH⁻ ⇌ 2 CrO4²⁻ (yellow) + H2O. (b) Redox Reactions (3 marks): (i) With H2S (sulfur is precipitated): Cr2O7²⁻ + 8 H⁺ + 3 H2S -> 2 Cr³⁺ + 3 S (precipitate) + 7 H2O. (ii) With Sn²⁺ (tin(II) to tin(IV)): Cr2O7²⁻ + 14 H⁺ + 3 Sn²⁺ -> 2 Cr³⁺ + 3 Sn⁴⁺ + 7 H2O. (iii) With SO2 (sulfur dioxide oxidized to sulfate): Cr2O7²⁻ + 2 H⁺ + 3 SO2 -> 2 Cr³⁺ + 3 SO4²⁻ + H2O. (c) Similarity of Zr and Hf (1 mark): Due to Lanthanoid Contraction (poor shielding by 14 intervened 4f electrons in Hf), Hf has an atomic radius (159 pm) virtually identical to that of Zr (160 pm), resulting in nearly identical chemical properties.
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Coordination, Magnetism, and Catalysis in Transition Metals The d-block elements occupy the middle of the periodic table between s- and p-blocks. The valence electrons enter (n-1)d orbitals, which are shielded by ns electrons. This configuration imparts distinctive physical and chemical properties such as variable valency, catalytic efficiency, high melting points, and coloured paramagnetic ions. Magnetic measurements provide crucial information about the oxidation state and coordination geometry of metal complexes. The spin-only magnetic moment is given by μ = √(n(n+2)) BM. (i) Why do transition metals exhibit variable oxidation states? (ii) Calculate the spin-only magnetic moment of V³⁺ (Z = 23). (iii) Why are Zn²⁺ salts white while Cu²⁺ salts are blue in aqueous solution? (iv) Name a transition metal catalyst used in Haber's process for the synthesis of ammonia.
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Transition Metal Chemistry
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) The energy levels of (n-1)d and ns orbitals are very close; hence both ns and unpaired (n-1)d electrons can be utilized for bonding. (ii) V (Z = 23): [Ar] 3d³ 4s². For V³⁺: [Ar] 3d² => n = 2 unpaired electrons. μ = √(2(2+2)) = √8 ≈ 2.83 BM. (iii) Zn²⁺ has [Ar] 3d¹⁰ configuration (completely filled d-subshell, no unpaired electrons), so d-d transitions cannot occur, making its salts white/colourless. Cu²⁺ has [Ar] 3d⁹ configuration (one unpaired electron), which undergoes d-d absorption in the red-orange region, transmitting blue light. (iv) Finely divided Iron (Fe) with Molybdenum (Mo) as promoter (or K2O/Al2O3).
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) Compare the chemistry of Lanthanoids and Actinoids with special reference to: (i) Electronic configuration (ii) Atomic and ionic sizes (iii) Oxidation states (iv) Chemical reactivity. (b) Why is Ce⁴⁺ used as an analytical reagent in volumetric titrations?
Answer: Lanthanoid vs Actinoid comparison and Ce⁴⁺ analytical use
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Detailed Comparison (4 marks): 1. Electronic Configuration: Lanthanoids involve filling of 4f orbitals ([Xe] 4f¹⁻¹⁴ 5d⁰⁻¹ 6s²). Actinoids involve filling of 5f orbitals ([Rn] 5f¹⁻¹⁴ 6d⁰⁻¹ 7s²). 2. Atomic/Ionic Sizes: Both exhibit contraction across the series (Lanthanoid vs Actinoid contraction). Actinoid contraction is greater due to poorer shielding by 5f electrons compared to 4f. 3. Oxidation States: Lanthanoids show primarily +3 state (with limited +2, +4). Actinoids show a wide range of oxidation states (+3, +4, +5, +6, +7) because 5f, 6d, and 7s levels are close in energy. 4. Chemical Reactivity: Actinoids are more reactive than lanthanoids due to lower ionization energies and greater electropositive character; all actinoids are radioactive. (b) Ce⁴⁺ as Analytical Reagent (1 mark): Ce⁴⁺ is a strong oxidizing agent (E°(Ce⁴⁺/Ce³⁺) = +1.74 V) in acidic solution. It quantitatively oxidizes Fe²⁺, oxalate, etc., Ce⁴⁺ + e⁻ -> Ce³⁺, making it an excellent primary standard in cerimetric titrations.
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Write the electronic configuration of Cr³⁺ and Cu⁺ (Z = 24 and 29). (b) Why are transition metal fluorides and oxides generally more ionic in low oxidation states and covalent in high oxidation states? (c) How is potassium manganate (K2MnO4) converted into potassium permanganate (KMnO4)?
Answer: Electronic configurations, ionic vs covalent nature, and manganate conversion
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Electronic Configurations (1.5 marks): - Cr (Z = 24): [Ar] 3d⁵ 4s¹ => Cr³⁺: [Ar] 3d³. - Cu (Z = 29): [Ar] 3d¹⁰ 4s¹ => Cu⁺: [Ar] 3d¹⁰. (b) Ionic vs Covalent Nature (1.5 marks): - In lower oxidation states (+2, +3), the metal ion has a larger radius and lower positive charge density, resulting in low polarizing power and primarily ionic bonding. - In higher oxidation states (+5, +6, +7), the metal ion has very high charge density and small radius, leading to immense polarizing power according to Fajan's rules, resulting in electron cloud distortion and strong covalent character (e.g. Mn2O7, CrO3). (c) Conversion of K2MnO4 to KMnO4 (2 marks): 1. Disproportionation in Acidic/Neutral Solution: 3 MnO4²⁻ (green) + 4 H⁺ -> 2 MnO4⁻ (purple) + MnO2 + 2 H2O. 2. Electrolytic Oxidation (Industrial Method): Electrolysis of alkaline manganate solution using iron electrodes: At Anode: MnO4²⁻ -> MnO4⁻ + e⁻.
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
Assign reasons for the following: (i) Copper(I) ion is not stable in aqueous solution. (ii) Actinoids show irregularities in their electronic configurations. (iii) Transition metals have high melting and boiling points. (iv) The lowest oxide of transition metal is basic, the intermediate is amphoteric, and the highest is acidic.
Answer: Four concise reasons for fundamental transition metal properties
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Cu⁺ disproportionates in water: 2 Cu⁺(aq) -> Cu²⁺(aq) + Cu(s) because the highly negative hydration enthalpy of Cu²⁺ easily compensates for the second ionization enthalpy. (ii) The energy difference between 5f, 6d, and 7s subshells is extremely small; electrons easily shift between these orbitals depending on slight differences in nuclear charge and exchange energy. (iii) Strong metallic bonding involving both ns and unpaired (n-1)d electrons requires tremendous thermal energy to break. (iv) In lower oxides (e.g. MnO), the metal has low oxidation state and can donate electrons/oxide ions (basic). In intermediate oxides (MnO2), it exhibits amphoteric character. In high oxides (Mn2O7), high charge density makes the metal highly electronegative, withdrawing electron density from oxygen and acting as a Lewis acid.
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) What are interstitial compounds? Why are such compounds well known for transition metals? (b) State three characteristic properties of interstitial compounds. (c) Calculate the number of unpaired electrons in Mn⁴⁺ ion (Z = 25).
Answer: Interstitial compounds definition, properties, and unpaired electrons in Mn⁴⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Definition and Formation (2 marks): - Interstitial compounds are non-stoichiometric chemical compounds formed when small non-metallic atoms (such as H, B, C, N) occupy the interstitial interstitial voids in the close-packed crystal lattice of transition metals (e.g. TiC, Mn4N, Fe3H). - Transition metals have large atomic radii and open crystal structures that provide voids of suitable dimensions to house these small atoms. (b) Properties (2 marks): 1. Very high melting points, higher than those of the corresponding pure metals. 2. Extremely hard; some borides approach diamond in hardness. 3. Retain metallic electrical and thermal conductivity. 4. Chemically inert and resistant to attack by acids and bases. (c) Unpaired electrons in Mn⁴⁺ (1 mark): - Mn (Z = 25): [Ar] 3d⁵ 4s². - Mn⁴⁺: [Ar] 3d³. - Number of unpaired electrons n = 3.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
A violet compound 'A' of manganese decomposes on heating to 513 K to give a green compound 'B' and a black compound 'C' along with oxygen gas. Compound 'B' on treatment with dilute H2SO4 turns violet again yielding 'A' and 'C'. Identify compounds A, B, and C, and write balanced chemical equations for all reactions involved.
Answer: A = KMnO4, B = K2MnO4, C = MnO2 with balanced equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Identification: - Compound A: Potassium permanganate (KMnO4) - violet/purple solid. - Compound B: Potassium manganate (K2MnO4) - dark green solid. - Compound C: Manganese dioxide (MnO2) - black powder. 2. Chemical Reactions: - Heating of A (513 K): 2 KMnO4 (A) -(513 K)-> K2MnO4 (B) + MnO2 (C) + O2(g) - Reaction of B with dilute H2SO4 (disproportionation): 3 K2MnO4 (B) + 2 H2SO4 -> 2 KMnO4 (A, violet) + MnO2 (C, black) + 2 K2SO4 + 2 H2O.
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) Give reasons for the following: (i) Transition elements show catalytic properties. (ii) Zinc has lowest enthalpy of atomization in 3d series. (iii) Transition metals form coloured complexes. (b) Write balanced chemical equations for: (i) Reaction of acidified KMnO4 with sodium sulfite (Na2SO3). (ii) Reaction of alkaline KMnO4 with potassium iodide (KI).
Answer: Explanations of catalytic, atomization, and colour properties, and balanced redox equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Explanations (3 marks): (i) Catalytic Properties: Ability to adopt multiple oxidation states to form reaction intermediates, vacant d-orbitals to coordinate reactants, and provision of active surface sites for chemisorption. (ii) Zinc Enthalpy of Atomization: In Zn ([Ar] 3d¹⁰ 4s²), all 3d orbitals are completely filled and do not participate in metallic bonding. Only weak 4s metallic bonding exists. (iii) Coloured Complexes: In presence of ligands, d-orbitals split into t2g and eg sets. Absorption of light in visible region causes excitation of electrons from lower to higher d-orbital (d-d transition), and complementary color is transmitted. (b) Balanced Equations (2 marks): (i) With Na2SO3 (acidic medium): 2 MnO4⁻ + 5 SO3²⁻ + 6 H⁺ -> 2 Mn²⁺ + 5 SO4²⁻ + 3 H2O. (ii) With KI (faintly alkaline / neutral medium - iodide is oxidized to iodate): 2 MnO4⁻ + I⁻ + H2O -> 2 MnO2 + IO3⁻ (iodate) + 2 OH⁻.
Inorganic Chemistry
Chapter 5: Coordination Compounds
7 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The correct IUPAC name of the complex [Co(NH3)5(CO3)]Cl is:
(a) Pentaamminecarbonatocobalt(III) chloride
(b) Pentaamminecarbonatocobalt(II) chloride
(c) Carbonatopentaamminecobalt(III) chloride
(d) Pentaamminechlorocobalt(III) carbonate
Answer: (a) Pentaamminecarbonatocobalt(III) chloride
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Ligands are named alphabetically: 'ammine' (5 = pentaammine) before 'carbonato'. Oxidation state of Co: x + 5(0) + (-2) + (-1) = 0 => x = +3. Hence: Pentaamminecarbonatocobalt(III) chloride.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following is an ambidentate ligand?
(a) NO2⁻
(b) H2O
(c) NH3
(d) C2O4²⁻
Answer: (a) NO2⁻
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
An ambidentate ligand has two different donor atoms through which it can coordinate to the central metal atom. NO2⁻ can coordinate via nitrogen (-NO2, nitro) or via oxygen (-ONO, nitrito).
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The coordination number and oxidation state of cobalt in the complex [Co(en)2Cl2]⁺ are respectively:
(a) 6 and +3
(b) 4 and +2
(c) 6 and +2
(d) 4 and +3
Answer: (a) 6 and +3
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
'en' (ethane-1,2-diamine) is a bidentate ligand contributing 2 donor atoms each (2 × 2 = 4). Cl⁻ is unidentate (2 × 1 = 2). Total coordination number = 4 + 2 = 6. Oxidation state: x + 2(0) + 2(-1) = +1 => x = +3.
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following complexes is an inner orbital complex and diamagnetic?
(a) [Co(NH3)6]³⁺
(b) [CoF6]³⁻
(c) [Ni(H2O)6]²⁺
(d) [Fe(H2O)6]²⁺
Answer: (a) [Co(NH3)6]³⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Co³⁺ has [Ar] 3d⁶. NH3 acts as a strong field ligand for Co(III), causing pairing of 3d electrons into t2g⁶ eg⁰ (0 unpaired electrons, diamagnetic). Two vacant 3d orbitals, one 4s, and three 4p orbitals hybridize to form d²sp³ (inner orbital complex).
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The type of isomerism shown by [Co(NH3)5(SO4)]Br and [Co(NH3)5Br]SO4 is:
(a) Ionisation isomerism
(b) Linkage isomerism
(c) Coordination isomerism
(d) Hydrate isomerism
Answer: (a) Ionisation isomerism
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
These two isomers give different ions in aqueous solution: the first gives bromide ion Br⁻ (giving cream precipitate with AgNO3), while the second gives sulfate ion SO4²⁻ (giving white precipitate with BaCl2).
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The complex [Pt(NH3)2Cl2] exists in two geometrical isomeric forms. The cis-isomer is commercially known as:
(a) Cisplatin
(b) Chelate
(c) Ferrocene
(d) Zeise's salt
Answer: (a) Cisplatin
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
cis-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2] is famously known as Cisplatin and is widely used as a potent chemotherapy drug in cancer treatment.
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
According to Crystal Field Theory, for an octahedral d⁴ complex with strong field ligands (Δo > P), the electronic configuration is:
(a) t2g⁴ eg⁰
(b) t2g³ eg¹
(c) t2g² eg²
(d) t2g¹ eg³
Answer: (a) t2g⁴ eg⁰
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
When crystal field splitting energy Δo is greater than the pairing energy P (Δo > P), electrons prefer to pair up in the lower-energy t2g orbitals rather than jump to higher-energy eg orbitals, yielding low-spin t2g⁴ eg⁰.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
The relationship between crystal field splitting in tetrahedral (Δt) and octahedral (Δo) complexes is:
(a) Δt = 4/9 Δo
(b) Δt = 9/4 Δo
(c) Δt = Δo / 2
(d) Δt = 2 Δo
Answer: (a) Δt = 4/9 Δo
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In a tetrahedral field, there are only 4 ligands compared to 6 in octahedral field (factor of 4/6), and the ligands do not point directly along the metal d-orbital lobes (factor of 2/3). Thus: Δt = (4/6) * (2/3) Δo = 4/9 Δo.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
Which of the following complexes is optically active?
(a) cis-[Co(en)2Cl2]⁺
(b) trans-[Co(en)2Cl2]⁺
(c) trans-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2]
(d) [Co(NH3)4Cl2]⁺
Answer: (a) cis-[Co(en)2Cl2]⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
cis-[Co(en)2Cl2]⁺ lacks a plane of symmetry and center of inversion, making it chiral. It exists as non-superimposable d- and l-enantiomers. The trans-isomer has a center of symmetry and plane of symmetry, so it is achiral (optically inactive).
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The hybridization and shape of [Ni(CN)4]²⁻ and [NiCl4]²⁻ respectively are:
(a) dsp² (square planar) and sp³ (tetrahedral)
(b) sp³ (tetrahedral) and dsp² (square planar)
(c) dsp² (square planar) and dsp² (square planar)
(d) sp³ (tetrahedral) and sp³ (tetrahedral)
Answer: (a) dsp² (square planar) and sp³ (tetrahedral)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Ni²⁺ is 3d⁸. CN⁻ is a strong field ligand that forces pairing of the two unpaired 3d electrons into one d-orbital, freeing one 3d orbital for dsp² hybridization (square planar, diamagnetic). Cl⁻ is a weak field ligand that cannot force pairing, so 4s and 4p orbitals hybridize as sp³ (tetrahedral, paramagnetic with 2 unpaired electrons).
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
The chelating ligand among the following is:
(a) Oxalate (ox)
(b) Cyanide (CN⁻)
(c) Ammonia (NH3)
(d) Chloride (Cl⁻)
Answer: (a) Oxalate (ox)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Oxalate ion (C2O4²⁻) is a didentate ligand with two carboxylate oxygen donor atoms that coordinate to the same central metal ion to form a stable 5-membered chelate ring.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
The correct order of increasing field strength of ligands according to the spectrochemical series is:
(a) I⁻ < Cl⁻ < F⁻ < H2O < NH3 < CN⁻ < CO
(b) CO < CN⁻ < NH3 < H2O < F⁻ < Cl⁻ < I⁻
(c) Cl⁻ < I⁻ < H2O < F⁻ < CN⁻ < NH3 < CO
(d) H2O < I⁻ < Cl⁻ < F⁻ < NH3 < CO < CN⁻
Answer: (a) I⁻ < Cl⁻ < F⁻ < H2O < NH3 < CN⁻ < CO
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In the spectrochemical series, halides are weak field ligands, oxygen donors are intermediate, nitrogen donors are stronger, and carbon-donor ligands (cyanide, carbonyl) are the strongest field ligands.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Synergic bonding in metal carbonyls involves:
(a) σ-donation from C to metal and π-back donation from metal d-orbital to vacant π* of CO
(b) only σ-bond formation
(c) only π-bond formation
(d) electron transfer from metal to carbon σ-orbital
Answer: (a) σ-donation from C to metal and π-back donation from metal d-orbital to vacant π* of CO
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In metal carbonyls, a σ bond is formed by donation of lone pair of electrons on carbonyl carbon into a vacant d-orbital of the metal. Simultaneously, a π-back bond is formed by donation of a pair of electrons from a filled d-orbital of metal into the vacant antibonding π* orbital of CO. This synergic effect strengthens the M-C bond.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following compounds gives 3 moles of AgCl precipitate per mole of complex when treated with excess AgNO3 solution?
(a) [Co(NH3)6]Cl3
(b) [Co(NH3)5Cl]Cl2
(c) [Co(NH3)4Cl2]Cl
(d) [Co(NH3)3Cl3]
Answer: (a) [Co(NH3)6]Cl3
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
[Co(NH3)6]Cl3 has three ionizable chloride ions outside the coordination sphere: [Co(NH3)6]Cl3 -> [Co(NH3)6]³⁺ + 3 Cl⁻. Reaction with excess AgNO3 precipitates 3 moles of AgCl.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Facial (fac) and meridional (mer) isomerism is exhibited by complexes of the general formula:
(a) [Ma3b3]
(b) [Ma4b2]
(c) [M(AA)2b2]
(d) [Ma2b2]
Answer: (a) [Ma3b3]
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Octahedral complexes of the type [Ma3b3] (such as [Co(NH3)3(NO2)3]) exhibit facial (fac) isomerism when three identical ligands occupy the corners of an octahedral face, and meridional (mer) isomerism when they occupy positions around the meridian.

CBSE 2021 Term-1
The number of donor atoms present in EDTA⁴⁻ ligand is:
(a) 6 (hexadentate)
(b) 4 (tetradentate)
(c) 2 (bidentate)
(d) 1 (unidentate)
Answer: (a) 6 (hexadentate)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
EDTA⁴⁻ (ethylenediaminetetraacetate ion) is a hexadentate ligand possessing 2 nitrogen atoms and 4 carboxylate oxygen atoms as donor sites.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
The spin-only magnetic moment of [Mn(CN)6]³⁻ is (Mn Z = 25):
(a) 2.83 BM
(b) 4.90 BM
(c) 5.92 BM
(d) 1.73 BM
Answer: (a) 2.83 BM
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Mn³⁺ has [Ar] 3d⁴. CN⁻ is a strong field ligand, forcing pairing: t2g⁴ eg⁰. Number of unpaired electrons n = 2. Spin-only magnetic moment μ = √(2(2+2)) = √8 ≈ 2.83 BM.
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Which of the following complex ions is expected to absorb visible light and appear violet?
(a) [Ti(H2O)6]³⁺
(b) [Sc(H2O)6]³⁺
(c) [Zn(H2O)6]²⁺
(d) [Cu(CN)4]³⁻
Answer: (a) [Ti(H2O)6]³⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
[Ti(H2O)6]³⁺ has Ti³⁺ (3d¹). The single electron in t2g orbital absorbs green-yellow light (~500 nm) to get promoted to eg orbital (d-d transition). The transmitted complementary colour is violet. Sc³⁺, Zn²⁺, and Cu⁺ have d⁰ or d¹⁰ configurations and are colourless.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
What is the oxidation state of Fe in brown ring complex [Fe(H2O)5(NO)]SO4?
(a) +1
(b) +2
(c) +3
(d) 0
Answer: (a) +1
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In the brown ring complex, nitric oxide coordinates as the nitrosonium cation (NO⁺): [Fe(H2O)5(NO⁺)]²⁺ SO4²⁻. Thus, x + 5(0) + (+1) = +2 => x = +1.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Due to the 'chelate effect', a complex containing chelating ligands is:
(a) more stable than similar complexes containing unidentate ligands
(b) less stable
(c) highly unstable and decomposes
(d) optically inactive always
Answer: (a) more stable than similar complexes containing unidentate ligands
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Chelating ligands form cyclic ring structures with the central metal ion. Chelate formation causes a large increase in entropy (ΔS > 0) due to release of displaced unidentate solvent molecules, making ΔG° more negative and conferring enhanced thermodynamic stability.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
The primary valency and secondary valency in Werner's coordination theory correspond respectively to:
(a) oxidation state and coordination number
(b) coordination number and oxidation state
(c) charge and magnetic moment
(d) valence electrons and atomic number
Answer: (a) oxidation state and coordination number
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
According to Werner's theory, primary valency is ionizable and satisfied by negative ions (corresponding to the oxidation state). Secondary valency is non-ionizable, directional, and satisfied by neutral molecules or negative ions (corresponding to the coordination number).
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
Which of the following has a square planar geometry?
(a) [Ni(CN)4]²⁻
(b) [NiCl4]²⁻
(c) [Ni(CO)4]
(d) [ZnCl4]²⁻
Answer: (a) [Ni(CN)4]²⁻
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
[Ni(CN)4]²⁻ contains Ni²⁺ (3d⁸) and strong ligand CN⁻. The electrons pair up to vacate one 3d orbital, giving dsp² hybridization which corresponds to square planar geometry.
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The complex [Cr(H2O)6]Cl3 (violet) and [Cr(H2O)5Cl]Cl2·H2O (grey-green) are examples of:
(a) Hydrate (Solvate) isomerism
(b) Ionisation isomerism
(c) Coordination isomerism
(d) Linkage isomerism
Answer: (a) Hydrate (Solvate) isomerism
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
These isomers differ in whether water molecules are directly bonded to the metal center as ligands inside the coordination sphere or present as free water of crystallization in the crystal lattice.

Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which of the following compounds is used in the treatment of lead poisoning?
(a) Ca-EDTA chelate
(b) Cisplatin
(c) D-penicillamine
(d) Dimercaprol
Answer: (a) Ca-EDTA chelate
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Calcium disodium EDTA (Ca-EDTA chelate) is administered in lead poisoning; lead ions displace calcium from the chelate because Pb-EDTA complex is far more stable, and the soluble Pb-chelate is excreted safely in urine.
Q26. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2024
Assertion (A): [Fe(CN)6]³⁻ is weakly paramagnetic with one unpaired electron, whereas [FeF6]³⁻ is strongly paramagnetic with 5 unpaired electrons. Reason (R): CN⁻ is a strong field ligand that causes pairing of 3d electrons, whereas F⁻ is a weak field ligand that cannot force pairing.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R correctly explains A. Fe³⁺ is 3d⁵. With CN⁻, Δo > P resulting in t2g⁵ eg⁰ (n = 1). With F⁻, Δo < P resulting in t2g³ eg² (n = 5).

Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)

Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Using Valence Bond Theory (VBT), predict the hybridization, geometry, and magnetic behaviour of [CoF6]³⁻. (Atomic number of Co = 27).
Answer: sp³d², octahedral, paramagnetic (4 unpaired electrons)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Oxidation state: Co³⁺ (Z = 27): [Ar] 3d⁶ 4s⁰ 4p⁰ 4d⁰. 2. Ligand nature: Fluoride ion (F⁻) is a weak field ligand, so pairing of 3d electrons does not take place (pairing energy P > Δo). 3. 3d configuration remains: ↑↓ ↑ ↑ ↑ ↑ (4 unpaired electrons). 4. Empty orbitals used: One 4s, three 4p, and two outer 4d orbitals hybridize to give sp³d² hybridization. 5. Geometry: Octahedral (outer orbital complex). 6. Magnetic Nature: Paramagnetic with 4 unpaired electrons (μ = √24 ≈ 4.90 BM).
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
Draw the structures of optical isomers of [Co(en)3]³⁺.
Answer: d- and l-enantiomers of [Co(en)3]³⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Structures: [Co(en)3]³⁺ is an octahedral complex with three bidentate ethylenediamine ligands. It has no plane of symmetry and forms two non-superimposable mirror image enantiomers: 1. Dextrorotatory (d or Δ-isomer): The three 'en' rings form a right-handed propeller helix. 2. Laevorotatory (l or Λ-isomer): Mirror image with left-handed propeller orientation.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (3 Marks)
Define the following terms with one example each: (i) Ambidentate ligand (ii) Chelate ligand (iii) Coordination sphere
Answer: Definitions and examples
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Ambidentate Ligand: A unidentate ligand containing two different donor atoms that can coordinate to the central metal atom through either atom. Example: NO2⁻ (coordinates through N as nitro, or through O as nitrito). (ii) Chelate Ligand: A di- or polydentate ligand that coordinates to a single metal ion via two or more donor atoms simultaneously, forming a cyclic ring structure. Example: Ethane-1,2-diamine (en) or oxalate (ox). (iii) Coordination Sphere: The central metal atom/ion together with its directly coordinated ligands enclosed within square brackets [ ], which behaves as a single non-ionizable discrete entity in solution. Example: [Co(NH3)6]³⁺ in [Co(NH3)6]Cl3.
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (2 Marks)
What is spectrochemical series? Explain the difference between a weak field ligand and a strong field ligand.
Answer: Spectrochemical series and ligand field strength
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Spectrochemical Series: An experimentally determined series arranging ligands in increasing order of their crystal field splitting energy (Δo) values. 2. Difference: - Weak Field Ligand: Ligands for which crystal field splitting is small (Δo < P). They cannot force electron pairing and produce high-spin complexes (e.g. I⁻, Br⁻, Cl⁻, F⁻). - Strong Field Ligand: Ligands producing large crystal field splitting (Δo > P). They force electrons to pair up in lower orbitals and form low-spin complexes (e.g. CN⁻, CO, en, NH3).
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (3 Marks)
Give the formula and IUPAC name of the coordination entity formed when: (i) Iron(II) coordinates with six cyanide ions. (ii) Platinum(II) coordinates with two ammonia molecules and two chloride ions. (iii) Nickel(0) coordinates with four carbon monoxide molecules.
Answer: Formulas and IUPAC names
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Formula: [Fe(CN)6]⁴⁻; IUPAC Name: Hexacyanidoferrate(II) ion. (ii) Formula: [Pt(NH3)2Cl2]; IUPAC Name: Diamminedichloridoplatinum(II). (iii) Formula: [Ni(CO)4]; IUPAC Name: Tetracarbonylnickel(0).
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (2 Marks)
Draw the geometrical isomers (cis and trans) of [Pt(NH3)2Cl2]. Which of these isomers shows antitumor activity?
Answer: cis- and trans-isomers, Cisplatin is antitumor
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Structures: Square planar Pt(II) complex. - cis-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2]: The two Cl⁻ ligands occupy adjacent positions (90° apart), and the two NH3 ligands occupy adjacent positions. - trans-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2]: The two Cl⁻ ligands are opposite (180° apart), and the two NH3 ligands are opposite. 2. Antitumor Activity: cis-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2] (Cisplatin) specifically binds to DNA of cancer cells and inhibits replication, exhibiting potent antitumor properties. The trans-isomer is biologically inactive.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (3 Marks)
Explain why [NiCl4]²⁻ is paramagnetic while [Ni(CO)4] is diamagnetic, though both are tetrahedral.
Answer: Comparison of magnetic nature based on VBT/ligand strength
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. [NiCl4]²⁻: Nickel is in +2 oxidation state (3d⁸). Cl⁻ is a weak field ligand, so no electron pairing occurs. 3d subshell retains two unpaired electrons (↑↓ ↑↓ ↑↓ ↑ ↑). 4s and 4p orbitals hybridize as sp³ (tetrahedral). Due to 2 unpaired electrons, [NiCl4]²⁻ is paramagnetic (μ = 2.83 BM). 2. [Ni(CO)4]: Nickel is in 0 oxidation state (3d⁸ 4s²). CO is a very strong field ligand; it forces both 4s electrons into the 3d subshell, pairing up all electrons to give a completely filled 3d¹⁰ configuration (↑↓ ↑↓ ↑↓ ↑↓ ↑↓). The empty 4s and 4p orbitals hybridize as sp³ (tetrahedral). With zero unpaired electrons, [Ni(CO)4] is diamagnetic.
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (2 Marks)
Explain linkage isomerism with a suitable example.
Answer: Explanation and example of linkage isomerism
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Explanation: Linkage isomerism arises in a coordination compound containing an ambidentate ligand that can bind to the central metal atom through either of two different donor atoms. 2. Example: - Yellow form: [Co(NH3)5(NO2)]Cl2 (Pentaamminenitrito-N-cobalt(III) chloride) where NO2⁻ coordinates via Nitrogen atom (Co-NO2). - Red form: [Co(NH3)5(ONO)]Cl2 (Pentaamminenitrito-O-cobalt(III) chloride) where NO2⁻ coordinates via Oxygen atom (Co-ONO).
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
What is the crystal field splitting energy (CFSE)? Draw a diagram showing splitting of d-orbitals in an octahedral crystal field.
Answer: CFSE definition and octahedral splitting diagram
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. CFSE: The energy difference between the lower energy t2g set (dxy, dyz, dzx) and higher energy eg set (dx²-y², dz²) resulting from electrostatic repulsion between metal d-electrons and surrounding ligands. Designated as Δo. 2. Diagram: - Free metal ion has five degenerate d-orbitals. - In spherical field, energy raises uniformly. - In octahedral field, splits into: - Lower triplet t2g (stabilized by -0.4 Δo or -2/5 Δo) - Upper doublet eg (destabilized by +0.6 Δo or +3/5 Δo) - Total separation = Δo (or 10 Dq).
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) Discuss the nature of bonding in metal carbonyls with the help of a neat orbital overlap diagram. (b) Write the formula and calculate the coordination number of central metal in: (i) Potassium trioxalatoferrate(III) (ii) Dichloridobis(ethane-1,2-diamine)platinum(IV) nitrate. (c) Why are tetrahedral complexes always of high spin?
Answer: Bonding in metal carbonyls, formulas & CN, and tetrahedral high spin justification
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Synergic Bonding in Metal Carbonyls (2.5 marks): - In metal carbonyls (e.g. Ni(CO)4, Fe(CO)5), bonding involves both σ and π components: 1. Metal-Carbon σ-bond: Formed by donation of lone pair of electrons on the carbonyl carbon atom into a vacant hybrid orbital of the transition metal (M <- :C≡O). 2. Metal-Carbon π-bond (Back-bonding): Formed by donation of a pair of electrons from a filled metal d-orbital (dxy, dyz, or dzx) into the empty antibonding π* molecular orbital of carbon monoxide (M -> π* CO). - This synergic bonding creates a mutual strengthening effect: σ-donation increases electron density on metal, facilitating π-back donation, which in turn strengthens M-C bond while weakening C-O bond. (b) Formulas and Coordination Numbers (1.5 marks): (i) Potassium trioxalatoferrate(III): K3[Fe(C2O4)3]. Oxalate is bidentate (3 × 2 = 6). Coordination number = 6. (ii) Dichloridobis(ethane-1,2-diamine)platinum(IV) nitrate: [Pt(en)2Cl2](NO3)2. Coordination number = 2(2) + 2(1) = 6. (c) Why Tetrahedral Complexes are High Spin (1 mark): - In tetrahedral geometry, Δt = (4/9) Δo. The crystal field splitting is very small and is always less than the pairing energy (Δt < P). Consequently, electrons always prefer to enter higher energy orbitals rather than pairing up, so low-spin tetrahedral complexes are rarely observed.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
Apply Crystal Field Theory (CFT) to explain: (a) Why [Co(NH3)6]³⁺ is diamagnetic whereas [CoF6]³⁻ is paramagnetic. (b) The electronic configuration of d⁴, d⁵, d⁶, and d⁷ in an octahedral field for both weak field (Δo < P) and strong field (Δo > P) ligands.
Answer: CFT analysis of Co(III) complexes and CF electronic configurations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Co(III) Complexes (2.5 marks): - In both complexes, cobalt is in +3 state: Co³⁺ (3d⁶). - [Co(NH3)6]³⁺: NH3 is a strong field ligand. Δo > P. Electrons pair up in t2g orbitals: configuration is t2g⁶ eg⁰. Since there are 0 unpaired electrons, it is diamagnetic. - [CoF6]³⁻: F⁻ is a weak field ligand. Δo < P. Pairing does not occur until all orbitals are singly occupied: configuration is t2g⁴ eg² (↑↓ ↑ ↑ in t2g, ↑ ↑ in eg). There are 4 unpaired electrons; hence it is paramagnetic (μ ≈ 4.90 BM). (b) Octahedral Configurations (2.5 marks): - d⁴: Weak field (Δo < P): t2g³ eg¹ (High spin, n = 4) Strong field (Δo > P): t2g⁴ eg⁰ (Low spin, n = 2) - d⁵: Weak field: t2g³ eg² (High spin, n = 5) Strong field: t2g⁵ eg⁰ (Low spin, n = 1) - d⁶: Weak field: t2g⁴ eg² (High spin, n = 4) Strong field: t2g⁶ eg⁰ (Low spin, n = 0) - d⁷: Weak field: t2g⁵ eg² (High spin, n = 3) Strong field: t2g⁶ eg¹ (Low spin, n = 1).
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) A coordination compound CrCl3·6H2O is violet in colour. One mole of this compound on treatment with excess AgNO3 yields 3 moles of AgCl precipitate. (i) Write the structural formula of the compound. (ii) Write its IUPAC name. (b) Another isomer of the same formula is green in colour and gives only 1 mole of AgCl with excess AgNO3. (i) Write the structural formula of this isomer. (ii) Write its IUPAC name. (c) What type of isomerism is exhibited by these two compounds?
Answer: Structural identification of chromium(III) hydrate isomers
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Violet Compound (2 marks): (i) Since 1 mole yields 3 moles of AgCl, all three chloride ions must be ionizable and located outside the coordination sphere: [Cr(H2O)6]Cl3. (ii) IUPAC Name: Hexaaquachromium(III) chloride. (b) Green Isomer (2 marks): (i) Since only 1 mole of AgCl is precipitated, only 1 chloride is outside the coordination sphere. Two chlorides are inside as ligands: [Cr(H2O)4Cl2]Cl·2H2O. (ii) IUPAC Name: Tetraaquadichloridochromium(III) chloride dihydrate. (c) Type of Isomerism (1 mark): Hydrate (or Solvate) Isomerism, because the isomers differ in the number of water molecules coordinated directly to the metal ion versus those held as water of crystallization.
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Coordination Complexes in Medicine and Biology Coordination compounds play indispensable roles in living organisms and pharmacological therapies. Chlorophyll, the green pigment in plants responsible for photosynthesis, is a coordination compound of magnesium. Hemoglobin, the oxygen-transport protein in red blood cells, contains an iron(II)-porphyrin complex (heme). Vitamin B12 (cyanocobalamin) is a coordination complex of cobalt(III). In modern medicine, synthetic chelating agents are deployed to remove toxic heavy metals from the body (chelation therapy). For example, excess copper in Wilson's disease is removed by D-penicillamine, and lead toxicity is treated with calcium-disodium EDTA. (i) Name the central metal ion present in: (a) Chlorophyll, (b) Hemoglobin. (ii) Explain how EDTA removes lead ions from the bloodstream in lead poisoning. (iii) Which coordination compound of platinum is used as an anticancer drug? State its geometry. (iv) What is meant by the 'chelate effect'?
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Bioinorganic Coordination Chemistry
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) (a) Chlorophyll: Magnesium (Mg²⁺); (b) Hemoglobin: Iron (Fe²⁺). (ii) Calcium disodium EDTA [Ca(EDTA)]²⁻ is injected intravenously. Lead (Pb²⁺) forms a far more thermodynamically stable chelate complex with EDTA⁴⁻ than Ca²⁺ (higher stability constant). Pb²⁺ displaces Ca²⁺, and the stable, water-soluble [Pb(EDTA)]²⁻ chelate is safely excreted in urine. (iii) Cisplatin (cis-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2]); Geometry: Square planar (dsp² hybridization). (iv) Chelate Effect: The enhanced thermodynamic stability of a coordination complex formed by polydentate (chelating) ligands compared to analogous complexes formed by unidentate ligands, driven primarily by favorable entropy increase (ΔS > 0).
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) State Werner's coordination theory. Write its main postulates. (b) What are the primary and secondary valencies of cobalt in the following compounds: (i) [Co(NH3)6]Cl3 (ii) [Co(NH3)5Cl]Cl2 (iii) [Co(NH3)4Cl2]Cl (c) How many ions are produced by each compound in aqueous solution?
Answer: Werner's theory postulates, valencies, and ionization behaviour
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Werner's Postulates (2 marks): 1. In coordination compounds, metals exhibit two types of valency: Primary (ionizable) and Secondary (non-ionizable). 2. Primary valency corresponds to the oxidation state of the metal and is satisfied only by negative ions. 3. Secondary valency corresponds to the coordination number and is satisfied by neutral molecules or negative ions. 4. Secondary valencies are directed towards fixed spatial positions around the metal, determining the definite stereochemistry/geometry of the complex. (b) & (c) Valencies and Ionization (3 marks): (i) [Co(NH3)6]Cl3: - Primary valency = 3 (oxidation state Co³⁺) - Secondary valency = 6 (coordination number) - Ions produced = 4 ions (1 [Co(NH3)6]³⁺ + 3 Cl⁻). (ii) [Co(NH3)5Cl]Cl2: - Primary valency = 3 - Secondary valency = 6 (5 NH3 + 1 Cl⁻) - Ions produced = 3 ions (1 [Co(NH3)5Cl]²⁺ + 2 Cl⁻). (iii) [Co(NH3)4Cl2]Cl: - Primary valency = 3 - Secondary valency = 6 (4 NH3 + 2 Cl⁻) - Ions produced = 2 ions (1 [Co(NH3)4Cl2]⁺ + 1 Cl⁻).
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Give reasons for the following: (i) [Ni(CO)4] possesses tetrahedral geometry while [Ni(CN)4]²⁻ is square planar. (ii) [Fe(H2O)6]³⁺ is strongly paramagnetic whereas [Fe(CN)6]³⁻ is weakly paramagnetic. (b) Write the IUPAC names and draw the structures of geometrical isomers of [Co(NH3)4Cl2]⁺.
Answer: VBT geometry comparisons and cis/trans isomers of [Co(NH3)4Cl2]⁺
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Reasons (3 marks): (i) Geometry Comparison: In [Ni(CO)4], Ni(0) has 3d⁸ 4s². Strong ligand CO forces 4s electrons into 3d to make 3d¹⁰; 4s and 4p hybridize as sp³ (tetrahedral). In [Ni(CN)4]²⁻, Ni(II) is 3d⁸; strong ligand CN⁻ forces pairing of 3d electrons, leaving one empty 3d orbital for dsp² hybridization (square planar). (ii) Paramagnetism: In [Fe(H2O)6]³⁺, H2O is a weak field ligand (Δo < P). The 3d⁵ electrons remain unpaired (t2g³ eg²), giving 5 unpaired electrons (strongly paramagnetic, μ = 5.92 BM). In [Fe(CN)6]³⁻, CN⁻ is a strong field ligand (Δo > P), forcing pairing into t2g⁵ eg⁰, leaving only 1 unpaired electron (weakly paramagnetic, μ = 1.73 BM). (b) IUPAC and Geometrical Isomers (2 marks): - IUPAC Name: Tetraamminedichloridocobalt(III) ion. - cis-isomer: Two Cl⁻ ligands at 90° to each other (adjacent corners of octahedron). - trans-isomer: Two Cl⁻ ligands at 180° to each other (opposite axial positions).
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
Explain the following types of isomerism in coordination compounds with one suitable example for each: (i) Ionisation isomerism (ii) Solvate (hydrate) isomerism (iii) Coordination isomerism (iv) Linkage isomerism.
Answer: Four structural isomerisms with representative examples
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Ionisation Isomerism: Arises when counter ion in a coordination compound is itself a potential ligand and can displace a ligand from the coordination sphere, producing different ions in solution. Example: [Co(NH3)5SO4]Br (gives Br⁻) and [Co(NH3)5Br]SO4 (gives SO4²⁻). (ii) Solvate Isomerism: Compounds having identical empirical formula but differing in whether solvent molecules (like water) act as ligands or are held as crystal water. Example: [Cr(H2O)6]Cl3 (violet) and [Cr(H2O)5Cl]Cl2·H2O (grey-green). (iii) Coordination Isomerism: Occurs in compounds where both cation and anion are complex ions, arising from interchange of ligands between the two metal centers. Example: [Co(NH3)6][Cr(CN)6] and [Cr(NH3)6][Co(CN)6]. (iv) Linkage Isomerism: Occurs in complexes containing ambidentate ligands having two donor atoms. Example: [Co(NH3)5(NO2)]Cl2 (nitro, yellow) and [Co(NH3)5(ONO)]Cl2 (nitrito, red).
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) What are the limitations of Valence Bond Theory (VBT)? (b) How does Crystal Field Theory (CFT) overcome these limitations? (c) Calculate the CFSE for high-spin and low-spin d⁶ octahedral complexes.
Answer: VBT limitations, CFT advantages, and CFSE calculations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Limitations of VBT (2 marks): 1. It does not explain the origin of colour and electronic absorption spectra of complexes. 2. It does not give a quantitative interpretation of magnetic data (temperature dependence of magnetism). 3. It does not distinguish clearly between weak and strong field ligands. 4. It fails to give quantitative thermodynamic or kinetic stability of complexes. (b) How CFT Overcomes Limitations (1.5 marks): - CFT treats ligands as point charges/dipoles splitting degenerate d-orbitals into t2g and eg sets. It directly explains colour in terms of d-d electronic transitions, accounts quantitatively for magnetic moments, and establishes the spectrochemical series. (c) CFSE Calculations for d⁶ (1.5 marks): Formula: CFSE = [ - 0.4 * n(t2g) + 0.6 * n(eg) ] * Δo + m * P 1. High Spin (Weak Field, Δo < P): Configuration = t2g⁴ eg². CFSE = [ - 0.4(4) + 0.6(2) ] Δo = [ - 1.6 + 1.2 ] Δo = - 0.4 Δo. 2. Low Spin (Strong Field, Δo > P): Configuration = t2g⁶ eg⁰. CFSE = [ - 0.4(6) + 0.6(0) ] Δo + 2 P = - 2.4 Δo + 2 P.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
(a) What is meant by unidentate, bidentate, and ambidentate ligands? Give one example of each. (b) Write the formulas of the following: (i) Potassium tetrahydroxidozincate(II) (ii) Hexaammineplatinum(IV) chloride.
Answer: Ligand classifications and chemical formulas
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Ligand Classifications (2 marks): 1. Unidentate Ligand: A ligand that binds to the metal ion through a single donor atom. Example: Cl⁻, NH3, H2O. 2. Bidentate Ligand: A ligand that can coordinate to the metal ion through two donor atoms simultaneously. Example: Ethane-1,2-diamine (en: H2N-CH2-CH2-NH2) or oxalate ion (C2O4²⁻). 3. Ambidentate Ligand: A unidentate ligand that possesses two different donor atoms and can coordinate through either of them. Example: SCN⁻ (thiocyanato via S, or isothiocyanato via N). (b) Chemical Formulas (2 marks): (i) Potassium tetrahydroxidozincate(II): K2[Zn(OH)4]. (ii) Hexaammineplatinum(IV) chloride: [Pt(NH3)6]Cl4.
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) Explain why [Fe(H2O)6]³⁺ is strongly paramagnetic whereas [Fe(CN)6]³⁻ is weakly paramagnetic using CFT. (b) Draw the structures of optical isomers of: (i) [Cr(C2O4)3]³⁻ (ii) cis-[Pt(en)2Cl2]²⁺. (c) Name one coordination compound of each used in: (i) Cancer therapy (ii) Water hardness determination.
Answer: CFT explanation, optical isomer structures, and applications
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) CFT Explanation (2 marks): - Fe³⁺ has 3d⁵ configuration. - In [Fe(H2O)6]³⁺: H2O is a weak field ligand (Δo < P). The 5 electrons occupy 5 degenerate d-orbitals singly: t2g³ eg² (5 unpaired electrons, spin-only magnetic moment μ = √35 ≈ 5.92 BM, strongly paramagnetic). - In [Fe(CN)6]³⁻: CN⁻ is a strong field ligand (Δo > P). The electrons pair up in the t2g set: t2g⁵ eg⁰ (only 1 unpaired electron, μ = √3 ≈ 1.73 BM, weakly paramagnetic). (b) Optical Isomer Structures (2 marks): (i) [Cr(C2O4)3]³⁻: Forms d- and l-enantiomers (non-superimposable mirror images) with three bidentate oxalate chelate rings arranged in a propeller-like configuration. (ii) cis-[Pt(en)2Cl2]²⁺: Octahedral complex lacking plane of symmetry, forming non-superimposable d- and l-forms. (c) Applications (1 mark): (i) Cancer therapy: Cisplatin (cis-[Pt(NH3)2Cl2]). (ii) Water hardness determination: Disodium salt of EDTA (Na2EDTA forms stable chelate complexes with Ca²⁺ and Mg²⁺).
Organic Chemistry
Chapter 6: Haloalkanes and Haloarenes
6 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following alkyl halides undergoes nucleophilic substitution by SN1 mechanism most rapidly?
(a) (CH3)3C-Br
(b) (CH3)2CH-Br
(c) CH3-CH2-Br
(d) CH3-Br
Answer: (a) (CH3)3C-Br
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
SN1 reaction proceeds via the formation of a carbocation intermediate in the rate-determining step. The stability of carbocations follows the order: 3° > 2° > 1° > methyl. Tertiary butyl bromide (CH3)3C-Br forms the highly stable tertiary carbocation (CH3)3C⁺, and thus reacts fastest via SN1.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The conversion of an alkyl halide to an alkyl fluoride using AgF, Hg2F2, or CoF2 is known as:
(a) Swarts reaction
(b) Finkelstein reaction
(c) Wurtz reaction
(d) Sandmeyer reaction
Answer: (a) Swarts reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Heating an alkyl chloride or bromide in the presence of a metallic fluoride such as AgF, Hg2F2, CoF2, or SbF3 gives alkyl fluorides. This reaction is known as the Swarts reaction.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
An alkyl halide reacts with alcoholic KCN to form an alkyl cyanide as the major product, whereas with alcoholic AgCN it forms an alkyl isocyanide because:
(a) KCN is ionic providing cyanide ion with C-attack, whereas AgCN is predominantly covalent with N-lone pair attack
(b) AgCN is ionic and KCN is covalent
(c) Cyanide is a monodentate ligand
(d) Isocyanides are thermodynamically more stable than cyanides
Answer: (a) KCN is ionic providing cyanide ion with C-attack, whereas AgCN is predominantly covalent with N-lone pair attack
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
KCN is ionic and dissociates into K⁺ and :C≡N:⁻. Attack occurs primarily through carbon since C-C bond is much stronger than C-N bond, forming alkyl cyanides (R-CN). AgCN is predominantly covalent; only nitrogen has a free lone pair to attack the carbon of alkyl halide, forming alkyl isocyanides (R-NC).
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following compounds exhibits optical isomerism (chirality)?
(a) 2-Chlorobutane
(b) 1-Chlorobutane
(c) 2-Chloropropane
(d) Chloromethane
Answer: (a) 2-Chlorobutane
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In 2-chlorobutane (CH3-*CH(Cl)-CH2-CH3), the C-2 carbon atom is bonded to four completely different groups: -H, -Cl, -CH3, and -CH2CH3. This asymmetric (chiral) carbon makes the molecule chiral and optically active.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The major product formed when 2-bromopentane is heated with alcoholic KOH is:
(a) Pent-2-ene
(b) Pent-1-ene
(c) Pentan-2-ol
(d) Pentan-1-ol
Answer: (a) Pent-2-ene
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
According to Saytzeff's (Zaitsev's) rule, dehydrohalogenation of an alkyl halide yields predominantly the more substituted, highly alkylated, and thermodynamically more stable alkene. Elimination from 2-bromopentane yields Pent-2-ene (major, ~81%) and Pent-1-ene (minor, ~19%).
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Among the isomeric dichlorobenzenes, p-dichlorobenzene has a significantly higher melting point than ortho and meta isomers because:
(a) p-isomer has symmetrical structure that fits closely in the crystal lattice
(b) p-isomer has higher dipole moment
(c) p-isomer has stronger hydrogen bonding
(d) p-isomer has lower molecular weight
Answer: (a) p-isomer has symmetrical structure that fits closely in the crystal lattice
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The para-isomer has a highly symmetrical centrosymmetric shape. It packs much more tightly and uniformly into the solid crystal lattice than the less symmetrical ortho and meta isomers, requiring significantly more thermal energy to melt (mp: para = 325 K, ortho = 256 K, meta = 249 K).
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
SN2 reactions proceed with complete:
(a) Inversion of configuration (Walden inversion)
(b) Retention of configuration
(c) Racemization
(d) None of these
Answer: (a) Inversion of configuration (Walden inversion)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The SN2 mechanism is a concerted, single-step bimolecular reaction where the incoming nucleophile attacks the substrate from the side exactly opposite (180°) to the leaving group (backside attack). This causes an umbrella-like flip in spatial geometry, resulting in complete inversion of configuration (Walden inversion).
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Chlorobenzene is extremely less reactive towards nucleophilic substitution compared to chloroethane because of:
(a) Resonance stabilization and sp² hybridization of carbon in C-Cl bond
(b) Lower boiling point
(c) Presence of chlorine atom
(d) High polarity of benzene ring
Answer: (a) Resonance stabilization and sp² hybridization of carbon in C-Cl bond
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Low reactivity of chlorobenzene is due to: (1) Resonance delocalization imparting partial double bond character to C-Cl bond (shorter and stronger, 169 pm vs 177 pm), (2) sp² hybridized aromatic carbon is more electronegative than sp³ carbon, holding bonding electrons tightly, (3) Instability of phenyl cation, and (4) Electrostatic repulsion by π-electron cloud.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
Chloroform (CHCl3) is stored in closed dark brown bottles filled completely to the neck to prevent:
(a) Oxidation by air and light to poisonous phosgene (COCl2)
(b) Evaporation of chloroform
(c) Formation of chloromethane
(d) Reduction of chloroform
Answer: (a) Oxidation by air and light to poisonous phosgene (COCl2)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In the presence of light and atmospheric oxygen, chloroform is slowly oxidized into an extremely poisonous and suffocating gas called phosgene (carbonyl chloride, COCl2): 2 CHCl3 + O2 -(light)-> 2 COCl2 + 2 HCl.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The best reagent for converting ethanol (CH3CH2OH) into chloroethane (CH3CH2Cl) is:
(a) SOCl2 in presence of pyridine
(b) PCl5
(c) PCl3
(d) Conc. HCl + ZnCl2
Answer: (a) SOCl2 in presence of pyridine
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Thionyl chloride (SOCl2) is preferred (Darzens halogenation) because the side products formed, sulfur dioxide (SO2) and hydrogen chloride (HCl), are both gases that escape into the atmosphere, leaving behind virtually pure alkyl chloride without demanding separation steps.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Reaction of chlorobenzene with methyl chloride in the presence of sodium metal and dry ether is an example of:
(a) Wurtz-Fittig reaction
(b) Wurtz reaction
(c) Fittig reaction
(d) Ullmann reaction
Answer: (a) Wurtz-Fittig reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
A mixture of an aryl halide (chlorobenzene) and an alkyl halide (methyl chloride) reacts with metallic sodium in dry ether to form an alkylarene (toluene). This is known as the Wurtz-Fittig reaction: C6H5Cl + 2 Na + CH3Cl -> C6H5CH3 + 2 NaCl.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Which of the following compounds has the highest dipole moment?
(a) CH3Cl
(b) CH3F
(c) CH3Br
(d) CH3I
Answer: (a) CH3Cl
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Dipole moment μ = q × d. Although fluorine is more electronegative than chlorine (q is larger for C-F), the C-Cl bond length (d = 178 pm) is significantly longer than C-F bond length (d = 139 pm). The product of charge and distance is larger for CH3Cl (1.860 D) than for CH3F (1.847 D).
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
The addition of HBr to propene in the presence of benzoyl peroxide gives:
(a) 1-Bromopropane
(b) 2-Bromopropane
(c) 1,2-Dibromopropane
(d) Allyl bromide
Answer: (a) 1-Bromopropane
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In the presence of peroxide, HBr adds to unsymmetrical alkenes contrary to Markovnikov's rule (Kharasch / Peroxide effect) via a free-radical mechanism, yielding 1-bromopropane as the major product: CH3-CH=CH2 + HBr -(peroxide)-> CH3-CH2-CH2-Br.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following haloalkanes will react fastest with aqueous KOH via SN2 mechanism?
(a) CH3-I
(b) CH3-Br
(c) CH3-Cl
(d) CH3-F
Answer: (a) CH3-I
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In SN2 reactions with identical alkyl groups, reaction rate depends on leaving group ability. Iodide ion (I⁻) is the best leaving group because C-I bond has the lowest bond dissociation enthalpy and largest bond length. Reactivity order: R-I > R-Br > R-Cl > R-F.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Preparation of alkyl iodides by the reaction of alkyl chlorides or bromides with sodium iodide in dry acetone is called:
(a) Finkelstein reaction
(b) Swarts reaction
(c) Kolbe's reaction
(d) Reimer-Tiemann reaction
Answer: (a) Finkelstein reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
R-X + NaI -(dry acetone)-> R-I + NaX (where X = Cl, Br). NaCl and NaBr precipitate in dry acetone and are removed, driving the equilibrium forward according to Le Chatelier's principle. This is the Finkelstein reaction.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Reaction of bromobenzene with magnesium metal in dry ether gives:
(a) Phenylmagnesium bromide (Grignard reagent)
(b) Biphenyl
(c) Benzene
(d) Phenol
Answer: (a) Phenylmagnesium bromide (Grignard reagent)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
C6H5Br + Mg -(dry ether)-> C6H5MgBr (phenylmagnesium bromide). This is an organometallic compound known as Grignard reagent.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
The presence of a nitro (-NO2) group at which position(s) drastically accelerates the nucleophilic substitution of chlorobenzene?
(a) Ortho and Para positions only
(b) Meta position only
(c) Meta and Para positions only
(d) All positions equally
Answer: (a) Ortho and Para positions only
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
During nucleophilic aromatic substitution, a carbanion intermediate (Meisenheimer complex) is formed. Resonance structures show that the negative charge delocalizes exclusively onto the ortho and para positions. Strong electron-withdrawing -NO2 groups at these positions stabilize the carbanion directly by resonance, dramatically increasing substitution rate.
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Which of the following compounds has zero dipole moment?
(a) Carbon tetrachloride (CCl4)
(b) Chloroform (CHCl3)
(c) Dichloromethane (CH2Cl2)
(d) Chloromethane (CH3Cl)
Answer: (a) Carbon tetrachloride (CCl4)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
CCl4 has a regular symmetrical tetrahedral geometry with four identical C-Cl bond dipoles pointing toward vertices of a regular tetrahedron. The vector sum of the individual dipoles cancels out completely, resulting in a net dipole moment μ = 0.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Freon-12 (CF2Cl2) is widely manufactured from CCl4 by the reaction with antimony trifluoride in the presence of SbCl5. This is an application of:
(a) Swarts reaction
(b) Finkelstein reaction
(c) Wurtz reaction
(d) Sandmeyer reaction
Answer: (a) Swarts reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
3 CCl4 + 2 SbF3 -(SbCl5 catalyst)-> 3 CCl2F2 (Freon-12) + 2 SbCl3. Fluorination using heavy metal fluorides is a Swarts halogen exchange reaction.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
A racemic mixture is optically inactive because of:
(a) External compensation
(b) Internal compensation
(c) Molecular symmetry
(d) Absence of chiral center
Answer: (a) External compensation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
A racemic mixture contains equimolar (50:50) quantities of dextrorotatory (+) and laevorotatory (-) enantiomers. The optical rotation caused by molecules of one enantiomer is cancelled out exactly by the equal and opposite rotation caused by molecules of the other enantiomer. This cancellation is called external compensation.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Reaction of chlorobenzene with concentrated HNO3 and concentrated H2SO4 yields mainly:
(a) 1-Chloro-4-nitrobenzene (p-chloronitrobenzene)
(b) 1-Chloro-2-nitrobenzene (o-chloronitrobenzene)
(c) 1-Chloro-3-nitrobenzene (m-chloronitrobenzene)
(d) Nitrobenzene
Answer: (a) 1-Chloro-4-nitrobenzene (p-chloronitrobenzene)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Chlorine is ortho/para-directing in electrophilic aromatic substitutions due to +R resonance stabilization. The para-isomer is formed as the major product (~80%) due to minimum steric hindrance compared to the crowded ortho position.

Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following compounds gives a yellow precipitate of CHI3 when treated with I2 and NaOH (Iodoform test)?
(a) Ethanol
(b) Methanol
(c) 1-Propanol
(d) Diethyl ether
Answer: (a) Ethanol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Compounds containing CH3-CH(OH)- group or CH3-C=O group give a positive iodoform test. Ethanol (CH3-CH2-OH) contains the CH3-CH(OH)- unit and is oxidized by I2/NaOH to acetaldehyde, which undergoes iodination and cleavage to form yellow crystalline CHI3 (iodoform).
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Grignard reagents are synthesized and preserved under strictly anhydrous conditions because:
(a) they react with traces of moisture (water) to form hydrocarbons
(b) they decompose into magnesium metal
(c) ether evaporates in moisture
(d) they are soluble in water
Answer: (a) they react with traces of moisture (water) to form hydrocarbons
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The carbon-magnesium bond in Grignard reagents is highly polarized (R^δ⁻ - Mg^δ⁺X). The carbanionic alkyl group acts as a powerful base and readily abstracts a proton even from weakly acidic sources like water: R-MgX + H2O -> R-H + Mg(OH)X, destroying the reagent.

Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
Explain why: (i) Alkyl halides, though polar, are practically immiscible with water. (ii) The dipole moment of chlorobenzene is lower than that of cyclohexyl chloride.
Answer: Explanations of water immiscibility and dipole moment difference
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Immiscibility: Alkyl halides cannot form intermolecular hydrogen bonds with water molecules. The new dipole-dipole attractions formed between alkyl halide and water are far weaker than the strong existing hydrogen bonds among water molecules, making dissolution energetically unfavorable. (ii) Dipole Moment: In chlorobenzene, carbon attached to chlorine is sp² hybridized (more s-character, more electronegative, opposes electron withdrawal by chlorine) and resonance delocalizes chlorine lone pairs towards ring (+R effect). In cyclohexyl chloride, carbon is sp³ hybridized and only electron-withdrawing -I effect operates without resonance opposition.
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Give the mechanism of SN1 and SN2 reactions taking 2-bromobutane and 1-bromobutane as examples respectively.
Answer: Mechanisms of SN1 and SN2 reactions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. SN1 Mechanism (e.g., 2-bromobutane): - Step 1 (Slow, Rate-determining): Ionization of C-Br bond to form a planar carbocation intermediate: CH3-CH(Br)-CH2CH3 -> [CH3-CH⁺-CH2CH3] + Br⁻. - Step 2 (Fast): Nucleophile (OH⁻) attacks the planar carbocation from either face with equal probability, yielding a racemic mixture (~50% retention + ~50% inversion). 2. SN2 Mechanism (e.g., 1-bromobutane): - Single-step concerted mechanism: Incoming nucleophile (OH⁻) attacks C-1 from the backside (180° opposite to Br). - A pentacoordinated transition state [HO···CH2(C3H7)···Br]⁻ is formed where C-OH bond formation and C-Br bond cleavage occur simultaneously. - Bromide departs, yielding alcohol with complete inversion of stereochemical configuration.
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
How do you convert: (i) Propene to 1-iodopropane (ii) Benzene to diphenyl (biphenyl)?
Answer: Conversions with chemical equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Propene to 1-iodopropane: - Step 1: Propene is treated with HBr in presence of peroxide (anti-Markovnikov addition) to give 1-bromopropane: CH3-CH=CH2 + HBr -(peroxide)-> CH3-CH2-CH2-Br. - Step 2: 1-Bromopropane is heated with NaI in dry acetone (Finkelstein reaction): CH3-CH2-CH2-Br + NaI -(dry acetone)-> CH3-CH2-CH2-I + NaBr(s). (ii) Benzene to diphenyl (Fittig reaction): - Step 1: Bromination of benzene using Br2/FeBr3 yields bromobenzene: C6H6 + Br2 -(FeBr3)-> C6H5Br + HBr. - Step 2: Bromobenzene is treated with sodium metal in dry ether: 2 C6H5Br + 2 Na -(dry ether)-> C6H5-C6H5 (diphenyl) + 2 NaBr.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (3 Marks)
Write the major monohalo product in each of the following reactions: (i) CH3-CH2-CH=CH2 + HBr -(peroxide)-> (ii) Cyclohexanol + SOCl2 -> (iii) Toluene + Br2 -(Fe / dark)->
Answer: Major products of 3 organic reactions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) CH3-CH2-CH=CH2 + HBr -(peroxide)-> CH3-CH2-CH2-CH2-Br (1-Bromobutane, anti-Markovnikov addition). (ii) Cyclohexanol + SOCl2 -> Chlorocyclohexane + SO2(g) + HCl(g) (Darzens halogenation). (iii) Toluene + Br2 -(Fe/dark)-> 4-Bromotoluene (p-bromotoluene, major product due to less steric hindrance) + 2-bromotoluene (minor).
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (2 Marks)
Explain why chlorobenzene is resistant to nucleophilic substitution, but 2,4,6-trinitrochlorobenzene is readily hydrolyzed with warm water alone.
Answer: Effect of nitro groups on nucleophilic aromatic substitution
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Chlorobenzene: The C-Cl bond possesses partial double bond character due to resonance (+R effect). Furthermore, the carbanion intermediate formed upon nucleophilic attack is unstable in the absence of electron-withdrawing groups. 2. 2,4,6-Trinitrochlorobenzene (Picryl chloride): The three powerful electron-withdrawing nitro groups (-NO2) at ortho and para positions exert immense -M and -I effects, drastically decreasing electron density on the benzene ring and strongly stabilizing the carbanionic Meisenheimer intermediate by dispersing negative charge onto the electronegative oxygen atoms. As a result, C-Cl bond is cleaved extremely easily by warm water.
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (3 Marks)
State Saytzeff's rule. Give the elimination reaction of 2-bromopentane with alcoholic KOH, naming all products and indicating the major product.
Answer: Saytzeff's rule and dehydrohalogenation of 2-bromopentane
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Saytzeff's Rule: In dehydrohalogenation reactions of alkyl halides, the preferred alkene is the one which has the greater number of alkyl substituents attached to the doubly bonded carbon atoms (more highly substituted alkene). 2. Reaction of 2-bromopentane with alc. KOH: CH3-CH2-CH2-CH(Br)-CH3 + alc. KOH -(heat)-> - Major Product (~81%): Pent-2-ene (CH3-CH2-CH=CH-CH3) - disubstituted alkene. - Minor Product (~19%): Pent-1-ene (CH3-CH2-CH2-CH=CH2) - monosubstituted alkene.
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (2 Marks)
What is meant by: (i) Chiral carbon atom (ii) Enantiomers?
Answer: Definitions of chiral carbon and enantiomers
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Chiral (Asymmetric) Carbon: An sp³ hybridized carbon atom bonded to four completely different atoms or groups of atoms. A molecule containing a single chiral carbon lacks an alternating axis of symmetry and is non-superimposable on its mirror image. (ii) Enantiomers: Stereoisomers that are non-superimposable mirror images of each other. Enantiomers possess identical physical properties (boiling point, melting point, refractive index) except that they rotate plane-polarized light in equal magnitudes but opposite directions.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (3 Marks)
Write chemical equations for the following named reactions: (i) Sandmeyer reaction (ii) Finkelstein reaction (iii) Wurtz reaction
Answer: Equations for named organic reactions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Sandmeyer Reaction: Benzene diazonium chloride reacts with Cu2Cl2/HCl (or Cu2Br2/HBr) to form chlorobenzene (or bromobenzene): C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ -(Cu2Cl2/HCl)-> C6H5Cl + N2(g). (ii) Finkelstein Reaction: Alkyl chloride/bromide reacts with NaI in dry acetone to form alkyl iodide: CH3-CH2-Br + NaI -(dry acetone)-> CH3-CH2-I + NaBr(s). (iii) Wurtz Reaction: Two molecules of alkyl halide react with sodium metal in dry ether to form a symmetrical alkane: 2 CH3-Br + 2 Na -(dry ether)-> CH3-CH3 (ethane) + 2 NaBr.
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (2 Marks)
Which alkyl halide in each pair would you expect to react faster in an SN2 reaction and why? (i) 1-Bromobutane or 2-Bromobutane (ii) 1-Chlorobutane or 1-Iodobutane
Answer: SN2 reactivity predictions with justifications
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) 1-Bromobutane reacts faster than 2-bromobutane because 1-bromobutane is a primary (1°) halide with minimal steric hindrance at the reactive carbon center, allowing unobstructed backside approach of the nucleophile. 2-Bromobutane is a secondary (2°) halide with greater steric congestion. (ii) 1-Iodobutane reacts faster than 1-chlorobutane because iodide ion (I⁻) is a much better leaving group than chloride (Cl⁻) due to longer C-I bond length and lower C-I bond dissociation energy.
