Answer: IUPAC names of haloalkanes
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) CH3-CH(Cl)-CH(CH3)-CH3: Numbering from left gives lower locants (2,3). Name: 2-Chloro-3-methylbutane. (ii) (CCl3)3C-Cl: Longest continuous carbon chain has 3 carbons (propane). C-2 is bonded to a chloro group and three trichloromethyl groups, giving: 2-(Trichloromethyl)-1,1,1,2,3,3,3-heptachloropropane.
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' having molecular formula C4H9Br on reaction with alcoholic KOH yields compound 'B'. Compound 'B' on ozonolysis gives only ethanal. Identify compounds 'A' and 'B', write all reactions involved, and explain the mechanism of elimination. (b) Why does primary alkyl halide prefer SN2 while tertiary alkyl halide prefers SN1 mechanism?
Answer: Identification of A = 2-bromobutane, B = but-2-ene, and SN1 vs SN2 mechanistic comparison
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3 marks): - Ozonolysis product is ethanal (CH3CHO): Two molecules of ethanal indicate that compound 'B' is But-2-ene (CH3-CH=CH-CH3): CH3-CH=CH-CH3 + O3 -> Ozonide -(Zn/H2O)-> 2 CH3CHO. - Since 'B' is But-2-ene formed by dehydrohalogenation of C4H9Br with alc. KOH, compound 'A' must be 2-Bromobutane: CH3-CH2-CH(Br)-CH3 (A) + alc. KOH -(heat)-> CH3-CH=CH-CH3 (B, But-2-ene, major) + KBr + H2O. - Mechanism: It follows E2 (bimolecular elimination) where base (OH⁻) removes a proton from β-carbon while Br⁻ leaves from α-carbon simultaneously, driven by Saytzeff orientation. (b) SN1 vs SN2 Preference (2 marks): - 1° Alkyl Halides prefer SN2 because the primary carbon has only one alkyl substituent, minimizing steric hindrance and allowing easy backside attack by incoming nucleophile. Conversely, 1° carbocations are extremely unstable, making SN1 ionization energetically prohibitive. - 3° Alkyl Halides prefer SN1 because three bulky alkyl groups sterically shield the carbon center, blocking backside nucleophilic approach (disfavoring SN2). Meanwhile, ionization generates a tertiary carbocation that is exceptionally stabilized by hyperconjugation and +I inductive effects.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) Discuss the electrophilic substitution reactions of chlorobenzene: halogenation, nitration, sulfonation, and Friedel-Crafts alkylation. Why is the halogen atom ortho/para-directing but deactivating? (b) How will you convert: (i) Chlorobenzene to phenol (ii) Ethyl chloride to propanoic acid?
Answer: Electrophilic substitutions of chlorobenzene and chemical conversions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Electrophilic Substitutions and Directing Nature (3.5 marks): 1. Ortho/Para Directing but Deactivating: - Halogen has lone pairs that participate in resonance with the π-system of benzene (+R effect), increasing electron density specifically at ortho and para positions compared to meta positions. - However, halogen is strongly electronegative and withdraws electron density via inductive effect (-I effect), deactivating the ring overall relative to benzene. - Since inductive effect dominates resonance in deactivation, halogen deactivates the ring, but orientation is governed by resonance (+R), directing electrophiles to ortho and para positions. 2. Reactions: - Halogenation: Chlorobenzene + Cl2 -(anhyd. FeCl3)-> 1,4-Dichlorobenzene (p-major) + 1,2-dichlorobenzene (o-minor). - Nitration: Chlorobenzene + conc. HNO3/H2SO4 -> 1-Chloro-4-nitrobenzene (p-major) + 1-chloro-2-nitrobenzene (o-minor). - Sulfonation: Chlorobenzene + conc. H2SO4 -(heat)-> 4-Chlorobenzenesulfonic acid (p-major). - Friedel-Crafts Alkylation: Chlorobenzene + CH3Cl -(anhyd. AlCl3)-> 1-Chloro-4-methylbenzene (p-chlorotoluene, major). (b) Conversions (1.5 marks): (i) Chlorobenzene to Phenol (Dow's Process): C6H5Cl + 2 NaOH -(623 K, 300 atm)-> C6H5ONa -(H⁺)-> C6H5OH (Phenol). (ii) Ethyl chloride to Propanoic acid: CH3CH2Cl + alc. KCN -> CH3CH2CN -(H3O⁺/heat)-> CH3CH2COOH.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) What are polyhalogen compounds? Write short notes on the environmental and health impacts of: (i) Chloroform (CHCl3) (ii) Carbon tetrachloride (CCl4) (iii) Freons (CFCs) (iv) DDT. (b) Write the structural formula and IUPAC name of DDT.
Answer: Detailed account of polyhalogen compounds and DDT structure
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Polyhalogen Compounds and Impacts (3.5 marks): - Definition: Carbon compounds containing more than one halogen atom. (i) Chloroform (CHCl3): Inhaling chloroform depresses the central nervous system (anesthetic). Chronic exposure damages liver and kidneys. Oxidizes in light and air to form lethal phosgene gas (COCl2). (ii) Carbon tetrachloride (CCl4): Highly toxic liver poison (carcinogen). In atmosphere, it depletes the ozone layer, leading to increased UV-B radiation exposure. (iii) Freons (CFCs, e.g. CF2Cl2): Extremely stable, volatile chlorofluorocarbons used as refrigerants and propellants. They diffuse into the stratosphere where UV radiation breaks C-Cl bonds, releasing chlorine free radicals (Cl•) that catalytically destroy the stratospheric ozone layer. (iv) DDT (p,p'-Dichlorodiphenyltrichloroethane): Extremely persistent organochlorine pesticide. It is non-biodegradable, highly lipid-soluble, and undergoes biomagnification along food chains, disrupting calcium metabolism in birds and thinning eggshells. (b) Structural Formula and IUPAC Name of DDT (1.5 marks): - Structure: (p-Cl-C6H4)2-CH-CCl3. - IUPAC Name: 1,1,1-Trichloro-2,2-bis(4-chlorophenyl)ethane.
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Nucleophilic Substitution in Alkyl Halides Nucleophilic substitution reactions of alkyl halides occur by two distinct pathways: SN1 (unimolecular) and SN2 (bimolecular). The choice of pathway depends crucially on the structure of the substrate, the nature of the leaving group, the nucleophile, and the solvent polarity. In SN2 reactions, rate = k [RX] [Nu⁻]; the process is concerted and results in complete inversion of configuration (Walden inversion). In SN1 reactions, rate = k [RX]; ionization produces a planar carbocation that reacts rapidly with nucleophiles to yield racemic mixtures. Bulky alkyl substituents disfavor SN2 by steric shielding but favor SN1 by carbocation stabilization. (i) Which compound undergoes SN1 faster: 1-bromobutane or 2-bromobutane? Why? (ii) Predict the stereochemical outcome of an SN2 reaction on an optically active alkyl halide. (iii) Why are allylic and benzylic halides exceptionally reactive towards SN1 substitution? (iv) What is the effect of changing solvent from water to acetone on the rate of an SN2 reaction?
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Nucleophilic Substitution Kinetics and Stereochemistry
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) 2-Bromobutane reacts faster via SN1 because it forms a secondary carbocation (CH3-CH⁺-CH2CH3) which is more stable than the primary carbocation formed by 1-bromobutane. (ii) Complete inversion of stereochemical configuration (Walden inversion). (iii) Allylic and benzylic halides ionize to form allyl (CH2=CH-CH2⁺) and benzyl (C6H5CH2⁺) carbocations, which are exceptionally stable due to resonance delocalization of positive charge across the π-system. (iv) Acetone is a polar aprotic solvent that does not solvate or encapsulate nucleophiles via hydrogen bonding. This leaves the nucleophile 'naked' and highly reactive, dramatically accelerating the rate of SN2 reactions.
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) An alkyl halide C5H11Br (A) reacts with ethanolic KOH to give an alkene (B), which on ozonolysis gives a mixture of propan-2-one and ethanal. Deduce the structures of (A) and (B) and write all reactions. (b) Give reasons: (i) Alkyl halides give cyanides with KCN but isocyanides with AgCN. (ii) Haloarenes are insoluble in water.
Answer: Deduction of C5H11Br, equations, and chemical explanations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Deduction of Structures (3 marks): - Ozonolysis products are propan-2-one (acetone, (CH3)2C=O) and ethanal (CH3CHO). - Joining the carbonyl fragments with a double bond gives alkene 'B': (CH3)2C=CH-CH3 (2-Methylbut-2-ene). - Since alkene (B) is formed by dehydrohalogenation of C5H11Br (A) using ethanolic KOH, by Saytzeff's rule compound 'A' must be 2-Bromo-2-methylbutane: (CH3)2C(Br)-CH2-CH3 (A). Reaction: (CH3)2C(Br)-CH2-CH3 + alc. KOH -(heat)-> (CH3)2C=CH-CH3 (B) + KBr + H2O. Ozonolysis: (CH3)2C=CH-CH3 + O3 -(Zn/H2O)-> (CH3)2C=O + CH3CHO. (b) Reasons (2 marks): (i) KCN is predominantly ionic; the cyanide ion (:C≡N:⁻) is an ambidentate nucleophile. Attack via carbon occurs because C-C bond (348 kJ/mol) is more stable than C-N bond (305 kJ/mol), forming alkyl cyanide. AgCN is covalent; only the lone pair on nitrogen is available for attack, forming alkyl isocyanide (R-NC). (ii) Haloarenes cannot form hydrogen bonds with water molecules, nor can they overcome the existing strong hydrogen-bonding network between water molecules, rendering them insoluble in water.
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Write short notes on: (i) Wurtz-Fittig reaction (ii) Fittig reaction (iii) Ullmann reaction. (b) What happens when: (i) Ethyl bromide is treated with silver nitrite (AgNO2)? (ii) Methyl chloride is treated with sodium ethoxide (C2H5ONa)?
Answer: Coupling reactions and chemical equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Named Coupling Reactions (3 marks): (i) Wurtz-Fittig Reaction: An aryl halide reacts with an alkyl halide in the presence of sodium metal in dry ether to form an alkylarene: C6H5Br + 2 Na + CH3Br -(dry ether)-> C6H5CH3 (toluene) + 2 NaBr. (ii) Fittig Reaction: Two molecules of aryl halide react with sodium metal in dry ether to form a diaryl (biphenyl): 2 C6H5Br + 2 Na -(dry ether)-> C6H5-C6H5 (diphenyl) + 2 NaBr. (iii) Ullmann Reaction: Heating an iodobenzene with copper powder in a sealed tube yields biphenyl: 2 C6H5I + Cu -(heat)-> C6H5-C6H5 + CuI2. (b) Reactions (2 marks): (i) With AgNO2: Silver nitrite is predominantly covalent. Nitrogen lone pair attacks the ethyl group to give nitroethane: CH3CH2Br + AgNO2 -> CH3CH2NO2 (nitroethane) + AgBr(s). (ii) With C2H5ONa (Williamson Ether Synthesis): CH3Cl + C2H5O⁻Na⁺ -> CH3-O-C2H5 (methoxyethane / ethyl methyl ether) + NaCl.
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
Arrange each set of compounds in order of increasing property indicated: (i) 1-Chloropropane, 2-chloropropane, 1-chlorobutane (boiling points) (ii) 1-Bromobutane, 1-bromobut-2-ene, 1-bromo-2-methylpropane (reactivity towards SN1) (iii) CH3Cl, CH3Br, CH3I, CH3F (reactivity towards SN2) (iv) Chlorobenzene, p-nitrochlorobenzene, 2,4-dinitrochlorobenzene (reactivity towards nucleophilic substitution).
Answer: Four property arrangements with chemical reasoning
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Boiling Points: 2-Chloropropane < 1-Chloropropane < 1-Chlorobutane. Reason: Higher molecular mass increases van der Waals forces (1-chlorobutane > chloropropanes); for isomeric chloropropanes, straight-chain 1-chloropropane has larger surface area than branched 2-chloropropane. (ii) Reactivity towards SN1: 1-Bromobutane < 1-Bromo-2-methylpropane < 1-Bromobut-2-ene. Reason: SN1 rate depends on carbocation stability. 1-Bromobut-2-ene forms resonance-stabilized allylic carbocation (CH3-CH=CH-CH2⁺ ↔ CH3-CH⁺-CH=CH2). (iii) Reactivity towards SN2: CH3F < CH3Cl < CH3Br < CH3I. Reason: Leaving group ability increases down the halogen group (I⁻ > Br⁻ > Cl⁻ > F⁻) due to lower bond dissociation energy. (iv) Nucleophilic Substitution: Chlorobenzene < p-Nitrochlorobenzene < 2,4-Dinitrochlorobenzene. Reason: Strongly electron-withdrawing -NO2 groups at ortho/para positions stabilize the carbanion Meisenheimer intermediate.
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) Define optical activity. Explain the terms: plane-polarized light, dextrorotatory, and laevorotatory. (b) What is a racemic mixture? Why is it optically inactive? (c) Draw the structures of optical isomers (enantiomers) of lactic acid (2-hydroxypropanoic acid).
Answer: Optical activity concepts, racemic mixture, and lactic acid enantiomers
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Optical Activity Definitions (2 marks): - Optical Activity: The ability of certain chiral organic substances to rotate the plane of polarized light when a beam passes through their solution. - Plane-Polarized Light: Light whose electric field oscillations are confined strictly to a single plane. - Dextrorotatory (+ or d): A substance that rotates the plane of polarized light in a clockwise direction. - Laevorotatory (- or l): A substance that rotates the plane of polarized light in an anticlockwise direction. (b) Racemic Mixture (1.5 marks): - An equimolar (1:1) mixture of dextrorotatory and laevorotatory enantiomers of a compound is called a racemic modification (or racemic mixture), designated as (±) or dl. - It is optically inactive due to external compensation: the optical rotation caused by the molecules of one enantiomer is cancelled out completely by the equal and opposite optical rotation produced by the molecules of the other enantiomer. (c) Enantiomers of Lactic Acid (1.5 marks): - Lactic acid: CH3-*CH(OH)-COOH. Chiral center at C-2. - Enantiomer 1 (+)-lactic acid: COOH at top, CH3 at bottom, -OH on right, -H on left. - Enantiomer 2 (-)-lactic acid: Non-superimposable mirror image with -OH on left, -H on right.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
How will you carry out the following conversions: (i) 1-Chloropropane to 2-chloropropane (ii) Isopropyl alcohol to iodoform (iii) Chlorobenzene to p-nitrophenol (iv) 2-Bromopropane to 1-bromopropane?
Answer: Four multistep organic conversions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) 1-Chloropropane to 2-chloropropane: CH3CH2CH2Cl + alc. KOH -(heat)-> CH3-CH=CH2 + KCl + H2O. CH3-CH=CH2 + HCl -> CH3-CH(Cl)-CH3 (Markovnikov addition). (ii) Isopropyl alcohol to iodoform: CH3-CH(OH)-CH3 + 4 I2 + 6 NaOH -(warm)-> CHI3 (yellow crystals of iodoform) + CH3COONa + 5 NaI + 5 H2O. (iii) Chlorobenzene to p-nitrophenol: C6H5Cl + conc. HNO3/H2SO4 -> p-Chloronitrobenzene (major). p-Chloronitrobenzene + aq. NaOH -(443 K)-> Sodium p-nitrophenoxide -(dil. HCl)-> p-Nitrophenol. (iv) 2-Bromopropane to 1-bromopropane: CH3-CH(Br)-CH3 + alc. KOH -(heat)-> CH3-CH=CH2 + KBr + H2O. CH3-CH=CH2 + HBr -(peroxide)-> CH3-CH2-CH2-Br (anti-Markovnikov addition).
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) Discuss the mechanism of nucleophilic aromatic substitution in 1-chloro-4-nitrobenzene with aqueous NaOH. (b) Explain why a nitro group at meta position has practically no effect on the reactivity of chlorobenzene towards nucleophiles. (c) What happens when chlorobenzene is treated with chlorine in the presence of anhydrous FeCl3?
Answer: Mechanism of nucleophilic aromatic substitution, meta-nitro effect, and chlorination
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Mechanism in 1-Chloro-4-nitrobenzene (2.5 marks): - Step 1 (Slow, Rate-determining step): Hydroxide ion (OH⁻) attacks C-1 carrying the chlorine atom, generating a resonance-stabilized carbanionic σ-complex (Meisenheimer complex). The negative charge delocalizes over the ortho and para positions of the ring and directly onto the oxygen atoms of the para-nitro group: [-O-N⁺(=O)=C6H4(Cl)(OH)]⁻. - Step 2 (Fast): Aromaticity is regenerated by the rapid expulsion of the chloride leaving group (Cl⁻), yielding 4-nitrophenol (p-nitrophenol). (b) Why Meta-Nitro has negligible effect (1.5 marks): - When nucleophile attacks at C-1, negative charge appears on carbons ortho and para to the site of attack (positions 2, 4, and 6). - If -NO2 is at meta position (position 3 or 5), none of the resonance contributors places the negative charge on the carbon bearing the nitro group. Consequently, the nitro group cannot disperse negative charge by its powerful +M resonance effect, leaving the carbanion unstabilized by resonance. (c) Chlorination of Chlorobenzene (1 mark): C6H5Cl + Cl2 -(anhyd. FeCl3)-> 1,4-Dichlorobenzene (p-isomer, major product) + 1,2-Dichlorobenzene (o-isomer, minor product).
Organic Chemistry
Chapter 7: Alcohols, Phenols and Ethers
6 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following compounds gives a white precipitate of 2,4,6-tribromophenol with bromine water?
(a) Phenol
(b) Anisole
(c) Benzoic acid
(d) Benzyl alcohol
Answer: (a) Phenol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In aqueous solution, phenol dissociates into phenoxide ion which strongly activates the benzene ring towards electrophilic attack. Treatment with bromine water results in polyhalogenation, forming a white precipitate of 2,4,6-tribromophenol.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Lucas reagent is a mixture of:
(a) Anhydrous ZnCl2 and concentrated HCl
(b) Hydrated ZnCl2 and dilute HCl
(c) Pyridine and SOCl2
(d) Anhydrous AlCl3 and concentrated HCl
Answer: (a) Anhydrous ZnCl2 and concentrated HCl
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Lucas reagent is an equimolar mixture of anhydrous zinc chloride (ZnCl2) and concentrated hydrochloric acid (conc. HCl). It is used to distinguish between primary, secondary, and tertiary alcohols based on the rate of turbidity formation (alkyl chloride precipitation).
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The major product formed in the reaction of tert-butyl bromide with sodium methoxide is:
(a) 2-Methylpropene (Isobutylene)
(b) tert-Butyl methyl ether
(c) 2-Methylpropan-2-ol
(d) But-2-ene
Answer: (a) 2-Methylpropene (Isobutylene)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Sodium methoxide (CH3ONa) is a strong base as well as a nucleophile. When reacted with a bulky tertiary alkyl halide ((CH3)3C-Br), steric hindrance suppresses SN2 substitution and elimination (E2) predominates, yielding 2-methylpropene (isobutylene) as the major product.
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Ortho-nitrophenol is more steam-volatile than para-nitrophenol because of:
(a) Intramolecular hydrogen bonding in o-nitrophenol
(b) Intermolecular hydrogen bonding in o-nitrophenol
(c) Higher molecular weight of p-nitrophenol
(d) Higher dipole moment of o-nitrophenol
Answer: (a) Intramolecular hydrogen bonding in o-nitrophenol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
o-Nitrophenol forms an intramolecular hydrogen bond (chelation) between the phenolic -OH and the ortho -NO2 group. This prevents association with other molecules, lowering its boiling point and making it steam-volatile. p-Nitrophenol forms extensive intermolecular hydrogen bonds, leading to molecular association, higher boiling point, and non-volatility.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The industrial preparation of phenol from cumene involves oxidation of cumene to cumene hydroperoxide followed by treatment with dilute acid. The valuable by-product obtained is:
(a) Acetone
(b) Acetaldehyde
(c) Ethanol
(d) Formaldehyde
Answer: (a) Acetone
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cumene (isopropylbenzene) is oxidized by air to cumene hydroperoxide, which on acid-catalyzed rearrangement (dil. H2SO4) cleaves quantitatively into phenol and propan-2-one (acetone): C6H5CH(CH3)2 + O2 -> C6H5C(CH3)2(OOH) -(H⁺/H2O)-> C6H5OH + CH3COCH3.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which of the following alcohols gives an alkene upon heating with copper at 573 K instead of a carbonyl compound?
(a) 2-Methylpropan-2-ol (tert-butyl alcohol)
(b) Propan-2-ol
(c) Ethanol
(d) Butan-1-ol
Answer: (a) 2-Methylpropan-2-ol (tert-butyl alcohol)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Primary alcohols undergo catalytic dehydrogenation over Cu at 573 K to give aldehydes; secondary alcohols give ketones. Tertiary alcohols lack an α-hydrogen atom and therefore undergo catalytic dehydration over Cu at 573 K to form an alkene (2-methylpropene).
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Phenol reacts with chloroform in the presence of aqueous sodium hydroxide at 340 K to form salicylaldehyde. This reaction is known as:
(a) Reimer-Tiemann reaction
(b) Kolbe's reaction
(c) Cannizzaro reaction
(d) Rosenmund reduction
Answer: (a) Reimer-Tiemann reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The Reimer-Tiemann reaction involves electrophilic attack of dichlorocarbene (:CCl2), generated in situ from CHCl3 and NaOH, on the electron-rich phenoxide ion, followed by alkaline hydrolysis to form 2-hydroxybenzaldehyde (salicylaldehyde).
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
The reaction of anisole with concentrated HI at 373 K yields:
(a) Phenol and methyl iodide
(b) Iodobenzene and methanol
(c) Phenol and methanol
(d) Iodobenzene and methyl iodide
Answer: (a) Phenol and methyl iodide
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In methyl phenyl ether (anisole), the phenyl-oxygen bond has partial double bond character due to resonance and is much stronger than the alkyl C-O bond. Protonation gives [C6H5-O⁺(H)-CH3]; iodide ion (I⁻) attacks the less hindered methyl group via SN2, cleaving the alkyl-oxygen bond to yield phenol and methyl iodide (CH3I).
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
The acid-catalyzed dehydration of ethanol at 413 K produces diethyl ether, whereas at 443 K it produces:
(a) Ethene
(b) Ethanal
(c) Ethanoic acid
(d) Butane
Answer: (a) Ethene
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
At 413 K (140 °C), ethanol reacts with concentrated H2SO4 via bimolecular nucleophilic substitution (SN2) to form diethyl ether: 2 C2H5OH -> C2H5OC2H5 + H2O. At 443 K (170 °C), unimolecular elimination (E1) takes over, yielding ethene: C2H5OH -> CH2=CH2 + H2O.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Which of the following compounds is the most acidic?
(a) 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol (Picric acid)
(b) 4-Nitrophenol
(c) Phenol
(d) 4-Methoxyphenol
Answer: (a) 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol (Picric acid)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The three strong electron-withdrawing nitro groups (-NO2) at positions 2, 4, and 6 exert massive -M and -I effects, stabilizing the conjugate base (picrate anion) extensively across multiple oxygen atoms. With a pKa of ~0.7, picric acid is even stronger than many carboxylic acids.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Aspirin is chemically known as:
(a) Acetylsalicylic acid
(b) Methyl salicylate
(c) Phenyl salicylate
(d) Salicylaldehyde
Answer: (a) Acetylsalicylic acid
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Acetylation of the phenolic -OH group of salicylic acid using acetic anhydride in the presence of an acid catalyst produces acetylsalicylic acid, commonly known as Aspirin.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Hydroboration-oxidation of propene with B2H6 followed by alkaline H2O2 yields:
(a) Propan-1-ol
(b) Propan-2-ol
(c) Propane-1,2-diol
(d) Propanone
Answer: (a) Propan-1-ol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Hydroboration-oxidation yields anti-Markovnikov hydration of alkenes without carbocation rearrangement. Boron attaches to the less hindered terminal carbon atom; alkaline H2O2 oxidation replaces boron with an -OH group, producing Propan-1-ol.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Phenol reacts with neutral ferric chloride (FeCl3) solution to give a characteristic:
(a) Violet coloration
(b) Blue precipitate
(c) Yellow solution
(d) Red gas
Answer: (a) Violet coloration
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Phenols react with neutral aqueous FeCl3 solution to form a soluble coordination complex [Fe(OC6H5)6]³⁻, giving an intense violet (or blue-violet) coloration. This serves as a diagnostic test distinguishing phenols from aliphatic alcohols.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
In the reaction of tert-butyl methyl ether with one equivalent of HI, the products are:
(a) tert-Butyl iodide and methanol
(b) tert-Butyl alcohol and methyl iodide
(c) Isobutylene and methanol
(d) Methyl iodide and water
Answer: (a) tert-Butyl iodide and methanol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
When an ether has a tertiary alkyl group, protonation forms (CH3)3C-O⁺(H)-CH3. Cleavage proceeds via an SN1 pathway because the tertiary carbocation ((CH3)3C⁺) formed is exceptionally stable. Iodide ion attacks the carbocation to form tert-butyl iodide ((CH3)3C-I), and methanol (CH3OH) is released.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which reagent is used to selectively oxidize primary alcohols to aldehydes without over-oxidation to carboxylic acids?
(a) Pyridinium chlorochromate (PCC)
(b) Acidified KMnO4
(c) Acidified K2Cr2O7
(d) Jones reagent (CrO3 in H2SO4)
Answer: (a) Pyridinium chlorochromate (PCC)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
PCC (Corey's reagent: C5H5NH⁺ ClCrO3⁻ in CH2Cl2) is a mild, non-aqueous oxidizing agent that stops the oxidation of primary alcohols cleanly at the aldehyde stage without oxidizing them to carboxylic acids.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
When phenol is heated with zinc dust, it is reduced to:
(a) Benzene
(b) Toluene
(c) Benzoic acid
(d) Cyclohexane

Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Distillation of phenol with zinc dust reduces the phenolic hydroxyl group: C6H5OH + Zn -(heat)-> C6H5-H (Benzene) + ZnO.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Kolbe's reaction of sodium phenoxide with CO2 at 400 K and 4-7 atm pressure followed by acidification yields:
(a) Salicylic acid (2-hydroxybenzoic acid)
(b) Benzoic acid
(c) Salicylaldehyde
(d) Phthalic acid
Answer: (a) Salicylic acid (2-hydroxybenzoic acid)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Sodium phenoxide reacts with weak electrophile carbon dioxide (CO2) at 400 K and 4-7 atm to form sodium salicylate, which on acidification yields salicylic acid (2-hydroxybenzoic acid).
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Which of the following compounds does NOT react with Lucas reagent at room temperature even after 30 minutes?
(a) Butan-1-ol
(b) Butan-2-ol
(c) 2-Methylpropan-2-ol
(d) Pentan-2-ol
Answer: (a) Butan-1-ol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Butan-1-ol is a primary (1°) alcohol. Primary carbocations are unstable, so primary alcohols do not react with Lucas reagent at room temperature; turbidity appears only upon vigorous heating.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
The boiling point of ethanol (78 °C) is much higher than that of dimethyl ether (-24 °C), although both have the same molecular formula C2H6O, because:
(a) Ethanol molecules associate via intermolecular hydrogen bonding
(b) Ethanol has higher molecular weight
(c) Dimethyl ether is ionic
(d) Ethanol has non-polar bonding
Answer: (a) Ethanol molecules associate via intermolecular hydrogen bonding
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In ethanol (CH3CH2OH), hydrogen is bonded directly to highly electronegative oxygen, establishing strong intermolecular hydrogen bonding. Dimethyl ether (CH3-O-CH3) has oxygen bonded only to carbon atoms and cannot form intermolecular hydrogen bonds.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017

(a) Benzoquinone
(b) Hydroquinone
(c) Catechol
(d) Resorcinol
Answer: (a) Benzoquinone
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Oxidation of phenol with chromic acid / sodium dichromate produces a conjugated diketone called p-benzoquinone (or 1,4-benzoquinone).
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
The Williamson ether synthesis involves which mechanistic pathway?
(a) SN2
(b) SN1
(c) E2
(d) Electrophilic addition
Answer: (a) SN2
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Williamson synthesis involves the nucleophilic attack of an alkoxide ion (R-O⁻) on an unhindered primary alkyl halide (R'-X) via an SN2 displacement of halide ion.
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
The IUPAC name of the compound CH3-CH(OH)-CH2-O-CH3 is:
(a) 1-Methoxypropan-2-ol
(b) 3-Methoxypropan-2-ol
(c) 2-Hydroxypropyl methyl ether
(d) 1-Methoxypropan-1-ol
Answer: (a) 1-Methoxypropan-2-ol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The principal functional group is the -OH group (alcohol suffix '-ol'), which takes priority over the ether alkoxy prefix. Numbering from right gives C-1 to methoxy-bearing carbon and C-2 to hydroxyl: 1-Methoxypropan-2-ol.
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following ethers cannot be prepared by Williamson's synthesis?
(a) Diphenyl ether
(b) Diethyl ether
(c) Ethyl methyl ether
(d) tert-Butyl ethyl ether
Answer: (a) Diphenyl ether
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Preparation of diphenyl ether would require an SN2 attack of phenoxide ion on an aryl halide (bromobenzene or chlorobenzene). Haloarenes are unreactive towards SN2 nucleophilic substitution due to partial double bond character of the C-X bond and steric/electronic repulsion of the aromatic ring.
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Phenol reacts with dilute nitric acid (HNO3) at 298 K to give:
(a) A mixture of ortho- and para-nitrophenol
(b) 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol
(c) meta-Nitrophenol only
(d) Nitrobenzene
Answer: (a) A mixture of ortho- and para-nitrophenol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
With dilute HNO3 at low temperature (298 K), phenol is nitrated at activated ortho and para positions, giving a mixture of o-nitrophenol (~30-40%) and p-nitrophenol (~15-20%), which are separable by steam distillation.
Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1

Answer: (a) 2-Methylpropan-2-ol

2-Methylpropan-2-ol is a tertiary alcohol. It reacts immediately at room temperature with Lucas reagent via an SN1 pathway to produce an insoluble layer of tert-butyl chloride, creating immediate cloudiness/turbidity.

(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.

(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.

Q29. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Assertion (A): Anisole on reaction with HI gives phenol and methyl iodide, not iodobenzene and methanol. Reason (R): In anisole, the C-O bond attached to the benzene ring has partial double bond character due to resonance and is stronger than the alkyl C-O bond.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R correctly explains why alkyl-oxygen bond cleaves rather than aryl-oxygen bond.
Q30. Assertion-Reason
CBSE 2020
Assertion (A): The boiling points of alcohols are higher than those of ethers of comparable molecular masses. Reason (R): Alcohols undergo extensive intermolecular hydrogen bonding, whereas ethers cannot form intermolecular hydrogen bonds among themselves.
(a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
(b) Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A.
(c) A is true but R is false.
(d) A is false but R is true.
Answer: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A.
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Both A and R are true and R explains A directly.
Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
Give chemical tests to distinguish between: (i) Phenol and Ethanol (ii) Propan-1-ol and Propan-2-ol.
Answer: Distinguishing chemical tests
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Phenol and Ethanol: Add neutral FeCl3 solution. - Phenol gives an intense violet coloration due to complex formation [Fe(OC6H5)6]³⁻. - Ethanol produces no coloration with neutral FeCl3. (Alternatively: Phenol gives white ppt with bromine water, ethanol does not). (ii) Propan-1-ol and Propan-2-ol: Iodoform Test. - Propan-2-ol (CH3-CH(OH)-CH3) has the CH3-CH(OH)- group; on warming with I2 and aqueous NaOH, it gives a yellow crystalline precipitate of iodoform (CHI3) with a characteristic medicinal smell. - Propan-1-ol does not give a yellow precipitate with I2/NaOH.
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Write the mechanism of acid-catalyzed dehydration of ethanol to yield ethene at 443 K.
Answer: Three-step mechanism of alcohol dehydration
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Step 1: Protonation of alcohol to form oxonium ion (fast): CH3-CH2-OH + H⁺ ⇌ CH3-CH2-O⁺H2. Step 2: Formation of carbocation (Slow, Rate-determining step): CH3-CH2-O⁺H2 -> [CH3-CH2⁺] (ethyl carbocation) + H2O. Step 3: Elimination of a proton to form ethene (fast): [CH3-CH2⁺] -> CH2=CH2 (ethene) + H⁺. The acid catalyst (H⁺) is regenerated.
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
Explain Williamson ether synthesis. Why cannot tert-butyl ethyl ether be prepared by treating tert-butyl bromide with sodium ethoxide?
Answer: Williamson synthesis and limitation with tertiary halides
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Williamson Ether Synthesis: An SN2 reaction where an alkoxide ion displaces a halide ion from an unhindered primary alkyl halide: R-ONa + R'-X -> R-O-R' + NaX. 2. Why it fails with tert-butyl bromide: Alkoxides are not only good nucleophiles but also strong Brønsted bases. With tertiary alkyl halides like (CH3)3C-Br, steric crowding completely blocks backside SN2 attack. Instead, the ethoxide base abstracts a β-proton, leading exclusively to E2 elimination to yield 2-methylpropene (isobutylene) rather than ether.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (3 Marks)
Write balanced chemical equations for: (i) Kolbe's reaction (ii) Reimer-Tiemann reaction (iii) Nitration of phenol with concentrated HNO3.
Answer: Three balanced organic equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Kolbe's Reaction: C6H5OH + NaOH -> C6H5ONa -(CO2, 400 K, 4-7 atm)-> Sodium salicylate -(H⁺)-> Salicylic acid (2-hydroxybenzoic acid). (ii) Reimer-Tiemann Reaction: C6H5OH + CHCl3 + 3 NaOH -(340 K)-> Salicylaldehyde (2-hydroxybenzaldehyde) + 3 NaCl + 2 H2O. (iii) Nitration with concentrated HNO3: C6H5OH + 3 conc. HNO3 -(conc. H2SO4)-> 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol (Picric acid) + 3 H2O.
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (2 Marks)
How will you synthesize: (i) 1-Phenylethanol from benzaldehyde using Grignard reagent? (ii) Phenol from cumene?
Answer: Two organic synthesis routes
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Benzaldehyde to 1-Phenylethanol: Benzaldehyde (C6H5CHO) reacts with methylmagnesium bromide (CH3MgBr) in dry ether to form an adduct, followed by acid hydrolysis: C6H5CHO + CH3MgBr -(dry ether)-> C6H5-CH(OMgBr)-CH3 -(H3O⁺)-> C6H5-CH(OH)-CH3 (1-phenylethanol) + Mg(OH)Br. (ii) Phenol from Cumene: Cumene (isopropylbenzene, C6H5CH(CH3)2) is oxidized by atmospheric air to cumene hydroperoxide, followed by hydrolysis with dilute H2SO4: C6H5CH(CH3)2 + O2 -> C6H5C(CH3)2(OOH) -(dil. H2SO4)-> C6H5OH (phenol) + CH3COCH3 (acetone).
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (3 Marks)
Account for the following observations: (i) Phenol does not undergo substitution of -OH by -Cl with HCl/ZnCl2. (ii) Alcohols have higher boiling points than isomeric ethers. (iii) The C-O-C bond angle in dimethyl ether (111.7°) is slightly greater than the tetrahedral angle (109.5°).
Answer: Explanations for phenol reactivity, boiling points, and ether bond angle
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Phenol resistance: In phenol, the C-O bond acquires partial double bond character due to resonance (+R effect). This makes the C-O bond much stronger and shorter (136 pm vs 142 pm in alcohols), preventing cleavage by Lucas reagent. (ii) Higher boiling point: Alcohols possess intermolecular hydrogen bonding between -OH groups, whereas ethers lack hydrogen directly attached to oxygen and cannot associate by H-bonding. (iii) Bond angle in ethers: The repulsive steric interaction between the two bulky alkyl groups (methyl groups) attached to oxygen pushes them apart, opening the C-O-C bond angle to 111.7°, which is slightly larger than the tetrahedral angle (109.5°).
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (2 Marks)
What happens when: (i) Salicylic acid is treated with acetic anhydride in the presence of concentrated H2SO4? (ii) Anisole is treated with methyl chloride in the presence of anhydrous AlCl3?
Answer: Aspirin synthesis and Friedel-Crafts alkylation of anisole
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Treatment of salicylic acid with acetic anhydride produces Aspirin (acetylsalicylic acid): 2-HOC6H4COOH + (CH3CO)2O -(conc. H2SO4)-> 2-(CH3COO)C6H4COOH (Aspirin) + CH3COOH. (ii) Treatment of anisole with CH3Cl/anhyd. AlCl3 gives 4-methoxytoluene (p-isomer, major product) and 2-methoxytoluene (o-isomer, minor product) due to Friedel-Crafts alkylation.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (3 Marks)
Arrange the following compounds in increasing order of acidity and justify your order: Propan-1-ol, 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol, 4-Nitrophenol, Phenol, 4-Methylphenol.
Answer: Propan-1-ol < 4-Methylphenol < Phenol < 4-Nitrophenol < 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Justification: - Propan-1-ol is least acidic because aliphatic alkoxide has no resonance and is destabilized by +I effect. - Phenols are far more acidic due to resonance stabilization of phenoxide ion. - 4-Methylphenol has electron-donating methyl group (+I, hyperconjugation) which destabilizes phenoxide, making it less acidic than phenol. - 4-Nitrophenol has strong electron-withdrawing -NO2 group (-M, -I) at para position which stabilizes phenoxide ion, making it much more acidic than phenol. - 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol (Picric acid) has three powerful -NO2 groups that disperse negative charge extensively, making it exceptionally acidic (pKa ~ 0.7).
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (2 Marks)
Explain hydroboration-oxidation of propene with balanced chemical equations.
Answer: Hydroboration-oxidation of propene
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Diborane (B2H6) reacts with propene by syn-addition of B-H bond across the double bond in anti-Markovnikov fashion to form tripropylborane: 6 CH3-CH=CH2 + B2H6 -> 2 (CH3-CH2-CH2)3B. Tripropylborane on oxidation with alkaline hydrogen peroxide (H2O2 / OH⁻) cleaves into propan-1-ol: (CH3-CH2-CH2)3B + 3 H2O2 -(OH⁻)-> 3 CH3-CH2-CH2-OH (Propan-1-ol) + B(OH)3.
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
Write the products formed when diethyl ether is heated with excess concentrated HI.
Answer: Cleavage of diethyl ether with excess HI
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
When diethyl ether is heated with excess concentrated HI, both alkyl-oxygen bonds are cleaved in a two-stage process: Stage 1: C2H5-O-C2H5 + HI -> C2H5I + C2H5OH. Stage 2: C2H5OH + HI -> C2H5I + H2O. Overall Reaction: C2H5-O-C2H5 + 2 HI -(heat)-> 2 C2H5I (ethyl iodide) + H2O.
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' (C6H6O) gives a violet colour with neutral FeCl3. When 'A' is treated with CHCl3 and aqueous NaOH at 340 K, it yields compound 'B' (C7H6O2) as the major product. Compound 'A' when treated with CO2 and NaOH at 400 K under pressure followed by acidification yields compound 'C' (C7H6O3). Identify A, B, and C, and write balanced chemical equations for all reactions. (b) How is Aspirin prepared from compound 'C'?
Answer: A = Phenol, B = Salicylaldehyde, C = Salicylic acid; Aspirin preparation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3.5 marks): - Compound 'A' (C6H6O) giving violet colour with neutral FeCl3 is Phenol (C6H5OH). - Reaction with CHCl3 and aq. NaOH (Reimer-Tiemann reaction) gives Salicylaldehyde (2-hydroxybenzaldehyde) as compound 'B': C6H5OH (A) + CHCl3 + 3 NaOH -(340 K)-> 2-HOC6H4CHO (B, Salicylaldehyde) + 3 NaCl + 2 H2O. - Reaction with CO2 and NaOH at 400 K and 4-7 atm (Kolbe's reaction) followed by acidification yields Salicylic acid (2-hydroxybenzoic acid) as compound 'C': C6H5OH (A) + NaOH -> C6H5ONa -(CO2, 400 K, 4-7 atm)-> 2-HOC6H4COONa -(H⁺)-> 2-HOC6H4COOH (C, Salicylic acid). (b) Preparation of Aspirin (1.5 marks): - Salicylic acid (compound C) is acetylated with acetic anhydride in the presence of concentrated H2SO4: 2-HOC6H4COOH + (CH3CO)2O -(conc. H2SO4)-> 2-(CH3COO)C6H4COOH (Aspirin / Acetylsalicylic acid) + CH3COOH.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) Write the mechanism of the reaction of HI with methoxymethane (CH3-O-CH3). (b) Predict the products when the following ethers are cleaved with 1 equivalent of HI: (i) Ethoxybenzene (phenetole) (ii) (CH3)3C-O-CH2CH3 (iii) Benzyl ethyl ether. (c) How will you distinguish between 1-phenylethanol and 2-phenylethanol?
Answer: Ether cleavage mechanism, HI reactions on varied ethers, and iodoform distinction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Mechanism of Methoxymethane Cleavage (2 marks): - Step 1 (Protonation of ether): CH3-O-CH3 + H-I ⇌ [CH3-O⁺(H)-CH3] + I⁻ (oxonium ion formation). - Step 2 (SN2 attack by iodide ion): Iodide ion attacks one of the methyl carbons from backside, displacing methanol: I⁻ + CH3-O⁺(H)-CH3 -> [I···CH3···O⁺(H)CH3] -> CH3I + CH3OH. (b) Ether Cleavages (2 marks): (i) Ethoxybenzene + HI -> Phenol (C6H5OH) + Ethyl iodide (C2H5I). (Aryl-O bond has partial double bond character and cannot be cleaved). (ii) (CH3)3C-O-CH2CH3 + HI -> tert-Butyl iodide ((CH3)3C-I) + Ethanol (C2H5OH). (Proceeds via SN1 due to tertiary carbocation stability). (iii) C6H5CH2-O-CH2CH3 + HI -> Benzyl iodide (C6H5CH2I) + Ethanol (C2H5OH). (Benzyl carbocation is highly resonance-stabilized). (c) Distinction (1 mark): - 1-Phenylethanol (C6H5-CH(OH)-CH3) has the CH3-CH(OH)- group and gives a positive Iodoform test (yellow precipitate of CHI3 with I2/NaOH). - 2-Phenylethanol (C6H5-CH2-CH2-OH) does not have this group and gives no yellow precipitate.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) Explain why phenols are more acidic than alcohols using resonance structures of phenol and phenoxide ion. (b) Discuss the effect of electron-donating (-CH3) and electron-withdrawing (-NO2) groups on the acidity of phenol. (c) Why is picric acid a strong acid even though it contains no carboxyl group?
Answer: Resonance analysis of phenol acidity, substituent effects, and picric acid acidity
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Acidity of Phenol vs Alcohol (2.5 marks): - In phenol, resonance involves charge separation (+ charge on oxygen, - charge on ortho/para ring carbons), making phenol less stable. - In phenoxide ion (C6H5O⁻), resonance involves no charge separation: the negative charge on oxygen is delocalized over the ortho and para positions of the benzene ring through five stable canonical structures. - Alcohols (R-OH) form alkoxide ions (R-O⁻) where the negative charge is localized strictly on oxygen, and +I effect of alkyl groups intensifies the charge. Therefore, phenoxide ion is vastly more stabilized than alkoxide ion, shifting equilibrium forward. (b) Substituent Effects (1.5 marks): - Electron-withdrawing groups (-NO2, -CN, halogens): Disperse the negative charge of the phenoxide ion through -M and -I effects, increasing phenoxide stability and enhancing acidity. The effect is especially pronounced at ortho and para positions. - Electron-donating groups (-CH3, -OCH3, -NH2): Intensify the negative charge on the phenoxide ion by +I and +M effects, destabilizing it and decreasing acidity. (c) Picric Acid (1 mark): 2,4,6-Trinitrophenol has three intensely electron-withdrawing nitro groups that pull electron density away from the phenoxide oxygen through resonance (-M) and inductive (-I) effects. The resulting picrate anion is exceptionally stable, giving picric acid a pKa of 0.7 (comparable to mineral acids).
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Industrial Alcohols and Their Biochemical Impacts Methanol and ethanol are two industrially vital alcohols. Methanol (CH3OH, 'wood spirit') is manufactured by catalytic hydrogenation of carbon monoxide: CO + 2 H2 -(ZnO-Cr2O3, 573-673 K, 200-300 atm)-> CH3OH. Ingestion of even small quantities (10-30 mL) of methanol is fatal; inside the liver, alcohol dehydrogenase oxidizes methanol to methanal (formaldehyde), which rapidly coagulates cellular protoplasm, while formic acid causes severe metabolic acidosis and blindness by damaging the optic nerve. Ethanol is industrially obtained by fermentation of sugars using invertase and zymase enzymes. To prevent misuse of industrial ethanol, it is 'denatured' by adding poisonous substances. (i) Write the chemical equation for the industrial synthesis of methanol. (ii) Why does ingestion of methanol cause blindness and death? (iii) What is 'denatured alcohol'? Name two substances added to denature ethanol. (iv) Name the enzymes used to convert sucrose into ethanol during fermentation.
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Methanol and Ethanol Chemistry
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) CO(g) + 2 H2(g) -(ZnO-Cr2O3 / 573-673 K, 200-300 atm)-> CH3OH(l). (ii) In the liver, alcohol dehydrogenase oxidizes methanol to methanal (HCHO) and formic acid (HCOOH). Methanal rapidly reacts with and coagulates cellular proteins/protoplasm in a manner similar to egg boiling. Formic acid inhibits cytochrome oxidase in the optic nerve, causing irreversible retinal damage and blindness, while severe systemic acidosis leads to death. (iii) Denatured alcohol is ethanol rendered unfit for drinking by mixing it with toxic, foul-smelling, or foul-tasting additives. Commonly added: Methanol (~5-10%), Pyridine (foul smell), and Copper sulfate (blue dye for visual identification). (iv) Invertase (hydrolyzes sucrose to glucose + fructose) and Zymase (ferments glucose/fructose to ethanol + CO2).
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound (A) with molecular formula C4H10O is soluble in concentrated H2SO4. (A) does not react with sodium metal or cold HI. On heating with excess HI, (A) gives only one alkyl iodide (B). Identify (A) and (B) and write the reactions involved. (b) Write the equations for the preparation of 1-propoxypropane from propan-1-ol. (c) What happens when propan-2-ol is heated with copper at 573 K?
Answer: Identification of C4H10O as diethyl ether, Williamson synthesis, and Cu/573K oxidation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification of Compounds (2.5 marks): - Compound (A) has formula C4H10O, dissolves in conc. H2SO4 (forming oxonium salt), but does not react with metallic Na (not an alcohol, therefore an ether). - It does not react with cold HI, but on heating with excess HI it yields ONLY ONE alkyl iodide (B). This proves that (A) is a symmetrical ether: Diethyl ether (CH3CH2-O-CH2CH3). - Alkyl iodide (B) is Ethyl iodide (CH3CH2I). - Reactions: CH3CH2-O-CH2CH3 (A) + conc. H2SO4 -> [(C2H5)2OH]⁺ HSO4⁻ (soluble oxonium salt). CH3CH2-O-CH2CH3 (A) + 2 HI -(heat)-> 2 CH3CH2I (B, Ethyl iodide) + H2O. (b) Preparation of 1-propoxypropane (1.5 marks): - Method 1: Intermolecular Dehydration of propan-1-ol with conc. H2SO4: 2 CH3CH2CH2OH -(conc. H2SO4, 413 K)-> CH3CH2CH2-O-CH2CH2CH3 + H2O. - Method 2: Williamson Synthesis: CH3CH2CH2OH + Na -> CH3CH2CH2ONa + 1/2 H2. CH3CH2CH2ONa + CH3CH2CH2Br -> CH3CH2CH2-O-CH2CH2CH3 + NaBr. (c) Reaction with Cu at 573 K (1 mark): Propan-2-ol undergoes catalytic dehydrogenation to give propanone (acetone): CH3-CH(OH)-CH3 -(Cu / 573 K)-> CH3-CO-CH3 (acetone) + H2(g).
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Illustrate the following name reactions with one chemical equation each: (i) Kolbe's reaction (ii) Reimer-Tiemann reaction (iii) Williamson ether synthesis. (b) Give the mechanism for the reaction of ethanol with concentrated H2SO4 at 413 K.
Answer: Three named reactions and bimolecular etherification mechanism
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Name Reactions (3 marks): (i) Kolbe's Reaction: C6H5ONa + CO2 -(400 K, 4-7 atm)-> Sodium salicylate -(H⁺)-> Salicylic acid (2-hydroxybenzoic acid). (ii) Reimer-Tiemann Reaction: C6H5OH + CHCl3 + 3 NaOH -(340 K)-> Salicylaldehyde + 3 NaCl + 2 H2O. (iii) Williamson Synthesis: CH3CH2ONa + CH3I -> CH3CH2-O-CH3 + NaI. (b) Mechanism at 413 K (2 marks): Bimolecular nucleophilic substitution (SN2): - Step 1: Protonation of ethanol: CH3CH2-OH + H⁺ ⇌ CH3CH2-O⁺H2. - Step 2: Nucleophilic attack by a second unprotonated ethanol molecule on the protonated species (SN2 displacement of water): CH3CH2-OH + CH3CH2-O⁺H2 -> [CH3CH2-O⁺(H)-CH2CH3] (diethyloxonium ion) + H2O. - Step 3: Deprotonation: [CH3CH2-O⁺(H)-CH2CH3] -> CH3CH2-O-CH2CH3 (diethyl ether) + H⁺.
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
How will you carry out the following conversions: (i) Propene to Propan-2-ol (ii) Phenol to p-Bromophenol (iii) Ethyl bromide to Diethyl ether (iv) Phenol to Benzoquinone?
Answer: Four organic synthesis pathways
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Propene to Propan-2-ol: Acid-catalyzed hydration: CH3-CH=CH2 + H2O -(H2SO4)-> CH3-CH(OH)-CH3 (Markovnikov addition). (ii) Phenol to p-Bromophenol: Bromination in non-polar solvent: C6H5OH + Br2 -(in CS2 or CHCl3 at 273 K)-> p-Bromophenol (major product) + HBr. (iii) Ethyl bromide to Diethyl ether: Williamson synthesis: CH3CH2Br + C2H5ONa -> CH3CH2-O-CH2CH3 (diethyl ether) + NaBr. (iv) Phenol to Benzoquinone: Oxidation with chromic acid: C6H5OH + Na2Cr2O7 + H2SO4 -> 1,4-Benzoquinone (p-benzoquinone).
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' having molecular formula C3H8O on treatment with Lucas reagent gives turbidity after 5 minutes. On oxidation with acidified potassium dichromate, 'A' gives compound 'B' (C3H6O). Compound 'B' gives a positive iodoform test. Identify 'A' and 'B', and write all reactions. (b) Explain why tertiary alcohols do not undergo oxidation under mild conditions.
Answer: Identification of propan-2-ol and acetone, and resistance of 3° alcohols to oxidation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3.5 marks): - Compound 'A' (C3H8O) gives turbidity with Lucas reagent after 5 minutes, indicating that 'A' is a secondary (2°) alcohol: Propan-2-ol (CH3-CH(OH)-CH3). - Reaction with Lucas reagent: CH3-CH(OH)-CH3 + conc. HCl -(anhyd. ZnCl2)-> CH3-CH(Cl)-CH3 (2-chloropropane) + H2O (turbidity in 5 min). - Oxidation of 'A' with acidified K2Cr2O7 yields compound 'B': CH3-CH(OH)-CH3 + [O] -(K2Cr2O7/H2SO4)-> CH3-CO-CH3 (B, Propan-2-one / acetone) + H2O. - Compound 'B' gives a positive iodoform test because it has a CH3-CO- group: CH3-CO-CH3 + 3 I2 + 4 NaOH -> CHI3 (yellow ppt of iodoform) + CH3COONa + 3 NaI + 3 H2O. (b) Why 3° Alcohols Resist Oxidation (1.5 marks): - Oxidation of alcohols involves the cleavage of an O-H bond and an α-C-H bond to form a C=O double bond. - In tertiary alcohols (e.g. (CH3)3C-OH), the carbon bearing the -OH group has no α-hydrogen atom (all 4 valencies of carbon are bonded to oxygen and carbon atoms). - Therefore, mild oxidizing agents cannot oxidise them; only drastic conditions (conc. HNO3, high temp) break strong C-C bonds to yield carboxylic acid mixtures with fewer carbon atoms.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
(a) What is meant by hydroboration-oxidation? Why does it give anti-Markovnikov alcohol? (b) Write the structural formulas of the following compounds: (i) 2,3-Diethylphenol (ii) 1-Ethoxypropane (iii) 2-Methylbutan-2-ol.
Answer: Hydroboration-oxidation principle and structural formulas
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Hydroboration-Oxidation Principle (2 marks): - Hydroboration-oxidation is a two-step method of converting alkenes into alcohols: diborane (B2H6) adds across the double bond to form a trialkylborane, which is subsequently oxidized by alkaline hydrogen peroxide (H2O2/OH⁻) to an alcohol. - Anti-Markovnikov Orientation: Boron is larger than hydrogen and less electronegative (B^δ⁺ - H^δ⁻). During addition, boron attaches to the less sterically hindered terminal carbon atom, and the hydride ion attaches to the more substituted carbon. Subsequent oxidation replaces boron with -OH with retention of configuration, resulting in net anti-Markovnikov hydration of the alkene. (b) Structural Formulas (2 marks): (i) 2,3-Diethylphenol: Benzene ring with -OH at C-1, -CH2CH3 at C-2, and -CH2CH3 at C-3. (ii) 1-Ethoxypropane: CH3-CH2-O-CH2-CH2-CH3. (iii) 2-Methylbutan-2-ol: CH3-C(CH3)(OH)-CH2-CH3.
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) Account for the following: (i) Phenol has higher boiling point than toluene. (ii) Electrophilic substitution in phenol takes place predominantly at ortho and para positions. (iii) Cleavage of phenyl alkyl ether with HI always yields phenol and alkyl iodide, never iodobenzene and alcohol. (b) Describe the preparation of phenol from aniline.
Answer: Three core chemical explanations and diazotization route to phenol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Explanations (3.5 marks): (i) Intermolecular Hydrogen Bonding: Phenol molecules are strongly associated via intermolecular hydrogen bonding through -OH groups, demanding high thermal energy to vaporize (bp 182 °C). Toluene molecules are held only by weak London dispersion forces (bp 111 °C). (ii) Ortho/Para Orientation: The lone pair on oxygen delocalizes into the π-system of the benzene ring (+R resonance effect). Resonance contributors show that electron density increases selectively at the ortho and para positions, making these sites vulnerable to electrophilic attack. (iii) Ether Cleavage Selectivity: The sp² carbon-oxygen bond between the aromatic ring and oxygen has partial double bond character due to resonance (+R effect) and is much stronger (shorter) than the sp³ carbon-oxygen alkyl bond. Therefore, nucleophilic attack by I⁻ cleaves the weaker alkyl C-O bond via SN2 to yield alkyl iodide, leaving the C-O bond attached to the aromatic ring intact as phenol. (b) Preparation of Phenol from Aniline (1.5 marks): - Step 1 (Diazotization): Aniline is dissolved in dilute HCl and treated with sodium nitrite (NaNO2) at 273-278 K (0-5 °C) to form benzene diazonium chloride: C6H5NH2 + NaNO2 + 2 HCl -(273-278 K)-> C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + NaCl + 2 H2O. - Step 2 (Hydrolysis): The diazonium salt solution is warmed with water or dilute H2SO4 to liberate nitrogen gas and yield phenol: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + H2O -(warm)-> C6H5OH (Phenol) + N2(g) + HCl.
Organic Chemistry
Chapter 8: Aldehydes, Ketones and Carboxylic Acids
8 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following compounds will undergo Cannizzaro reaction on treatment with 50% concentrated NaOH?
(a) Benzaldehyde (C6H5CHO)
(b) Acetaldehyde (CH3CHO)
(c) Acetone (CH3COCH3)
(d) Propionaldehyde (CH3CH2CHO)
Answer: (a) Benzaldehyde (C6H5CHO)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Aldehydes having no α-hydrogen atom undergo self-oxidation and reduction (disproportionation) on heating with concentrated alkali (Cannizzaro reaction). Benzaldehyde lacks α-hydrogens and yields benzyl alcohol and sodium benzoate.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The reduction of benzoyl chloride to benzaldehyde using H2 in the presence of Pd-BaSO4 poisoned with sulfur or quinoline is known as:
(a) Rosenmund reduction
(b) Stephen reduction
(c) Clemmensen reduction
(d) Wolff-Kishner reduction
Answer: (a) Rosenmund reduction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Catalytic hydrogenation of an acyl chloride to an aldehyde using Pd supported on BaSO4 and partially poisoned with sulfur or quinoline (to prevent further reduction to alcohol) is called Rosenmund reduction: C6H5COCl + H2 -(Pd-BaSO4/quinoline)-> C6H5CHO + HCl.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The correct order of increasing reactivity towards nucleophilic addition reactions is:
(a) Acetophenone < Acetone < Acetaldehyde < Formaldehyde
(b) Formaldehyde < Acetaldehyde < Acetone < Acetophenone
(c) Acetone < Acetophenone < Acetaldehyde < Formaldehyde
(d) Acetaldehyde < Formaldehyde < Acetone < Acetophenone
Answer: (a) Acetophenone < Acetone < Acetaldehyde < Formaldehyde
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Reactivity towards nucleophilic addition decreases with increasing steric crowding and electron donation (+I effect) by alkyl groups. Alkyl groups reduce the electrophilic character of carbonyl carbon: HCHO (no alkyl group, least hindered) > CH3CHO (one +I methyl) > CH3COCH3 (two +I methyls) > C6H5COCH3 (resonance delocalization from phenyl ring reduces electrophilicity).
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Clemmensen reduction of a ketone is carried out using:
(a) Zn-Hg and concentrated HCl
(b) NH2NH2 and KOH in ethylene glycol
(c) LiAlH4 in dry ether
(d) NaBH4 in ethanol
Answer: (a) Zn-Hg and concentrated HCl
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Clemmensen reduction converts carbonyl group (>C=O) into a methylene group (>CH2) using zinc amalgam (Zn-Hg) and concentrated hydrochloric acid (conc. HCl).
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Which of the following compounds gives a bright silver mirror with Tollens' reagent?
(a) Ethanal
(b) Acetone
(c) Acetophenone
(d) Benzophenone
Answer: (a) Ethanal
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Tollens' reagent (ammoniacal silver nitrate solution [Ag(NH3)2]⁺) is a mild oxidizing agent that oxidizes aldehydes (both aliphatic and aromatic) to carboxylate anions while reducing Ag⁺ to metallic silver (silver mirror). Ketones do not reduce Tollens' reagent.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The Hell-Volhard-Zelinsky (HVZ) reaction is used for the preparation of:
(a) α-Halocarboxylic acids
(b) β-Halocarboxylic acids
(c) Acid chlorides
(d) Acid anhydrides
Answer: (a) α-Halocarboxylic acids
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Carboxylic acids having an α-hydrogen react with chlorine or bromine in the presence of a small amount of red phosphorus to form α-halocarboxylic acids (HVZ reaction): R-CH2-COOH + X2 -(red P / H2O)-> R-CH(X)-COOH + HX.
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Which of the following carboxylic acids has the lowest pKa value (is the strongest acid)?
(a) Trifluoroacetic acid (CF3COOH)
(b) Trichloroacetic acid (CCl3COOH)
(c) Chloroacetic acid (CH2ClCOOH)
(d) Acetic acid (CH3COOH)
Answer: (a) Trifluoroacetic acid (CF3COOH)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Fluorine is the most electronegative halogen and exerts the strongest electron-withdrawing inductive effect (-I). Three fluorine atoms in CF3COOH drastically stabilize the carboxylate anion by dispersing negative charge, giving it the lowest pKa (~0.23) and highest acidity.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Reaction of an aldehyde with 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazine (Brady's reagent) yields:
(a) a yellow, orange, or red crystalline precipitate
(b) a colourless gas
(c) a silver mirror
(d) a blue solution
Answer: (a) a yellow, orange, or red crystalline precipitate
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Aldehydes and ketones react with 2,4-DNP in acidic medium via nucleophilic addition followed by elimination of water to form brightly coloured (yellow, orange, or red) crystalline 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazones, serving as a classic test for the carbonyl functional group.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
The reaction of acetaldehyde with dilute NaOH gives 3-hydroxybutanal. This reaction is known as:
(a) Aldol condensation
(b) Cannizzaro reaction
(c) Etard reaction
(d) Kolbe's reaction
Answer: (a) Aldol condensation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Aldehydes with at least one α-hydrogen react in the presence of dilute alkali (dil. NaOH) to undergo nucleophilic addition of enolate anion to another carbonyl molecule, forming a β-hydroxyaldehyde (Aldol): 2 CH3CHO -(dil. NaOH)-> CH3-CH(OH)-CH2-CHO (3-hydroxybutanal).
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
In the reaction of benzaldehyde with Tollens' reagent and Fehling's solution:
(a) Benzaldehyde reduces Tollens' reagent but does not reduce Fehling's solution
(b) Benzaldehyde reduces both reagents
(c) Benzaldehyde reduces Fehling's solution but not Tollens' reagent
(d) Benzaldehyde reduces neither reagent
Answer: (a) Benzaldehyde reduces Tollens' reagent but does not reduce Fehling's solution
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Aromatic aldehydes like benzaldehyde are easily oxidized by Tollens' reagent (stronger oxidizing potential than Fehling's), forming a silver mirror. However, Fehling's solution is a weaker oxidizing agent and cannot oxidize aromatic aldehydes.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
The oxidation of toluene to benzaldehyde using chromyl chloride (CrO2Cl2) in CS2 followed by hydrolysis is called:
(a) Etard reaction
(b) Gattermann-Koch reaction
(c) Stephen reaction
(d) Cannizzaro reaction
Answer: (a) Etard reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Chromyl chloride (CrO2Cl2) in CS2 or CCl4 oxidizes the methyl group of toluene to a brown chromium complex [C6H5CH(OCrOHCl2)2], which upon aqueous acid hydrolysis cleaves cleanly to benzaldehyde. This is the Etard reaction.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
When acetone is heated with iodine and aqueous sodium hydroxide, a yellow precipitate of which compound is formed?
(a) CHI3
(b) CH3I
(c) CH2I2
(d) CI4
Answer: (a) CHI3

Compounds with a methyl carbonyl group (CH3-C=O) undergo the iodoform reaction. Acetone (CH3COCH3) reacts with I2 and NaOH to yield yellow crystalline triiodomethane (iodoform, CHI3) and sodium acetate: CH3COCH3 + 3 I2 + 4 NaOH -> CHI3 + CH3COONa + 3 NaI + 3 H2O.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Which of the following compounds does NOT undergo aldol condensation?
(a) Trichloroacetaldehyde (Chloral, CCl3CHO)
(b) Acetaldehyde (CH3CHO)
(c) Propanal (CH3CH2CHO)
(d) Acetone (CH3COCH3)
Answer: (a) Trichloroacetaldehyde (Chloral, CCl3CHO)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Aldol condensation requires at least one acidic α-hydrogen on the carbon adjacent to the carbonyl group. In chloral (CCl3-CHO), the α-carbon has three chlorine atoms and zero α-hydrogens; hence it cannot undergo aldol condensation.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Carboxylic acids do not give characteristic reactions of carbonyl group (>C=O), such as 2,4-DNP derivative formation, because:
(a) the lone pair of electrons on oxygen of -OH group is delocalized into the carbonyl group by resonance
(b) carboxyl carbon is sp³ hybridized
(c) carboxylic acids are basic
(d) the C=O bond is too strong to be broken
Answer: (a) the lone pair of electrons on oxygen of -OH group is delocalized into the carbonyl group by resonance
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In carboxylic acids, the lone pair of electrons on the hydroxyl oxygen is delocalized into the carbonyl group by resonance: R-C(=O)-OH <-> R-C(-O⁻)=O⁺H. This resonance significantly reduces the electrophilic character of the carbonyl carbon, preventing nucleophilic attack by 2,4-DNP, NaHSO3, or hydroxylamine.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1

(a) Methane (CH4)
(b) Ethane (C2H6)
(c) Propane
(d) Ethene
Answer: (a) Methane (CH4)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Heating the sodium salt of a carboxylic acid with soda lime (a 3:1 mixture of NaOH and CaO) eliminates carbon dioxide as Na2CO3, forming an alkane containing one less carbon atom than the parent acid: CH3COONa + NaOH -(CaO / heat)-> CH4 + Na2CO3.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Wolff-Kishner reduction of an aldehyde or ketone uses which of the following reagents?
(a) Hydrazine (NH2NH2) followed by KOH in ethylene glycol
(b) Zn-Hg and concentrated HCl
(c) H2 / Pd-BaSO4
(d) SnCl2 and concentrated HCl
Answer: (a) Hydrazine (NH2NH2) followed by KOH in ethylene glycol
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In Wolff-Kishner reduction, the carbonyl compound is first converted to a hydrazone with hydrazine (NH2NH2), which is then heated with a strong base (KOH) in high-boiling ethylene glycol (453-473 K) to evolve N2 gas and form the corresponding alkane.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
During the reaction of a ketone with semicarbazide (H2N-NH-CO-NH2), only one -NH2 group acts as a nucleophile because:
(a) the other -NH2 group is involved in resonance with the carbonyl group
(b) the other -NH2 group is sterically hindered
(c) only one nitrogen atom has a lone pair
(d) semicarbazide is an ambidentate nucleophile
Answer: (a) the other -NH2 group is involved in resonance with the carbonyl group
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Semicarbazide has two -NH2 groups: H2N-NH-CO-NH2. The lone pair on the amide -NH2 group directly attached to C=O is delocalized into the carbonyl π-system (H2N-C(=O)- <-> H2N⁺=C(-O⁻)-), greatly reducing its nucleophilicity. The terminal hydrazine -NH2 group is not involved in resonance, retaining full nucleophilic power to attack carbonyls.
Q18. MCQ

In the Gattermann-Koch reaction, benzene is converted into benzaldehyde by treatment with:
(a) CO + HCl in the presence of anhydrous AlCl3 / CuCl
(b) CrO2Cl2 in CS2
(c) Alkaline KMnO4
(d) CH3Cl in anhydrous AlCl3
Answer: (a) CO + HCl in the presence of anhydrous AlCl3 / CuCl
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
When benzene or its derivative is treated with carbon monoxide (CO) and hydrogen chloride (HCl) gas in the presence of anhydrous aluminum chloride (AlCl3) and cuprous chloride (CuCl), benzaldehyde is formed. This is the Gattermann-Koch reaction.
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Which of the following compounds will not give benzoic acid upon vigorous oxidation with alkaline KMnO4 followed by acidification?
(a) tert-Butylbenzene
(b) Toluene
(c) Ethylbenzene
(d) Isopropylbenzene
Answer: (a) tert-Butylbenzene
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Side-chain oxidation of alkylbenzenes by hot alkaline KMnO4 requires at least one benzylic hydrogen atom. In tert-butylbenzene (C6H5-C(CH3)3), the benzylic carbon is tertiary and lacks any benzylic hydrogen; therefore, it completely resists permanganate oxidation.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
The addition of hydrogen cyanide (HCN) to a carbonyl compound to form a cyanohydrin is catalyzed by a base because:
(a) the base deprotonates HCN to generate the strong nucleophile cyanide ion (CN⁻)
(b) the base protonates the carbonyl oxygen
(c) the base oxidizes the aldehyde
(d) the base removes water
Answer: (a) the base deprotonates HCN to generate the strong nucleophile cyanide ion (CN⁻)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
HCN is a very weak acid (Ka ≈ 10⁻¹⁰) and produces an insufficient concentration of nucleophilic cyanide ions on its own. Addition of base deprotonates HCN: HCN + OH⁻ ⇌ CN⁻ + H2O, generating the reactive nucleophile CN⁻ which readily attacks the carbonyl carbon.

Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The boiling points of aldehydes and ketones are lower than those of isomeric alcohols because:
(a) aldehydes and ketones lack intermolecular hydrogen bonding
(b) alcohols are less polar
(c) carbonyl compounds have higher molecular mass
(d) alcohols are non-polar
Answer: (a) aldehydes and ketones lack intermolecular hydrogen bonding
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Alcohols possess a hydrogen atom directly bonded to oxygen, forming strong intermolecular hydrogen bonds. Aldehydes and ketones have only dipole-dipole attractions between polar carbonyl groups, which are significantly weaker than hydrogen bonds.
