Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
Give simple chemical tests to distinguish between: (i) Ethanal and Propanal (ii) Benzoic acid and Phenol.
Answer: Distinguishing chemical tests
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Ethanal and Propanal: Iodoform Test. - Ethanal (CH3CHO) has a methyl carbonyl group (CH3-C=O); on warming with I2 and NaOH, it forms a yellow crystalline precipitate of iodoform (CHI3). - Propanal (CH3CH2CHO) does not have a CH3-C=O group and gives no yellow precipitate. (ii) Benzoic acid and Phenol: Sodium Bicarbonate (NaHCO3) Test. - Benzoic acid is stronger than carbonic acid; it reacts with saturated NaHCO3 solution to produce brisk effervescence of CO2 gas. - Phenol is a weaker acid and does not react with NaHCO3 (no effervescence).
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Write the mechanism of nucleophilic addition of HCN to an aldehyde in the presence of a base catalyst.
Answer: Base-catalyzed HCN addition mechanism
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Step 1: Generation of nucleophile (cyanide ion): HCN + OH⁻ ⇌ :C≡N:⁻ + H2O. (Base deprotonates HCN to generate the powerful nucleophile CN⁻). Step 2: Nucleophilic attack on carbonyl carbon (Slow, Rate-determining step): The cyanide ion attacks the electrophilic sp² hybridized carbonyl carbon perpendicularly to the planar carbonyl group, forming a tetrahedral alkoxide intermediate: R-CH(=O) + CN⁻ -> R-CH(CN)-O⁻. Step 3: Protonation (Fast): The tetrahedral alkoxide intermediate abstracts a proton from water to regenerate the base catalyst and form the cyanohydrin: R-CH(CN)-O⁻ + H2O -> R-CH(CN)-OH (Cyanohydrin) + OH⁻.
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
Explain the following named reactions with one example each: (i) Cannizzaro reaction (ii) Cross-aldol condensation.
Answer: Cannizzaro and cross-aldol condensation explanations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Cannizzaro Reaction: Disproportionation of an aldehyde lacking α-hydrogen in the presence of concentrated alkali (50% NaOH): 2 C6H5CHO + 50% NaOH -(heat)-> C6H5CH2OH (benzyl alcohol) + C6H5COONa (sodium benzoate). (ii) Cross-Aldol Condensation: Aldol condensation between two different carbonyl compounds. If both possess α-hydrogens, a mixture of four products is formed. When benzaldehyde (no α-H) reacts with ethanal in dilute NaOH, it yields 3-phenylprop-2-enal (cinnamaldehyde) cleanly: C6H5CHO + CH3CHO -(dil. NaOH / heat)-> C6H5-CH=CH-CHO + H2O.
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (3 Marks)
Arrange the following compounds in increasing order of property indicated: (i) Ethanal, Propanal, Propanone, Butanone (reactivity towards nucleophilic addition) (ii) CH3COOH, ClCH2COOH, FCH2COOH, Cl2CHCOOH (acidic strength) (iii) Pentan-1-ol, Pentanal, Ethoxypropane, Hexane (boiling points).
Answer: Three property orderings with reasoning
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Nucleophilic Addition: Butanone < Propanone < Propanal < Ethanal. Reason: Steric crowding and +I inductive effect of alkyl groups decrease the electrophilicity of carbonyl carbon. (ii) Acidic Strength: CH3COOH < ClCH2COOH < FCH2COOH < Cl2CHCOOH. Reason: Electron-withdrawing inductive effect (-I) stabilizes carboxylate anion; F is more electronegative than Cl, and two chlorines exert a stronger cumulative -I effect than one. (iii) Boiling Points: Hexane < Ethoxypropane < Pentanal < Pentan-1-ol. Reason: Hexane has weak dispersion forces; ether has weak dipole moments; aldehyde has strong dipole-dipole attractions; alcohol forms strong intermolecular hydrogen bonds.
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (2 Marks)
How will you carry out the following conversions: (i) Toluene to Benzaldehyde (ii) Benzene to Acetophenone?
Answer: Two aromatic organic conversions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Toluene to Benzaldehyde (Etard Reaction): Toluene is treated with chromyl chloride (CrO2Cl2) in CS2 followed by aqueous acid hydrolysis: C6H5CH3 + 2 CrO2Cl2 -(CS2)-> C6H5CH(OCrOHCl2)2 -(H3O⁺)-> C6H5CHO + 2 Cr(OH)2Cl2. (ii) Benzene to Acetophenone (Friedel-Crafts Acylation): Benzene reacts with acetyl chloride in the presence of anhydrous AlCl3: C6H6 + CH3COCl -(anhyd. AlCl3)-> C6H5COCH3 (acetophenone) + HCl.
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (3 Marks)
An organic compound 'A' (C3H6O) forms an orange-red precipitate with 2,4-DNP reagent and gives a yellow precipitate on heating with iodine in the presence of sodium hydroxide. It does not reduce Tollens' reagent or Fehling's solution. Identify compound 'A' and write all chemical reactions involved.
Answer: Identification of compound A as Propanone (Acetone)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Deduction: - Compound 'A' (C3H6O) reacts with 2,4-DNP, confirming it is an aldehyde or ketone. - It does not reduce Tollens' reagent or Fehling's solution, confirming it is a ketone, not an aldehyde. - It gives a positive iodoform test (yellow precipitate with I2/NaOH), confirming the presence of a methyl ketone (CH3-CO-) group. - The only 3-carbon ketone is Propanone (Acetone, CH3COCH3). 2. Chemical Reactions: - With 2,4-DNP: (CH3)2C=O + H2N-NH-C6H3(NO2)2 -(H⁺)-> (CH3)2C=N-NH-C6H3(NO2)2 (Acetone 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazone, yellow-orange ppt) + H2O. - With I2/NaOH (Iodoform reaction): CH3COCH3 + 3 I2 + 4 NaOH -(heat)-> CHI3 (yellow crystals of iodoform) + CH3COONa + 3 NaI + 3 H2O.
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (2 Marks)
What is the Hell-Volhard-Zelinsky (HVZ) reaction? Give one example with chemical equation.
Answer: HVZ reaction definition and chemical equation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Definition: Carboxylic acids having α-hydrogen atoms undergo halogenation exclusively at the α-position when treated with chlorine or bromine in the presence of a catalytic amount of red phosphorus, followed by aqueous workup. 2. Example: Propanoic acid reacts with bromine and red phosphorus to yield 2-bromopropanoic acid (α-bromopropanoic acid): CH3-CH2-COOH + Br2 -(red P / H2O)-> CH3-CH(Br)-COOH + HBr.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (3 Marks)
Explain why: (i) Carboxylic acids are stronger acids than phenols. (ii) Benzaldehyde is less reactive than propanal towards nucleophilic addition. (iii) Aromatic carboxylic acids do not undergo Friedel-Crafts reaction.
Answer: Explanations for relative acidities, reactivity, and Friedel-Crafts failure
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Carboxylic acid vs Phenol: In carboxylate ion (RCOO⁻), the negative charge is delocalized equally over two highly electronegative oxygen atoms in two equivalent resonance structures. In phenoxide ion (C6H5O⁻), the negative charge is delocalized onto less electronegative carbon atoms in non-equivalent structures. Thus, carboxylate ion is far more stable. (ii) Benzaldehyde vs Propanal: The carbonyl group in benzaldehyde is in conjugation with the benzene ring. Resonance delocalizes π-electrons of the ring into the carbonyl carbon (+R effect), decreasing its partial positive charge (electrophilicity). Propanal has no resonance stabilization of carbonyl carbon. (iii) Friedel-Crafts Failure: The -COOH group is strongly electron-withdrawing (-M, -I) and strongly deactivates the benzene ring. Additionally, the Lewis acid catalyst (anhyd. AlCl3) bonds to the lone pairs on carboxyl oxygen, completely destroying its catalytic activity.
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (2 Marks)
Write the structural formulas of: (i) Semicarbazone of cyclopentanone (ii) Oxime of benzaldehyde.
Answer: Structural formulas of carbonyl derivatives
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Semicarbazone of cyclopentanone: Cyclopentane ring with C=N-NH-CO-NH2. Reaction: Cyclopentanone + H2N-NH-CO-NH2 -> C5H8=N-NH-CO-NH2 + H2O. (ii) Oxime of benzaldehyde (Benzaldoxime): C6H5-CH=N-OH.
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
State Clemmensen and Wolff-Kishner reductions. Mention the key difference between their reaction conditions.
Answer: Comparison between Clemmensen and Wolff-Kishner reductions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Clemmensen Reduction: Converts carbonyl >C=O to >CH2 using Zn-Hg and concentrated HCl (conducted under strongly acidic conditions). Suitable for substrates sensitive to base. 2. Wolff-Kishner Reduction: Converts carbonyl >C=O to >CH2 by heating with hydrazine (NH2NH2) and KOH in ethylene glycol at 453-473 K (conducted under strongly basic conditions). Suitable for substrates sensitive to acid.
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' (molecular formula C8H8O) gives a positive 2,4-DNP test and forms a yellow precipitate of compound 'B' on treatment with I2 and NaOH. Compound 'A' does not reduce Tollens' reagent or Fehling's solution. On drastic oxidation with chromic acid (KMnO4/H2SO4), 'A' gives a monocarboxylic acid 'C' (molecular formula C7H6O2). Identify A, B, and C, and write balanced chemical equations for all reactions. (b) Write the mechanism of aldol condensation of ethanal in the presence of dilute NaOH.
Answer: Identification of A = Acetophenone, B = Iodoform, C = Benzoic acid; Aldol mechanism
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3 marks): - Compound 'A' (C8H8O) gives positive 2,4-DNP: it is a carbonyl compound (aldehyde or ketone). - It does not reduce Tollens' or Fehling's solution: it is a ketone, not an aldehyde. - It gives a yellow precipitate with I2/NaOH: it contains a methyl ketone group (-COCH3). - Vigorous oxidation gives C7H6O2 (Benzoic acid): hence 'A' is Acetophenone (C6H5COCH3). - Compound 'B' is Iodoform (CHI3). - Compound 'C' is Benzoic acid (C6H5COOH). - Reactions: 1. C6H5COCH3 (A) + 2,4-DNP -(H⁺)-> Acetophenone 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazone + H2O. 2. C6H5COCH3 + 3 I2 + 4 NaOH -> CHI3 (B, yellow ppt) + C6H5COONa + 3 NaI + 3 H2O. 3. C6H5COCH3 + 3 [O] -(KMnO4/H2SO4, heat)-> C6H5COOH (C, Benzoic acid) + CO2 + H2O. (b) Mechanism of Aldol Condensation (2 marks): - Step 1 (Enolate formation): Base (OH⁻) removes an acidic α-hydrogen from ethanal to form resonance-stabilized enolate ion: OH⁻ + H-CH2-CHO ⇌ [:CH2-CHO <-> CH2=CH-O⁻] + H2O. - Step 2 (Nucleophilic addition): The nucleophilic enolate ion attacks the carbonyl carbon of a second ethanal molecule: CH3-CH(=O) + :CH2-CHO -> CH3-CH(O⁻)-CH2-CHO (alkoxide ion). - Step 3 (Protonation): The alkoxide abstracts a proton from water to form 3-hydroxybutanal (aldol) and regenerates OH⁻: CH3-CH(O⁻)-CH2-CHO + H2O -> CH3-CH(OH)-CH2-CHO (Aldol) + OH⁻. - Upon heating, elimination of water gives but-2-enal (crotonaldehyde): CH3-CH=CH-CHO.
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) Account for the following: (i) Oxidation of propanal is easier than that of propanone. (ii) Monochloroethanoic acid is a stronger acid than ethanoic acid. (iii) Benzaldehyde reduces Tollens' reagent but not Fehling's solution. (b) Write chemical equations for the following: (i) Etard reaction (ii) Hell-Volhard-Zelinsky reaction.
Answer: Three chemical justifications and two named reaction equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Explanations (3 marks): (i) Aldehyde vs Ketone Oxidation: Propanal contains a hydrogen atom directly attached to the carbonyl carbon (formyl C-H bond). This C-H bond is relatively weak and is cleaved without breaking any carbon-carbon bonds. Propanone contains no hydrogen on carbonyl carbon; its oxidation requires breaking strong C-C bonds, requiring vigorous conditions. (ii) Acidity of Monochloroethanoic Acid: Chlorine is strongly electronegative and exerts an electron-withdrawing inductive effect (-I). This disperses the negative charge on the carboxylate oxygen, stabilizing the chloroacetate anion. Ethanoic acid has an electron-donating methyl group (+I) that intensifies negative charge, destabilizing acetate. (iii) Benzaldehyde and Fehling's: Aromatic aldehydes have carbonyl carbon conjugated with the benzene ring, reducing electrophilic character. Tollens' reagent is a stronger oxidizing agent than Fehling's solution, so Tollens' oxidizes benzaldehyde to benzoate while Fehling's cannot. (b) Equations (2 marks): (i) Etard Reaction: C6H5CH3 + 2 CrO2Cl2 -(CS2)-> C6H5CH(OCrOHCl2)2 -(H3O⁺)-> C6H5CHO + 2 Cr(OH)2Cl2. (ii) Hell-Volhard-Zelinsky (HVZ) Reaction: CH3COOH + Cl2 -(red P / H2O)-> ClCH2COOH + HCl.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) Describe the mechanism of esterification of a carboxylic acid with an alcohol in the presence of an acid catalyst. (b) Complete the following reactions: (i) CH3-CO-CH3 -(Zn-Hg / conc. HCl)-> (ii) C6H5-CHO + HCHO -(conc. NaOH, heat)-> (iii) CH3-COOH + PCl5 ->
Answer: Mechanism of esterification and three completed chemical equations
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Mechanism of Esterification (3 marks): - Step 1 (Protonation): Proton from acid catalyst protonates carbonyl oxygen, enhancing electrophilicity of carbonyl carbon: R-C(=O)-OH + H⁺ ⇌ R-C(⁺OH)-OH. - Step 2 (Nucleophilic attack): Alcohol molecule (R'OH) attacks the electrophilic carbon to form a tetrahedral intermediate: R-C(⁺OH)-OH + R'-OH ⇌ [R-C(OH)2(O⁺HR')]. - Step 3 (Proton transfer): Proton shifts from the alkoxy oxygen to one of the -OH groups: [R-C(OH)2(O⁺HR')] ⇌ [R-C(OH)(O⁺H2)(OR')]. - Step 4 (Elimination of water): Water departs as leaving group, forming a protonated ester: [R-C(OH)(O⁺H2)(OR')] ⇌ [R-C(⁺OH)(OR')] + H2O. - Step 5 (Deprotonation): Deprotonation regenerates the acid catalyst, yielding ester: [R-C(⁺OH)(OR')] ⇌ R-COOR' + H⁺. (b) Completed Reactions (2 marks): (i) CH3-CO-CH3 -(Zn-Hg/conc. HCl)-> CH3-CH2-CH3 (Propane, Clemmensen reduction). (ii) C6H5-CHO + HCHO -(conc. NaOH)-> C6H5CH2OH (benzyl alcohol) + HCOONa (sodium formate). (Cross-Cannizzaro: HCHO is more reactive and oxidizes preferentially). (iii) CH3-COOH + PCl5 -> CH3-COCl (acetyl chloride) + POCl3 + HCl.
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Carbonyl Reactivity and Diagnostic Tests Aldehydes and ketones contain the polar carbonyl functional group (>C=O). The sp² hybridized carbon carries a partial positive charge, making it highly susceptible to nucleophilic attack. While both aldehydes and ketones form crystalline derivatives with 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazine (Brady's reagent), their differentiation relies on redox behavior. Aldehydes are easily oxidized to carboxylic acids by mild reagents such as Tollens' reagent and Fehling's solution. Tollens' reagent contains [Ag(NH3)2]⁺, which oxidizes aldehydes to carboxylate ions while depositing a brilliant silver mirror. In addition, methyl ketones undergo the haloform reaction with halogen and base to yield trihalomethane precipitates. (i) Why are aldehydes more easily oxidized than ketones? (ii) What observation confirms a positive Tollens' test? (iii) Which among butanal and butan-2-one will give a yellow precipitate with I2/NaOH? Write the formula of the precipitate. (iv) Why is Fehling's test negative for benzaldehyde?
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Carbonyl Chemical Tests and Reactivity
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Aldehydes possess a hydrogen atom directly bonded to the carbonyl carbon (formyl C-H bond), which can be cleaved without breaking any carbon-carbon bonds. Ketones have no such hydrogen and require cleavage of strong C-C bonds. (ii) Deposition of a shining silver mirror on the inner walls of the test tube (or grey-black precipitate of metallic silver). (iii) Butan-2-one (CH3-CO-CH2CH3) will give the yellow precipitate because it contains a methyl ketone (CH3-C=O) group. The precipitate is Iodoform (CHI3). (iv) Benzaldehyde is an aromatic aldehyde; resonance with the benzene ring decreases electrophilicity of carbonyl carbon, and Fehling's solution is too weak an oxidizing agent to oxidize aromatic aldehydes.
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' with molecular formula C5H10O does not reduce Tollens' reagent but forms an addition compound with sodium hydrogen sulfite (NaHSO3) and gives a positive iodoform test. On Clemmensen reduction, 'A' gives compound 'B' (C5H12). Identify 'A' and 'B', and write all reactions involved. (b) Why cannot carboxylic acids be reduced to alcohols using NaBH4?
Answer: Identification of A = Pentan-2-one, B = Pentane, and inertness of -COOH to NaBH4
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3.5 marks): - Compound 'A' (C5H10O) forms NaHSO3 addition compound: contains a carbonyl group (>C=O). - It does not reduce Tollens' reagent: it is a ketone, not an aldehyde. - It gives a positive iodoform test: it contains a methyl ketone group (CH3-CO-). - The only 5-carbon methyl ketone is Pentan-2-one (CH3-CO-CH2CH2CH3). - Clemmensen reduction converts >C=O to >CH2, giving Pentane (CH3-CH2-CH2-CH2-CH3) as compound 'B'. - Reactions: 1. CH3COCH2CH2CH3 + NaHSO3 -> CH3-C(OH)(SO3Na)-CH2CH2CH3 (crystalline bisulfite adduct). 2. CH3COCH2CH2CH3 + 3 I2 + 4 NaOH -> CHI3 (yellow ppt of iodoform) + CH3CH2CH2COONa + 3 NaI + 3 H2O. 3. CH3COCH2CH2CH3 (A) -(Zn-Hg / conc. HCl)-> CH3CH2CH2CH2CH3 (B, Pentane) + H2O. (b) Inertness of -COOH towards NaBH4 (1.5 marks): - NaBH4 is a mild reducing agent that readily reduces aldehydes and ketones. However, in carboxylic acids, resonance delocalization of lone pairs on the -OH oxygen into the carbonyl group greatly reduces the electrophilicity of the carbonyl carbon. - Additionally, the acidic carboxyl proton reacts immediately with hydride: RCOOH + BH4⁻ -> RCOO⁻ + H2 + BH3. The resulting carboxylate anion has an intensive negative charge that repels incoming hydride nucleophiles. Therefore, powerful reducing agents like LiAlH4 or B2H6 are required.
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Write chemical equations for the following: (i) Stephen reduction (ii) Gattermann-Koch reaction (iii) Wolff-Kishner reduction. (b) How will you convert: (i) Ethanal to But-2-enal (ii) Bromobenzene to Benzoic acid?
Answer: Three named reaction equations and two chemical conversions
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Chemical Equations (3 marks): (i) Stephen Reduction: Nitriles are reduced by SnCl2/HCl to imine hydrochloride followed by hydrolysis: CH3CN + SnCl2 + 2 HCl -> CH3CH=NH·HCl -(H3O⁺)-> CH3CHO (ethanal) + NH4Cl. (ii) Gattermann-Koch Reaction: Benzene + CO + HCl -(anhyd. AlCl3/CuCl)-> C6H5CHO (benzaldehyde) + HCl. (iii) Wolff-Kishner Reduction: CH3COCH3 + NH2NH2 -> (CH3)2C=N-NH2 -(KOH / ethylene glycol, 453-473 K)-> CH3CH2CH3 (propane) + N2(g). (b) Conversions (2 marks): (i) Ethanal to But-2-enal: Aldol condensation followed by dehydration: 2 CH3CHO -(dil. NaOH)-> CH3-CH(OH)-CH2-CHO -(heat, -H2O)-> CH3-CH=CH-CHO (But-2-enal). (ii) Bromobenzene to Benzoic acid: Grignard carboxylation: C6H5Br + Mg -(dry ether)-> C6H5MgBr -(CO2)-> C6H5COOMgBr -(H3O⁺)-> C6H5COOH (benzoic acid).
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
How will you distinguish between the following pairs of compounds by simple chemical tests: (i) Pentan-2-one and Pentan-3-one (ii) Benzaldehyde and Acetophenone (iii) Formic acid and Acetic acid (iv) Propanal and Propanone?
Answer: Four pairs distinguished by chemical tests
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Pentan-2-one and Pentan-3-one: Iodoform Test. Pentan-2-one (CH3-CO-CH2CH2CH3) is a methyl ketone and yields a yellow precipitate of CHI3 with I2/NaOH. Pentan-3-one (CH3CH2-CO-CH2CH3) has no CH3-CO- group and gives no precipitate. (ii) Benzaldehyde and Acetophenone: Tollens' Test. Benzaldehyde is an aldehyde and reduces Tollens' reagent to give a silver mirror. Acetophenone is a ketone and does not reduce Tollens' reagent. (iii) Formic acid and Acetic acid: Tollens' or Fehling's Test. Formic acid (HCOOH) possesses an aldehyde-like formyl group (-CHO) and reduces Tollens' reagent to metallic silver. Acetic acid (CH3COOH) does not reduce Tollens' reagent. (iv) Propanal and Propanone: Fehling's Test. Propanal is an aliphatic aldehyde and reduces Fehling's solution to form a red precipitate of Cu2O. Propanone is a ketone and does not react.
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' having molecular formula C2H4O on oxidation with acidified K2Cr2O7 gives compound 'B' (C2H4O2). 'A' undergoes aldol condensation. Compound 'B' on heating with soda lime yields a hydrocarbon 'C'. Identify A, B, and C, and write all reactions. (b) Why do aldehydes and ketones have high dipole moments?
Answer: Identification of A = Ethanal, B = Ethanoic acid, C = Methane; Dipole moment explanation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3.5 marks): - Compound 'A' (C2H4O) undergoes aldol condensation and has 2 carbons: hence 'A' is Ethanal (Acetaldehyde, CH3CHO). - Oxidation of 'A' gives 'B' (C2H4O2): hence 'B' is Ethanoic acid (Acetic acid, CH3COOH). - Decarboxylation of 'B' with soda lime yields hydrocarbon 'C': hence 'C' is Methane (CH4). - Reactions: 1. CH3CHO (A) + [O] -(K2Cr2O7/H2SO4)-> CH3COOH (B, Ethanoic acid). 2. 2 CH3CHO -(dil. NaOH)-> CH3-CH(OH)-CH2-CHO (Aldol). 3. CH3COOH + NaOH -> CH3COONa + H2O. CH3COONa + NaOH -(CaO / heat)-> CH4 (C, Methane) + Na2CO3. (b) Dipole Moment of Carbonyls (1.5 marks): - The carbonyl group (>C=O) consists of a carbon atom doubly bonded to an oxygen atom. - Oxygen is significantly more electronegative than carbon (3.44 vs 2.55). The polarizable π-electron cloud is strongly shifted towards oxygen, creating substantial partial charges: >C^δ⁺=O^δ⁻. - The dipolar resonance structure >C⁺-O⁻ contributes about 40-50% to the hybrid, resulting in large dipole moments (2.3 - 2.8 D).
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
Give the structures and IUPAC names of the main products in the following reactions: (i) Reaction of cyclohexanone with hydroxylamine (ii) Reaction of benzaldehyde with semicarbazide (iii) Hydrolysis of propanenitrile with dilute HCl (iv) Reaction of butanal with 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazine.
Answer: Structures and IUPAC names of four organic products
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Cyclohexanone + NH2OH -> Cyclohexanone oxime + H2O. Structure: Six-membered ring with =N-OH. (ii) Benzaldehyde + H2N-NH-CO-NH2 -> Benzaldehyde semicarbazone + H2O. Structure: C6H5-CH=N-NH-CO-NH2. (iii) CH3CH2CN + 2 H2O + HCl -(heat)-> CH3CH2COOH (Propanoic acid) + NH4Cl. (iv) Butanal + 2,4-DNP -> Butanal 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazone + H2O. Structure: CH3-CH2-CH2-CH=N-NH-C6H3(NO2)2.
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) What is cross-aldol condensation? What products are obtained when a mixture of ethanal and propanal is treated with dilute NaOH? Write their structural formulas. (b) Why is formic acid stronger than benzoic acid?
Answer: Cross-aldol condensation of ethanal and propanal, and formic vs benzoic acid acidity
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Cross-Aldol Condensation (3.5 marks): - When aldol condensation is carried out between two different aldehydes/ketones, it is called cross-aldol condensation. Since both ethanal and propanal possess α-hydrogens, four aldol condensation products are formed upon dehydration: 1. Self-aldol of ethanal: But-2-enal (CH3-CH=CH-CHO). 2. Self-aldol of propanal: 2-Methylpent-2-enal (CH3-CH2-CH=C(CH3)-CHO). 3. Cross-aldol (ethanal enolate + propanal carbonyl): CH3-CH2-CH=CH-CHO (Pent-2-enal). 4. Cross-aldol (propanal enolate + ethanal carbonyl): CH3-CH=C(CH3)-CHO (2-Methylbut-2-enal). (b) Formic Acid vs Benzoic Acid (1.5 marks): - In benzoic acid (C6H5COOH), the phenyl ring acts as an electron-donating group through resonance (+R effect), delocalizing π-electrons into the carboxyl carbon. This reduces the polarity of the O-H bond and destabilizes the benzoate anion relative to formate. - In formic acid (HCOOH), hydrogen has virtually no inductive (+I) or resonance (+R) effect, allowing the formate anion to be more stable. - Consequently, formic acid (pKa = 3.75) is stronger than benzoic acid (pKa = 4.20).
Organic Chemistry
Chapter 9: Amines
6 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following amines gives a foul-smelling isocyanide when heated with chloroform and alcoholic KOH (Carbylamine test)?
(a) Aniline (C6H5NH2)
(b) N-Methylaniline
(c) Triethylamine
(d) N,N-Dimethylaniline
Answer: (a) Aniline (C6H5NH2)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The carbylamine reaction is specific only to primary (1°) aliphatic and aromatic amines. Aniline is a primary aromatic amine and reacts with CHCl3 and alc. KOH to produce phenyl isocyanide (foul smell): C6H5NH2 + CHCl3 + 3 KOH -> C6H5NC + 3 KCl + 3 H2O. Secondary and tertiary amines do not show this reaction.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
The correct order of increasing basic strength of methyl-substituted amines in aqueous solution is:
(a) NH3 < (CH3)3N < CH3NH2 < (CH3)2NH
(b) (CH3)3N < (CH3)2NH < CH3NH2 < NH3
(c) NH3 < CH3NH2 < (CH3)2NH < (CH3)3N
(d) (CH3)2NH < CH3NH2 < (CH3)3N < NH3
Answer: (a) NH3 < (CH3)3N < CH3NH2 < (CH3)2NH
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In aqueous solution, the basic strength of methylamines is determined by the combined interplay of inductive effect (+I), steric hindrance, and hydration of the substituted ammonium cation. The resulting order is: 2° > 1° > 3° > NH3: (CH3)2NH > CH3NH2 > (CH3)3N > NH3.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Gabriel phthalimide synthesis is exclusively used for the preparation of:
(a) Primary aliphatic amines
(b) Primary aromatic amines
(c) Secondary amines
(d) Tertiary amines
Answer: (a) Primary aliphatic amines
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Gabriel phthalimide synthesis involves an SN2 attack of phthalimide anion on an alkyl halide, yielding primary aliphatic amines cleanly. Primary aromatic amines (like aniline) cannot be prepared by this method because aryl halides do not undergo SN2 nucleophilic substitution with phthalimide anion.
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Hoffmann bromamide degradation of propanamide (CH3CH2CONH2) yields:
(a) Ethylamine (CH3CH2NH2)
(b) Propylamine (CH3CH2CH2NH2)
(c) Methylamine (CH3NH2)
(d) Ethanamide
Answer: (a) Ethylamine (CH3CH2NH2)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Hoffmann bromamide degradation converts a primary carboxamide into a primary amine with one carbon atom less than the parent amide: CH3CH2CONH2 + Br2 + 4 KOH -> CH3CH2NH2 (ethylamine) + K2CO3 + 2 KBr + 2 H2O.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Hinsberg's reagent used to distinguish between primary, secondary, and tertiary amines is:
(a) Benzenesulfonyl chloride
(b) Benzoyl chloride
(c) Acetyl chloride
(d) p-Toluenesulfonic acid
Answer: (a) Benzenesulfonyl chloride
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Hinsberg's reagent is benzenesulfonyl chloride (C6H5SO2Cl). It reacts with primary amines to form N-alkylbenzenesulfonamides (soluble in alkali due to acidic N-H), with secondary amines to form insoluble sulfonamides, and does not react with tertiary amines.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Direct nitration of aniline with concentrated HNO3 and concentrated H2SO4 yields an unusually large amount (~47%) of:
(a) m-Nitroaniline
(b) o-Nitroaniline
(c) p-Nitroaniline
(d) 2,4,6-Trinitroaniline
Answer: (a) m-Nitroaniline
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In strongly acidic nitrating mixture, the basic amino group of aniline is protonated to form the anilinium ion (C6H5NH3⁺). The -NH3⁺ group has a positive charge and acts as a powerful electron-withdrawing, meta-directing group (-I effect), leading to 47% meta-nitroaniline (along with 51% para and 2% ortho).
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Aniline reacts with bromine water at room temperature to give a white precipitate of:
(a) 2,4,6-Tribromoaniline
(b) 4-Bromoaniline
(c) 2-Bromoaniline
(d) 3-Bromoaniline
Answer: (a) 2,4,6-Tribromoaniline
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The -NH2 group strongly activates the benzene ring towards electrophilic attack due to the powerful +R resonance effect of the nitrogen lone pair. Reaction with bromine water results in instantaneous bromination at all ortho and para positions, yielding 2,4,6-tribromoaniline as a white precipitate.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Which of the following is the strongest base in gaseous phase?
(a) (CH3)3N
(b) (CH3)2NH
(c) CH3NH2
(d) NH3
Answer: (a) (CH3)3N
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In the gas phase, solvent effects (hydration) and steric hindrances are absent; basicity is governed solely by the electron-donating inductive effect (+I) of alkyl groups. Since tertiary amine has three +I methyl groups, the electron density on nitrogen is highest: 3° > 2° > 1° > NH3.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
Azo coupling of benzenediazonium chloride with phenol in a basic medium (pH 9-10) yields:
(a) p-Hydroxyazobenzene (Orange dye)
(b) p-Aminoazobenzene (Yellow dye)
(c) Chlorobenzene
(d) Phenol
Answer: (a) p-Hydroxyazobenzene (Orange dye)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + C6H5OH -(pH 9-10 / OH⁻)-> C6H5-N=N-C6H4-OH (p-hydroxyazobenzene, brilliant orange dye) + Cl⁻ + H2O.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Reduction of nitrobenzene is best carried out industrially using Fe and concentrated HCl because:
(a) FeCl2 formed gets hydrolyzed to release HCl, requiring only a catalytic amount of HCl
(b) Fe is a noble metal
(c) it gives nitrosobenzene
(d) the reaction is reversible
Answer: (a) FeCl2 formed gets hydrolyzed to release HCl, requiring only a catalytic amount of HCl
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Reduction with Fe and HCl is preferred because the ferrous chloride (FeCl2) produced hydrolyzes in the aqueous reaction mixture (FeCl2 + 2 H2O -> Fe(OH)2 + 2 HCl), regenerating hydrochloric acid. Hence, only a small catalytic amount of HCl is needed to initiate the reaction.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Sulfanilic acid exists as a dipolar zwitterion because:
(a) proton transfers internally from acidic -SO3H group to basic -NH2 group
(b) it contains sulfur
(c) it has two benzene rings
(d) it is insoluble in acids
Answer: (a) proton transfers internally from acidic -SO3H group to basic -NH2 group
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Sulfanilic acid (p-aminobenzenesulfonic acid) contains both an acidic sulfonic group (-SO3H) and a basic amino group (-NH2) in the same molecule. An internal acid-base neutralization occurs: the sulfonic acid transfers its proton to the amino group, forming the dipolar zwitterion +H3N-C6H4-SO3⁻.
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Primary aliphatic amines react with cold nitrous acid (HNO2) to produce:
(a) Alcohols with quantitative liberation of nitrogen gas
(b) Stable diazonium salts
(c) Nitroalkanes
(d) Alkyl nitrites
Answer: (a) Alcohols with quantitative liberation of nitrogen gas
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
R-NH2 + HNO2 -(0-5 °C)-> [R-N2⁺Cl⁻] (unstable aliphatic diazonium salt) -> R-OH + N2(g)↑ + HCl. The evolution of nitrogen gas is quantitative and is utilized in amino acid estimation.
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Why does aniline not undergo Friedel-Crafts alkylation or acylation?
(a) The lone pair on nitrogen forms a coordinate complex with Lewis acid catalyst AlCl3
(b) Aniline is a gas
(c) Benzene ring is deactivated
(d) AlCl3 decomposes aniline
Answer: (a) The lone pair on nitrogen forms a coordinate complex with Lewis acid catalyst AlCl3
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Aniline is a Lewis base and reacts directly with the Lewis acid catalyst AlCl3 to form an insoluble adduct: C6H5NH2: -> AlCl3. This puts a formal positive charge on nitrogen (-N⁺H2-Al⁻Cl3), which strongly deactivates the benzene ring via -I effect and prevents electrophilic attack.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following compounds reacts with benzenesulfonyl chloride to give a solid insoluble in aqueous KOH?
(a) Diethylamine ((C2H5)2NH)
(b) Ethylamine (C2H5NH2)
(c) Triethylamine ((C2H5)3N)
(d) Aniline (C6H5NH2)
Answer: (a) Diethylamine ((C2H5)2NH)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Diethylamine is a secondary amine. It reacts with benzenesulfonyl chloride to form N,N-diethylbenzenesulfonamide: C6H5SO2Cl + HN(C2H5)2 -> C6H5SO2N(C2H5)2 + HCl. Because there is no acidic hydrogen attached to nitrogen, the sulfonamide is completely insoluble in aqueous KOH.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
The conversion of benzenediazonium chloride to fluorobenzene using fluoroboric acid (HBF4) followed by heating is known as:
(a) Balz-Schiemann reaction
(b) Sandmeyer reaction
(c) Gattermann reaction
(d) Swarts reaction
Answer: (a) Balz-Schiemann reaction
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + HBF4 -> C6H5N2⁺BF4⁻ (insoluble precipitate) -(heat)-> C6H5F (fluorobenzene) + BF3 + N2. This is the Balz-Schiemann reaction.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
The correct order of boiling points among isomeric butylamines is:
(a) Primary > Secondary > Tertiary
(b) Tertiary > Secondary > Primary
(c) Secondary > Primary > Tertiary
(d) Primary > Tertiary > Secondary
Answer: (a) Primary > Secondary > Tertiary
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Primary amines (CH3(CH2)3NH2) have two N-H bonds and form extensive intermolecular hydrogen-bonded networks. Secondary amines have one N-H bond and form weaker H-bonds. Tertiary amines have no N-H bonds and cannot form intermolecular hydrogen bonds among themselves, resulting in the lowest boiling points.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Which of the following is used to prepare pure p-bromoaniline from aniline?
(a) Acetylation with acetic anhydride before bromination, followed by hydrolysis
(b) Direct reaction with bromine water
(c) Reaction with Br2 in CS2
(d) Reaction with HBr
Answer: (a) Acetylation with acetic anhydride before bromination, followed by hydrolysis
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Direct bromination gives 2,4,6-tribromoaniline. To prepare monobromo derivative, the activating effect of -NH2 group is moderated by acetylation with (CH3CO)2O to form acetanilide. Bromination of acetanilide with Br2 in acetic acid yields 4-bromoacetanilide (major), which upon acid or alkaline hydrolysis yields 4-bromoaniline (p-bromoaniline).
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The reagent used in Mendius reaction to reduce nitriles to primary amines is:
(a) Sodium and ethanol (Na / C2H5OH)
(b) Zn-Hg / HCl
(c) NaBH4
(d) Sn / HCl
Answer: (a) Sodium and ethanol (Na / C2H5OH)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The reduction of alkyl or aryl cyanides (nitriles) to primary amines using sodium in boiling ethanol (or catalytic hydrogenation) is called the Mendius reaction: R-C≡N + 4 [H] -(Na/C2H5OH)-> R-CH2-NH2.

(a) Benzene

(d) Aniline
Answer: (a) Benzene

Mild reducing agents like hypophosphorous acid (H3PO2 / phosphinic acid) or ethanol reduce diazonium salts to arenes: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + H3PO2 + H2O -(Cu⁺)-> C6H6 (Benzene) + N2 + H3PO3 + HCl.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
Which of the following amines does not form hydrogen bonds with water?
(a) All aliphatic amines can form hydrogen bonds with water
(b) Primary amines only
(c) Secondary amines only
(d) Tertiary amines cannot
Answer: (a) All aliphatic amines can form hydrogen bonds with water
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
All lower aliphatic amines (1°, 2°, and 3°) possess a lone pair of electrons on the nitrogen atom which can accept hydrogen bonds from polar water molecules (R3N:···H-O-H), rendering lower members water-soluble.

Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
In the Sandmeyer reaction, benzenediazonium chloride is converted to chlorobenzene using:
(a) Cu2Cl2 / HCl
(b) Cu powder / HCl
(c) CuCl2 / HCl
(d) Cl2 / FeCl3
Answer: (a) Cu2Cl2 / HCl
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The Sandmeyer reaction utilizes cuprous chloride (Cu2Cl2) dissolved in concentrated HCl to replace the diazonium group with chlorine: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ -(Cu2Cl2/HCl)-> C6H5Cl + N2. (Using copper powder is the Gattermann reaction).
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following compounds is the least basic?
(a) C6H5NH2 (Aniline)
(b) CH3NH2
(c) (CH3)2NH
(d) NH3
Answer: (a) C6H5NH2 (Aniline)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In aniline, the lone pair of electrons on the nitrogen atom is delocalized over the aromatic benzene ring via +R resonance effect, making it significantly less available for protonation compared to ammonia and aliphatic amines (pKb of aniline is ~9.38, compared to 4.75 for NH3).

Q31. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2024 (2 Marks)
Distinguish between the following pairs using a simple chemical test: (i) Methylamine and Dimethylamine (ii) Aniline and Benzylamine.
Answer: Distinguishing chemical tests for amines
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Methylamine and Dimethylamine: Carbylamine Test. - Methylamine (1° amine) on warming with CHCl3 and alcoholic KOH gives an extremely offensive, foul smell of methyl isocyanide (CH3NC). - Dimethylamine (2° amine) does not give the carbylamine test. (ii) Aniline and Benzylamine: Azo Dye Test. - Aniline on diazotization at 0-5 °C followed by coupling with alkaline β-naphthol yields a brilliant orange-red azo dye. - Benzylamine forms an unstable aliphatic diazonium salt that decomposes immediately, liberating N2 gas with no dye formation.
Q32. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (3 Marks)
Explain the Hoffmann bromamide degradation reaction. Write its chemical equation and outline the reason for shortening of carbon chain.
Answer: Hoffmann bromamide degradation explanation and equation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Explanation: When a primary carboxamide is heated with bromine and aqueous or ethanolic potassium hydroxide, it degrades to form a primary amine containing one carbon atom less than the starting amide. 2. Chemical Equation: R-CONH2 + Br2 + 4 KOH -(heat)-> R-NH2 + K2CO3 + 2 KBr + 2 H2O. 3. Reason for Chain Shortening: The mechanism involves intramolecular rearrangement (migration) of the alkyl or aryl group (R) from the carbonyl carbon to the electron-deficient nitrogen atom of an intermediate nitrene/isocyanate (R-N=C=O). Subsequent alkaline hydrolysis expels the carbonyl carbon as carbonate ion (CO3²⁻), resulting in loss of one carbon atom.
Q33. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2023 (2 Marks)
Why is aniline a weaker base than aliphatic amines? Explain using resonance structures.
Answer: Resonance analysis of aniline basicity
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Aniline has 5 canonical resonance structures in which the lone pair of electrons on nitrogen is delocalized over the ortho and para positions of the benzene ring (+R effect). This reduces the electron density on nitrogen, lowering its proton-accepting ability. 2. Furthermore, when aniline accepts a proton to form anilinium ion (C6H5NH3⁺), the positive charge cannot delocalize into the ring; anilinium ion has only 2 resonance structures. Thus, protonation decreases resonance stabilization (loss of exchange energy), making aniline a much weaker base (pKb ~ 9.38 vs 3.3 for aliphatic amines).
Q34. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2022 (3 Marks)
How will you convert: (i) Nitrobenzene to Aniline (ii) Aniline to Fluorobenzene (iii) Benzenediazonium chloride to Benzene?
Answer: Three organic conversions involving diazonium and nitro compounds
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Nitrobenzene to Aniline: C6H5NO2 + 3 Fe + 6 HCl -> C6H5NH2 (Aniline) + 3 FeCl2 + 2 H2O. (ii) Aniline to Fluorobenzene (Balz-Schiemann reaction): - Step 1: Diazotization: C6H5NH2 + NaNO2 + 2 HCl -(273-278 K)-> C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻. - Step 2: Precipitation with HBF4: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + HBF4 -> C6H5N2⁺BF4⁻. - Step 3: Pyrolysis: C6H5N2⁺BF4⁻ -(heat)-> C6H5F (Fluorobenzene) + BF3 + N2. (iii) Benzenediazonium chloride to Benzene: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + H3PO2 + H2O -(Cu⁺)-> C6H6 (Benzene) + N2 + H3PO3 + HCl.
Q35. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2020 (2 Marks)
Write the chemical reaction of benzenediazonium chloride with: (i) Cu2Cl2 / HCl (ii) Alkaline Phenol.
Answer: Sandmeyer reaction and azo coupling
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) With Cu2Cl2/HCl (Sandmeyer Reaction): C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ -(Cu2Cl2 / HCl)-> C6H5Cl (Chlorobenzene) + N2(g). (ii) With Alkaline Phenol (Azo Coupling): C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + C6H5OH -(OH⁻, pH 9-10)-> C6H5-N=N-C6H4-OH (p-hydroxyazobenzene, orange dye) + Cl⁻ + H2O.
Q36. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2019 (3 Marks)
Describe Gabriel phthalimide synthesis with balanced chemical equations. Why cannot aromatic primary amines be prepared by this method?
Answer: Gabriel phthalimide synthesis and aromatic limitation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Procedure & Equations: - Phthalimide is reacted with ethanolic KOH to produce potassium phthalimide: C6H4(CO)2NH + KOH -> C6H4(CO)2N⁻K⁺ + H2O. - Potassium phthalimide is heated with an alkyl halide to yield N-alkylphthalimide via SN2: C6H4(CO)2N⁻K⁺ + R-X -> C6H4(CO)2N-R + KX. - Alkaline hydrolysis of N-alkylphthalimide yields primary aliphatic amine: C6H4(CO)2N-R + 2 NaOH -(aq)-> C6H4(COONa)2 (sodium phthalate) + R-NH2 (1° amine). 2. Limitation: Aromatic primary amines (like aniline) cannot be prepared because aryl halides (C6H5X) do not undergo nucleophilic substitution (SN2) with phthalimide anion under these conditions due to partial double bond character of C-X bond and ring repulsion.
Q37. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2018 (2 Marks)
Explain why: (i) Alkylamines are more basic than ammonia. (ii) Primary amines have higher boiling points than tertiary amines of comparable molecular mass.
Answer: Explanations for alkylamine basicity and boiling points
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Basicity: Alkyl groups have an electron-donating inductive effect (+I). In alkylamines (R-NH2), the +I effect pushes electron density onto nitrogen, increasing the availability of the lone pair for protonation and stabilizing the alkylammonium cation (R-NH3⁺). Hence, alkylamines are stronger bases than NH3. (ii) Boiling Points: Primary amines possess two hydrogen atoms directly bonded to electronegative nitrogen (N-H), forming extensive intermolecular hydrogen bonding. Tertiary amines have no hydrogen attached to nitrogen and cannot form intermolecular hydrogen bonds among themselves, resulting in much lower boiling points.
Q38. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2017 (3 Marks)
How will you separate and identify a mixture of 1°, 2°, and 3° amines using Hinsberg's reagent (benzenesulfonyl chloride)?
Answer: Hinsberg method for amine separation and identification
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Addition of Hinsberg's Reagent (C6H5SO2Cl) in aqueous KOH: - 1° Amine forms N-alkylbenzenesulfonamide (C6H5SO2NHR), which contains an acidic hydrogen on nitrogen. It dissolves completely in aqueous KOH to form a clear soluble potassium salt. - 2° Amine forms N,N-dialkylbenzenesulfonamide (C6H5SO2NR2), which has no acidic hydrogen. It remains insoluble and precipitates as an oily layer or solid in KOH. - 3° Amine does not react because it has no replaceable hydrogen on nitrogen, remaining insoluble in alkaline medium. 2. Separation: - Filter the mixture: the precipitate contains 2° amine sulfonamide; unreacted 3° amine is distilled or extracted. - Acidify the clear alkaline filtrate with dilute HCl: the 1° amine sulfonamide precipitates out and is recovered by acid hydrolysis.
Q39. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2016 (2 Marks)
Account for the fact that nitration of aniline with nitric acid produces a mixture of ortho, meta, and para-nitroanilines.
Answer: Explanation of direct nitration isomer distribution
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In strongly acidic nitrating medium (conc. HNO3 + conc. H2SO4), aniline (ortho/para-directing due to +R effect of -NH2) is partially protonated into the anilinium ion (C6H5NH3⁺). The -NH3⁺ group has a formal positive charge and exerts a powerful electron-withdrawing inductive effect (-I), strongly deactivating the ring and directing incoming nitronium ions (NO2⁺) to the meta position. Because of the equilibrium C6H5NH2 + H⁺ ⇌ C6H5NH3⁺, nitration occurs on both unprotonated aniline (yielding 51% para and 2% ortho) and anilinium ion (yielding 47% meta).
Q40. Short Answer (2-3M)
CBSE 2015 (2 Marks)
Write the structure of the zwitterion of sulfanilic acid. Why does it show amphoteric behaviour?
Answer: Zwitterion structure of sulfanilic acid and amphoteric character
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
1. Structure: +H3N-C6H4-SO3⁻ (dipolar ion with protonated amino group and deprotonated sulfonate group at para positions). 2. Amphoteric Nature: In acidic medium, the basic sulfonate group (-SO3⁻) accepts a proton to form +H3N-C6H4-SO3H (cation). In basic medium, the acidic ammonium group (-NH3⁺) donates a proton to form H2N-C6H4-SO3⁻ (anion). Thus, it reacts with both strong acids and strong bases.
Q41. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2024 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' having molecular formula C2H7N on treatment with nitrous acid gives an alcohol 'B' and liberates nitrogen gas. Compound 'A' on warming with CHCl3 and alcoholic KOH gives an offensive smelling compound 'C'. Identify A, B, and C, and write balanced chemical equations for all reactions. (b) Why is methylamine a stronger base than ammonia in aqueous solution? (c) Arrange the following in increasing order of basic strength in aqueous solution: C6H5NH2, C6H5N(CH3)2, (C2H5)2NH, CH3NH2.
Answer: A = Ethanamine, B = Ethanol, C = Ethyl isocyanide; Basicity analysis and ranking
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3 marks): - Compound 'A' (C2H7N) liberates N2 gas with HNO2: it is a primary aliphatic amine. - It gives an offensive smelling compound 'C' with CHCl3/alc. KOH (carbylamine test): confirms 'A' is a 1° amine: Ethanamine (CH3CH2NH2). - Reaction with HNO2 yields Ethanol (CH3CH2OH) as compound 'B': CH3CH2NH2 (A) + HNO2 -> CH3CH2OH (B, Ethanol) + N2(g)↑ + H2O. - Reaction with CHCl3/KOH yields Ethyl isocyanide (CH3CH2NC) as compound 'C': CH3CH2NH2 (A) + CHCl3 + 3 KOH -(heat)-> CH3CH2NC (C, Ethyl isocyanide) + 3 KCl + 3 H2O. (b) Methylamine vs Ammonia (1 mark): - The methyl group in CH3NH2 exerts an electron-donating inductive effect (+I), increasing electron density on nitrogen and stabilizing the methylammonium cation (CH3NH3⁺) via charge dispersal. - Ammonia lacks any +I group, making it a weaker base. (c) Basicity Ordering (1 mark): C6H5NH2 < C6H5N(CH3)2 < CH3NH2 < (C2H5)2NH. (Aromatic amines are much weaker than aliphatic amines due to resonance; secondary aliphatic amine is strongest in aqueous medium).
Q42. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2023 (5 Marks)
(a) How will you convert aniline into: (i) 4-Bromoaniline (ii) Benzonitrile (iii) p-Aminoazobenzene? (b) Why is pyridine added during the acylation of amines with acetyl chloride?
Answer: Three conversions of aniline and role of pyridine in acylation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Conversions of Aniline (3.5 marks): (i) Aniline to 4-Bromoaniline: - Step 1: Protection by acetylation: C6H5NH2 + (CH3CO)2O -(pyridine)-> C6H5NHCOCH3 (acetanilide) + CH3COOH. - Step 2: Bromination: C6H5NHCOCH3 + Br2 -(CH3COOH)-> 4-Br-C6H4-NHCOCH3 (4-bromoacetanilide, major). - Step 3: Deprotection by hydrolysis: 4-Br-C6H4-NHCOCH3 + H2O -(H⁺ or OH⁻)-> 4-Br-C6H4-NH2 (4-bromoaniline) + CH3COOH. (ii) Aniline to Benzonitrile: - Step 1: Diazotization: C6H5NH2 + NaNO2 + 2 HCl -(273-278 K)-> C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + NaCl + 2 H2O. - Step 2: Sandmeyer cyanation: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + CuCN -(KCN, heat)-> C6H5CN (Benzonitrile) + N2. (iii) Aniline to p-Aminoazobenzene: - Benzenediazonium chloride is coupled with aniline in mildly acidic medium (pH 4-5): C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + C6H5NH2 -(pH 4-5)-> C6H5-N=N-C6H4-NH2 (p-aminoazobenzene, yellow dye) + HCl. (b) Role of Pyridine in Acylation (1.5 marks): - The acylation reaction generates hydrochloric acid (HCl) as a byproduct: R-NH2 + CH3COCl -> R-NHCOCH3 + HCl. - Pyridine is a stronger base than the amine. It neutralizes the HCl formed, forming pyridinium chloride and driving the equilibrium forward according to Le Chatelier's principle. This also prevents the amine reactant from being protonated into an unreactive ammonium salt.
Q43. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2022 (5 Marks)
(a) Discuss the factors influencing the basic strength of aliphatic amines in aqueous medium: Inductive effect, Solvation effect, and Steric hindrance. (b) Why does the basicity order change from (CH3)2NH > CH3NH2 > (CH3)3N for methylamines to (C2H5)2NH > (C2H5)3N > C2H5NH2 for ethylamines?
Answer: Comprehensive analysis of amine basicity factors and alkyl substitution trends
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Three Factors Influencing Basicity in Water (3 marks): 1. Inductive Effect (+I): Alkyl groups push electron density toward nitrogen, stabilizing the protonated ammonium cation (R3NH⁺). Purely on inductive grounds, order should be: 3° > 2° > 1° > NH3. 2. Solvation Effect (Hydration): The substituted ammonium ion is stabilized in aqueous solution by hydrogen bonding with water molecules. Greater number of hydrogens attached to nitrogen allows more extensive H-bonding: RNH3⁺ (3 H-bonds) > R2NH2⁺ (2 H-bonds) > R3NH⁺ (1 H-bond). On solvation grounds, order is: 1° > 2° > 3°. 3. Steric Hindrance: Bulky alkyl groups crowd around the nitrogen atom, hindering attack of the proton and shielding the ammonium cation from stabilizing solvent water molecules. Greater crowding destabilizes 3° ammonium ions. (b) Contrast between Methyl and Ethyl Amines (2 marks): - In methylamines, the small methyl group produces minimal steric hindrance. Solvation effect dominates over inductive effect in tertiary amine, causing trimethylamine ((CH3)3N) to drop behind methylamine. The balance yields: 2° > 1° > 3° > NH3: (CH3)2NH > CH3NH2 > (CH3)3N > NH3. - In ethylamines, the larger ethyl group exerts a substantially stronger +I effect that outweighs loss of hydration in the tertiary amine compared to 1° amine, pushing triethylamine ahead of ethylamine. The balance yields: 2° > 3° > 1° > NH3: (C2H5)2NH > (C2H5)3N > C2H5NH2 > NH3.
Q44. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2021 (Case Study - 4 Marks)
Read the following passage and answer the questions: Case Study: Diazonium Salts in Synthetic Organic Chemistry Arenediazonium salts ([ArN2⁺]X⁻) are versatile synthetic intermediates prepared by diazotization of primary aromatic amines with nitrous acid (NaNO2 + HCl) at 273-278 K. The stability of diazonium salts arises from resonance delocalization of positive charge across the aromatic ring. Nitrogen gas (N2) is an exceptional leaving group due to its extreme thermodynamic stability and gaseous nature. Diazonium salts undergo two broad categories of reactions: (1) replacement reactions where N2 is displaced by -Cl, -Br, -I, -F, -CN, -OH, or -H, and (2) coupling reactions where the diazo group is retained, forming brightly coloured azo dyes. (i) Why must diazotization be conducted at low temperatures (0-5 °C)? (ii) Name the reaction used to introduce a fluorine atom into a benzene ring via diazonium salt. (iii) What product is formed when benzenediazonium chloride is heated with water? (iv) Give the structure and colour of the dye formed when benzenediazonium chloride couples with phenol.
Answer: Solutions to Case Study on Diazonium Salts Chemistry
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Diazonium salts are thermally unstable. Above 5 °C (278 K), they decompose rapidly by hydrolyzing with water to form phenol with evolution of nitrogen gas: ArN2⁺Cl⁻ + H2O -> ArOH + N2 + HCl. (ii) Balz-Schiemann reaction (treatment with fluoroboric acid HBF4 followed by thermal decomposition of ArN2⁺BF4⁻). (iii) Phenol (C6H5OH), with evolution of N2 gas and HCl. (iv) p-Hydroxyazobenzene (C6H5-N=N-C6H4-OH); Colour: Brilliant Orange dye.
Q45. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2020 (5 Marks)
(a) An aromatic compound 'A' on heating with Br2 and KOH forms a compound 'B' (molecular formula C6H7N) which reacts with CHCl3 and alc. KOH to produce an unbearable foul-smelling compound 'C'. Compound 'B' on reaction with NaNO2 and HCl at 273-278 K followed by reaction with KI gives compound 'D'. Identify A, B, C, and D, and write all chemical reactions. (b) Write the IUPAC name of (CH3)2CH-NH-CH3.
Answer: Identification of A = Benzamide, B = Aniline, C = Phenyl isocyanide, D = Iodobenzene; IUPAC name
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (4 marks): - Compound 'A' undergoes Hoffmann bromamide degradation (Br2/KOH) to give 'B' (C6H7N): hence 'B' is Aniline (C6H5NH2) and 'A' is Benzamide (C6H5CONH2). - Reaction with CHCl3/KOH (Carbylamine test) gives foul-smelling Phenyl isocyanide (C6H5NC) as compound 'C'. - Diazotization of 'B' followed by treatment with KI yields Iodobenzene (C6H5I) as compound 'D'. - Reactions: 1. C6H5CONH2 (A) + Br2 + 4 KOH -(heat)-> C6H5NH2 (B, Aniline) + K2CO3 + 2 KBr + 2 H2O. 2. C6H5NH2 (B) + CHCl3 + 3 KOH -> C6H5NC (C, Phenyl isocyanide) + 3 KCl + 3 H2O. 3. C6H5NH2 + NaNO2 + 2 HCl -(273-278 K)-> C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + NaCl + 2 H2O. 4. C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + KI -(warm)-> C6H5I (D, Iodobenzene) + KCl + N2(g). (b) IUPAC Name (1 mark): - (CH3)2CH-NH-CH3: The longest carbon chain bonded to nitrogen has 3 carbons (propan-2-amine). The methyl group is attached to nitrogen: N-Methylpropan-2-amine.
Q46. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2019 (5 Marks)
(a) Write short notes on: (i) Carbylamine reaction (ii) Diazotization (iii) Coupling reaction. (b) Why are aliphatic diazonium salts unstable even at 0 °C, whereas aromatic diazonium salts are stable at 0-5 °C?
Answer: Three named reactions and stability comparison of diazonium salts
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(a) Named Reactions (3 marks): (i) Carbylamine Reaction: Primary amine heated with chloroform and alcoholic KOH yields an offensive smelling isocyanide: R-NH2 + CHCl3 + 3 KOH -> R-NC + 3 KCl + 3 H2O. (ii) Diazotization: Conversion of primary aromatic amine into diazonium salt using NaNO2 and dilute HCl at 273-278 K: Ar-NH2 + NaNO2 + 2 HCl -> Ar-N2⁺Cl⁻ + NaCl + 2 H2O. (iii) Coupling Reaction: Arenediazonium salts react with electron-rich aromatic compounds (phenols or aromatic amines) to form brightly coloured azo compounds containing -N=N- linkage: ArN2⁺Cl⁻ + Ar'H -> Ar-N=N-Ar' + HCl. (b) Stability of Diazonium Salts (2 marks): - Aromatic diazonium salts ([C6H5-N⁺≡N]Cl⁻) are stabilized by resonance delocalization of the positive charge into the π-electron cloud of the benzene ring across four canonical structures. This resonance stabilization allows them to persist at low temperatures (0-5 °C). - Aliphatic diazonium salts ([R-N⁺≡N]Cl⁻) have no conjugated π-system to delocalize the positive charge. The C-N bond cleaves instantaneously to release gaseous nitrogen (N2) and form a highly reactive carbocation (R⁺), decomposing explosively even below 0 °C.
Q47. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2018 (4 Marks)
Arrange the following in the order directed: (i) (C2H5)2NH, (C2H5)3N, C2H5NH2, NH3 (decreasing basic strength in aqueous solution) (ii) Aniline, p-Nitroaniline, p-Toluidine (increasing basic strength) (iii) C2H5NH2, (C2H5)2NH, C2H5OH, CH3COOH (increasing boiling point) (iv) Ethanamine, N-Ethylethanamine, N,N-Diethylethanamine (increasing solubility in water).
Answer: Four rankings with chemical reasoning
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Decreasing basic strength: (C2H5)2NH > (C2H5)3N > C2H5NH2 > NH3. (Combination of +I effect, hydration of ammonium cation, and steric hindrance makes 2° > 3° > 1° > NH3). (ii) Increasing basic strength: p-Nitroaniline < Aniline < p-Toluidine. (The -NO2 group is electron-withdrawing (-M, -I) and decreases basicity; the -CH3 group is electron-donating (+I, hyperconjugation) and increases basicity). (iii) Increasing boiling point: C2H5NH2 < (C2H5)2NH < C2H5OH < CH3COOH. (Carboxylic acid forms dimers with two H-bonds; alcohol forms stronger H-bonds than amine because O is more electronegative than N; 2° amine has higher molecular mass than 1° amine). (iv) Increasing solubility in water: N,N-Diethylethanamine (3°) < N-Ethylethanamine (2°) < Ethanamine (1°). (Primary amine has smallest hydrophobic alkyl bulk and two N-H bonds to donate and accept hydrogen bonds with water).
Q48. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2017 (5 Marks)
(a) An organic compound 'A' (C3H5N) on reduction with LiAlH4 yields compound 'B'. Compound 'B' on treatment with HNO2 gives compound 'C' with evolution of N2 gas. Compound 'C' on oxidation with acidified K2Cr2O7 gives propanoic acid. Identify A, B, and C, and write all chemical reactions. (b) Why does primary amine have higher boiling point than tertiary amine of the same molecular formula?
Answer: Identification of A = Propanenitrile, B = Propan-1-amine, C = Propan-1-ol; Boiling point justification
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Identification and Reactions (3.5 marks): - Oxidation of compound 'C' gives Propanoic acid (CH3CH2COOH): hence 'C' must be Propan-1-ol (CH3CH2CH2OH). - Compound 'C' is obtained from 'B' with HNO2: hence 'B' is Propan-1-amine (CH3CH2CH2NH2). - Compound 'B' is obtained by reduction of 'A' (C3H5N): hence 'A' is Propanenitrile (CH3CH2CN). - Reactions: 1. CH3CH2CN (A) + 4 [H] -(LiAlH4)-> CH3CH2CH2NH2 (B, Propan-1-amine). 2. CH3CH2CH2NH2 (B) + HNO2 -> CH3CH2CH2OH (C, Propan-1-ol) + N2(g)↑ + H2O. 3. CH3CH2CH2OH (C) + 2 [O] -(K2Cr2O7/H2SO4)-> CH3CH2COOH (Propanoic acid) + H2O. (b) Boiling Point Justification (1.5 marks): - Primary amines (R-NH2) have two polar N-H bonds with hydrogen directly bonded to electronegative nitrogen. They form extensive intermolecular hydrogen-bonded networks. - Tertiary amines (R3N) have all three valencies of nitrogen bonded to alkyl carbons and lack any hydrogen on nitrogen. Consequently, they cannot form intermolecular hydrogen bonds among themselves, possessing only weak dipole-dipole attractions and lower boiling points.
Q49. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2016 (4 Marks)
How will you carry out the following conversions: (i) Benzamide to Aniline (ii) Aniline to Benzoic acid (iii) Ethanamine to Methanamine (iv) Methanamine to Ethanamine?
Answer: Four organic conversions involving amines and diazonium salts
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
(i) Benzamide to Aniline: Hoffmann bromamide degradation: C6H5CONH2 + Br2 + 4 KOH -(heat)-> C6H5NH2 (Aniline) + K2CO3 + 2 KBr + 2 H2O. (ii) Aniline to Benzoic acid: - Step 1: C6H5NH2 + NaNO2 + 2 HCl -(273-278 K)-> C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻. - Step 2: C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + CuCN -(KCN, heat)-> C6H5CN (Benzonitrile). - Step 3: C6H5CN + 2 H2O + H⁺ -(heat)-> C6H5COOH (Benzoic acid) + NH4⁺. (iii) Ethanamine to Methanamine (Step down): - Step 1: CH3CH2NH2 + HNO2 -> CH3CH2OH + N2 + H2O. - Step 2: CH3CH2OH + [O] -(KMnO4/H⁺)-> CH3COOH. - Step 3: CH3COOH + NH3 -(heat)-> CH3CONH2. - Step 4: CH3CONH2 + Br2 + 4 KOH -(heat)-> CH3NH2 (Methanamine). (iv) Methanamine to Ethanamine (Step up): - Step 1: CH3NH2 + HNO2 -> CH3OH + N2 + H2O. - Step 2: CH3OH + PCl5 -> CH3Cl + POCl3 + HCl. - Step 3: CH3Cl + alc. KCN -> CH3CN + KCl. - Step 4: CH3CN + 4 [H] -(LiAlH4 or H2/Ni)-> CH3CH2NH2 (Ethanamine).
Q50. Long Answer / Case Study (4-5M)
CBSE 2015 (5 Marks)
(a) State the reasons for each of the following: (i) Aniline does not undergo Friedel-Crafts reaction. (ii) Diazonium salts of aromatic amines are more stable than those of aliphatic amines. (iii) Gabriel phthalimide synthesis is preferred for synthesizing primary aliphatic amines. (b) Write the chemical equation for the reaction of benzenediazonium chloride with hypophosphorous acid.
Answer: Explanations for Friedel-Crafts failure, diazonium stability, Gabriel synthesis, and deamination equation
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Marking Scheme: (a) Explanations (3.5 marks): (i) Failure of Friedel-Crafts: Aniline is a Lewis base and donates its lone pair to the Lewis acid catalyst AlCl3, forming an insoluble coordination complex: C6H5NH2: -> AlCl3. The resulting positive formal charge on nitrogen strongly deactivates the benzene ring by -I effect, preventing electrophilic substitution. (ii) Stability of Aromatic Diazonium Salts: The positive charge on the diazonium group is delocalized over the π-system of the aromatic ring through four resonance structures. Aliphatic diazonium salts lack this resonance stabilization and decompose spontaneously even at 0 °C to evolve N2. (iii) Gabriel Phthalimide Preference: In Gabriel synthesis, the reaction proceeds by clean SN2 substitution on alkyl halide to yield purely primary amine, completely free from contaminating secondary and tertiary amines or quaternary ammonium salts (which always plague ammonolysis of alkyl halides). (b) Chemical Equation (1.5 marks): C6H5N2⁺Cl⁻ + H3PO2 + H2O -(Cu⁺ catalyst)-> C6H6 (Benzene) + N2(g) + H3PO3 (phosphorous acid) + HCl.
Organic Chemistry
Chapter 10: Biomolecules
7 Marks
Q1. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Which of the following amino acids is optically inactive?
(a) Glycine
(b) Alanine
(c) Valine
(d) Leucine
Answer: (a) Glycine
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Glycine (H2N-CH2-COOH) has two hydrogen atoms attached to the α-carbon. Lacking four different groups, the α-carbon is achiral (symmetric), making glycine the only optically inactive natural α-amino acid.
Q2. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Sucrose is a non-reducing sugar because:
(a) both reducing functional groups (C-1 of α-glucose and C-2 of β-fructose) are involved in the glycosidic bond
(b) it contains fructose
(c) it has no hydroxyl groups
(d) it is insoluble in water
Answer: (a) both reducing functional groups (C-1 of α-glucose and C-2 of β-fructose) are involved in the glycosidic bond
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In sucrose, the hemiacetal/hemiketal carbon C-1 of α-D-glucose is joined to C-2 of β-D-fructose via an α,β-(1->2) glycosidic linkage. Because both potential aldehyde and ketone carbonyl carbons are locked in the bond, there is no free carbonyl group to reduce Tollens' or Fehling's reagent.
Q3. MCQ
CBSE 2023
Which of the following nitrogenous bases is present in RNA but ABSENT in DNA?
(a) Uracil
(b) Thymine
(c) Cytosine
(d) Adenine
Answer: (a) Uracil
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
DNA contains Adenine (A), Guanine (G), Cytosine (C), and Thymine (T). RNA contains Adenine (A), Guanine (G), Cytosine (C), and Uracil (U). Uracil replaces thymine in RNA.
Q4. MCQ
CBSE 2023
During denaturation of a protein, which of the following structures remains intact?
(a) Primary structure
(b) Secondary structure
(c) Tertiary structure
(d) Quaternary structure
Answer: (a) Primary structure
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Denaturation disrupts the weak hydrogen bonds, ionic interactions, and hydrophobic bonds stabilizing the secondary, tertiary, and quaternary structures of the protein. The strong covalent peptide bonds of the primary structure (amino acid sequence) remain completely intact.
Q5. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Deficiency of Vitamin C (ascorbic acid) causes:
(a) Scurvy (bleeding gums)
(b) Beriberi
(c) Rickets
(d) Night blindness
Answer: (a) Scurvy (bleeding gums)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Vitamin C is essential for collagen biosynthesis. Its deficiency leads to Scurvy, characterized by bleeding spongy gums, loose teeth, poor wound healing, and fragile capillaries.
Q6. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Amylose, a water-soluble component of starch, consists of unbranched chains of α-D-glucose units linked by:
(a) C-1 to C-4 glycosidic linkages
(b) C-1 to C-6 glycosidic linkages
(c) C-1 to C-2 glycosidic linkages
(d) β-1,4-glycosidic linkages
Answer: (a) C-1 to C-4 glycosidic linkages
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Amylose constitutes about 15-20% of starch and is a long unbranched water-soluble polymer composed of 200-1000 α-D-(+)-glucose units held together exclusively by α-(1->4) glycosidic linkages.
Q7. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
On prolonged heating with concentrated hydriodic acid (HI) and red phosphorus, D-glucose yields:
(a) n-Hexane
(b) Gluconic acid
(c) Saccharic acid
(d) Hexanoic acid
Answer: (a) n-Hexane
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Prolonged heating of glucose with HI and red P reduces all six carbon atoms to an unbranched six-carbon alkane, n-hexane: C6H12O6 + 14 HI -(heat)-> CH3(CH2)4CH3 (n-hexane) + 6 H2O + 7 I2. This proved that all six carbon atoms in glucose are linked in a continuous straight chain.
Q8. MCQ
CBSE 2020
Which of the following is a water-soluble vitamin that is NOT excreted in urine and is stored in the liver?
(a) Vitamin B12
(b) Vitamin C
(c) Vitamin B1
(d) Vitamin B6
Answer: (a) Vitamin B12
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Unlike most water-soluble vitamins (such as Vitamin C and B-complex vitamins) which cannot be stored and are excreted in urine, Vitamin B12 (cobalamin) is stored in substantial quantities in the human liver.
Q9. MCQ
CBSE 2020 Delhi
The helical structure of proteins (α-helix) is stabilized by:
(a) Intramolecular hydrogen bonds between -NH- and -C=O groups
(b) Peptide bonds only
(c) Disulfide bonds
(d) van der Waals forces only
Answer: (a) Intramolecular hydrogen bonds between -NH- and -C=O groups
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In an α-helix, a polypeptide chain coils into a right-handed screw. The structure is stabilized by intramolecular hydrogen bonds formed between the -NH- group of each amino acid residue and the >C=O group of the fourth preceding residue along the turn.
Q10. MCQ
CBSE 2019
The two crystalline cyclic forms of D-glucose, α-D-glucose and β-D-glucose, are called:
(a) Anomers
(b) Enantiomers
(c) Epimers
(d) Racemers
Answer: (a) Anomers
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
α-D-glucose and β-D-glucose differ in configuration only around the newly created hemiacetal chiral carbon C-1 (the anomeric carbon). Isomers differing in configuration exclusively at the anomeric center are termed anomers.
Q11. MCQ
CBSE 2018
In DNA, the complementary base pairing between guanine (G) and cytosine (C) is held together by:
(a) Three hydrogen bonds
(b) Two hydrogen bonds
(c) One covalent bond
(d) Phosphodiester bonds
Answer: (a) Three hydrogen bonds
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
In the Watson-Crick double helical model of DNA, Guanine pairs with Cytosine through three specific hydrogen bonds (G≡C), while Adenine pairs with Thymine through two hydrogen bonds (A=T).
Q12. MCQ
CBSE 2017
When D-glucose is treated with bromine water (Br2 / H2O), the product formed is:
(a) Gluconic acid
(b) Saccharic acid
(c) Sorbitol
(d) Tartaric acid
Answer: (a) Gluconic acid
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Bromine water is a mild oxidizing agent that selectively oxidizes the terminal aldehyde group (-CHO) of glucose into a carboxylic acid (-COOH) without affecting the primary alcohol group (-CH2OH), producing Gluconic acid (HOCH2(CHOH)4COOH).
Q13. MCQ
CBSE 2016
Which of the following is a fibrous protein?
(a) Keratin
(b) Insulin
(c) Hemoglobin
(d) Albumin
Answer: (a) Keratin
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Keratin (found in hair, nails, skin, wool) consists of polypeptide chains running parallel and held together by disulfide and hydrogen bonds, forming long insoluble fibrous sheets. Insulin, hemoglobin, and albumin are globular proteins.
Q14. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The hydrolysis of sucrose produces an equimolar mixture of D-(+)-glucose and D-(-)-fructose known as 'invert sugar' because:
(a) the optical rotation changes from dextrorotatory (+66.5°) to laevorotatory (-39.9°)
(b) glucose is converted into fructose
(c) fructose has positive rotation
(d) sucrose is a reducing sugar
Answer: (a) the optical rotation changes from dextrorotatory (+66.5°) to laevorotatory (-39.9°)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Sucrose is dextrorotatory ([α]D = +66.5°). On hydrolysis, it yields D-(+)-glucose ([α]D = +52.5°) and D-(-)-fructose ([α]D = -92.4°). Because the laevorotation of fructose is much greater than the dextrorotation of glucose, the resulting hydrolysate is net laevorotatory ([α]D ≈ -39.9°). This inversion of the sign of optical rotation gives it the name invert sugar.
Q15. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
A nucleoside consists of:
(a) A nitrogenous base and a pentose sugar
(b) A nitrogenous base, a pentose sugar, and a phosphate group
(c) A pentose sugar and a phosphate group
(d) A nitrogenous base and a phosphate group
Answer: (a) A nitrogenous base and a pentose sugar
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
A nucleoside is formed by attachment of a nitrogenous heterocyclic base (purine or pyrimidine) to the C-1' position of a pentose sugar (ribose or deoxyribose). When a phosphate group is attached at C-5' of the sugar, it becomes a nucleotide.
Q16. MCQ
CBSE 2021 Term-1
Which of the following carbohydrates cannot be digested by the human gastrointestinal system?
(a) Cellulose
(b) Starch
(c) Glycogen
(d) Maltose
Answer: (a) Cellulose
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Cellulose consists of β-D-glucose units joined by β-(1->4) glycosidic linkages. The human digestive system produces enzymes (like amylase) that cleave only α-glycosidic bonds and lacks the enzyme cellulase needed to hydrolyze β-glycosidic linkages.
Q17. MCQ
CBSE 2020
At the isoelectric point (pI), an amino acid:
(a) exists as a dipolar zwitterion and does not migrate in an electric field
(b) exists as a cation and moves to the cathode
(c) exists as an anion and moves to the anode
(d) is completely uncharged
Answer: (a) exists as a dipolar zwitterion and does not migrate in an electric field
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
The isoelectric point (pI) is the specific pH at which an amino acid exists predominantly as an electrically neutral dipolar zwitterion (+H3N-CHR-COO⁻) with equal positive and negative charges, resulting in zero net electrical migration towards either electrode.
Q18. MCQ
CBSE 2019
Glucose on reaction with concentrated nitric acid (conc. HNO3) yields:
(a) Saccharic acid
(b) Gluconic acid
(c) Hexanoic acid
(d) Tartaric acid
Answer: (a) Saccharic acid
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Concentrated nitric acid is a powerful oxidizing agent that oxidizes both the aldehyde group (-CHO at C-1) and the primary alcohol group (-CH2OH at C-6) of glucose into carboxylic acid groups, yielding the dicarboxylic acid Saccharic acid (HOOC-(CHOH)4-COOH).
Q19. MCQ
CBSE 2018
Which of the following vitamins is fat-soluble and essential for blood clotting?
(a) Vitamin K
(b) Vitamin A
(c) Vitamin D
(d) Vitamin E
Answer: (a) Vitamin K
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Vitamin K (phylloquinone) is a fat-soluble vitamin essential for the hepatic synthesis of prothrombin and other blood-clotting factors. Its deficiency leads to impaired blood coagulation and prolonged bleeding time.
Q20. MCQ
CBSE 2017
In aqueous solution, an amino acid exists as a dipolar ion called a zwitterion formed by:
(a) internal transfer of a proton from -COOH group to -NH2 group
(b) loss of water molecule
(c) coordination with solvent
(d) loss of carbon dioxide
Answer: (a) internal transfer of a proton from -COOH group to -NH2 group
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Amino acids contain both an acidic carboxyl group (-COOH) and a basic amino group (-NH2). In aqueous solution, an intramolecular acid-base neutralization occurs: the -COOH loses a proton to become -COO⁻, and the -NH2 gains the proton to become -NH3⁺, producing the dipolar zwitterion +H3N-CHR-COO⁻.
Q21. MCQ
CBSE 2016
In a DNA molecule, consecutive nucleotides along a single strand are linked together by:
(a) Phosphodiester bonds between 3' and 5' carbon atoms
(b) Hydrogen bonds
(c) Peptide bonds
(d) Glycosidic bonds
Answer: (a) Phosphodiester bonds between 3' and 5' carbon atoms
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Nucleotides in a polynucleotide strand are covalently joined by 3',5'-phosphodiester linkages between the 3'-OH group of one pentose sugar and the 5'-phosphate group of the adjacent sugar.
Q22. MCQ
CBSE 2015
Which of the following tests is NOT given by D-glucose, proving its cyclic hemiacetal structure?
(a) Schiff's test and NaHSO3 addition
(b) Fehling's test
(c) Tollens' test
(d) Acetylation with acetic anhydride
Answer: (a) Schiff's test and NaHSO3 addition
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Despite having an aldehyde group in its open-chain formulation, glucose fails to restore the pink color of Schiff's reagent and does not form a bisulfite addition compound with NaHSO3, proving that the aldehyde group is predominantly masked in a cyclic six-membered hemiacetal pyranose ring.
Q23. MCQ
CBSE 2024
Glycogen, the carbohydrate reserve in animals, is structurally most similar to:
(a) Amylopectin
(b) Amylose
(c) Cellulose
(d) Sucrose
Answer: (a) Amylopectin
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Glycogen (animal starch) is a branched polymer of α-D-glucose with α-(1->4) linear chains and α-(1->6) branch points, resembling amylopectin but with a significantly higher degree of branching (branching occurs every 8-12 glucose units compared to 20-25 in amylopectin).
Q24. MCQ
CBSE 2023
The peptide linkage formed between two amino acid molecules is chemically an:
(a) Amide linkage (-CO-NH-)
(b) Ester linkage
(c) Ether linkage
(d) Anhydride linkage
Answer: (a) Amide linkage (-CO-NH-)
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
A peptide bond is a covalent amide linkage (-CO-NH-) formed between the carboxyl group of one amino acid and the amino group of an adjacent amino acid, with the elimination of a water molecule.
Q25. MCQ
CBSE 2022 Term-1
Deficiency of Vitamin D in growing children leads to:
(a) Rickets
(b) Scurvy
(c) Beriberi
(d) Night blindness
Answer: (a) Rickets
Step-by-Step Marking Scheme & Explanation:
Vitamin D regulates calcium and phosphate homeostasis. Its deficiency in children leads to Rickets, characterized by soft, malformed, and bowed leg bones due to failure of osteoid matrix mineralization.
